This prophecy, which is nearly identical with the description of the little horn of Daniel 7, unquestionably points to the papacy." — GC 439.1.

  > "The forty and two months are the same as the 'time and times and the dividing of time,' three years and a half, or 1260 days, of Daniel 7—the time during which the papal power was to oppress God's people. This period… began with the supremacy of the papacy, A.D. 538, and terminated in 1798." — GC 439.2.

### The 1260 years — 538 to 1798

- **Daniel 7:25 (d)** — "a time and times and the dividing of time" = 3½ prophetic years = 1260 prophetic days.

  > "A time, as we have seen from chapter 4:23, is one year; two times, the least that could be denoted by the plural, two years, and the dividing of time, or half a time… half a year.… We thus have three years and a half for the continuance of this power." — DAR 141.1.

- **Numbers 14:34** — "each day for a year, shall ye bear your iniquities." The day-year principle, stated plainly.

  > "The rule given us in the Bible is, that when a day is used as a symbol, it stands for a year. Ezekiel 4:6; Numbers 14:34." — DAR 141.1.

- **Ezekiel 4:6** — "I have appointed thee each day for a year." The second witness to the day-for-a-year rule of prophetic time.

  > "We must now consider that we are in the midst of symbolic prophecy; hence in this measurement the time is not literal, but symbolic also.… The rule given us in the Bible is, that when a day is used as a symbol, it stands for a year. Ezekiel 4:6; Numbers 14:34." — DAR 141.1.

- **Revelation 12:6** — "the woman fled into the wilderness... that they should feed her there a thousand two hundred and threescore days." The same 1260 of the church in the wilderness under papal supremacy.

  > "Three years and a half contained twelve hundred and sixty days. As each day stands for a year, we have twelve hundred and sixty years for the continuation of the supremacy of this horn." — DAR 141.

- **Revelation 12:14** — "a time, and times, and half a time" — the identical phrase as Daniel 7:25, fixing the three expressions (1260 days = 42 months = a time, times, and half a time) as one period.

  > "The Hebrew, or rather the Chaldaic, word for time in the text before us, is עִדָּן iddân, which Gesenius defines thus: 'Time. Spec, in prophetic language for a year. Daniel 7:25… for a year, also two years, and half a year; i. e., for three years and a half.'" — DAR 141.1.

- **Revelation 11:2-3** — "forty and two months... a thousand two hundred and threescore days." Forty-two months of thirty days = 1260 days — Scripture's own arithmetic equating the periods.

  > "The ordinary Jewish year, which must be used as the basis of reckoning, contained three hundred and sixty days. Three years and a half contained twelve hundred and sixty days. As each day stands for a year, we have twelve hundred and sixty years for the continuation of the supremacy of this horn." — DAR 141.1.

- **Daniel 7:25 fulfilled** — Justinian's edict (533) made the bishop of Rome head of all the churches, but took effect only when the Ostrogoths were driven from Rome in **538**. Counting forward: **538 + 1260 = 1798**, when Berthier with a French army entered Rome, proclaimed a republic, and took the pope prisoner — the deadly wound.

  > "The edict of the emperor Justinian, dated A. D. 533, made the bishop of Rome the head of all the churches. But this edict could not go into effect until the Arian Ostrogoths… were driven from Rome; and this was not accomplished… till A. D. 538.… For 538 + 1260 = 1798; and in the year 1798, Berthier, with a French army, entered Rome, proclaimed a republic, took the pope prisoner, and for a time abolished the papacy." — DAR 141.1.

  > "At that time the pope was made captive by the French army, the papal power received its deadly wound, and the prediction was fulfilled, 'He that leadeth into captivity shall go into captivity.'" — GC 439.2.

### The judgment scene — the Ancient of Days and the books

- **Daniel 7:9-10** — "The thrones were cast down [set up], and the Ancient of days did sit, whose garment was white as snow... the judgment was set, and the books were opened." The Ancient of Days = God the Father, a personal Being, taking the throne of judgment; "thrones were cast down" should read _set up_. The opened books bring the investigative judgment to view.

  > "By an unfortunate translation in verse 9, a wrong idea is almost sure to be conveyed. The words cast down are from a word which in the original signifies just the opposite, namely, to set up.… The thrones are not the thrones of earthly kingdoms, which are to be thrown down at the last day, but thrones of judgment, which are to be 'placed,' or set up, in the court of God on high just before the end." — DAR 119.4.

  > "The 'Ancient of days,' God the Father, takes the throne of judgment. Mark the description of his person. Those who believe in the impersonality of God are obliged to admit that he is here described as a personal being.… The books are opened, and the cases of all come up for examination before that great tribunal." — DAR 120.1.

- **Daniel 7:10 (b)** — "thousand thousands ministered unto him, and ten thousand times ten thousand stood before him." The heavenly court convenes — a scene that, by Daniel 8:14, is "even now transpiring in the sanctuary above."

  > "It will appear from the testimony of chapter 8:14, that this solemn work is even now transpiring in the sanctuary above." — DAR 121.1.

- **Daniel 8:14** — "Unto two thousand and three hundred days; then shall the sanctuary be cleansed." The judgment of Daniel 7 is the cleansing of the heavenly sanctuary, which opened in 1844 at the close of the 2300 days.

  > "It will appear from the testimony of chapter 8:14, that this solemn work is even now transpiring in the sanctuary above." — DAR 121.1.

- **Daniel 7:11-12** — "I beheld even till the beast was slain, and his body destroyed, and given to the burning flame. As concerning the rest of the beasts, they had their dominion taken away; yet their lives were prolonged for a season." The fourth beast persists, blaspheming, until destroyed by fire at the end — unlike the earlier beasts, whose peoples lived on after their dominion passed.

  > "The fourth terrible beast continues without change of character, and the little horn continues to utter its blasphemies… till the beast is given to the burning flame; and this is not its conversion, but its destruction.… The life of the fourth beast is not prolonged after its dominion is gone, as were the lives of the preceding beasts.… Its career ends in the lake of fire, and it has no existence beyond." — DAR 121.2-5.

- **Daniel 7:13-14** — "One like the Son of man came with the clouds of heaven, and came to the Ancient of days, and they brought him near before him. And there was given him dominion, and glory, and a kingdom." This is **not** the second advent to earth, for it is a coming _to_ the Ancient of Days in the heavenly temple. The Son of man receives His kingdom _before_ His return — at the close of His priestly work, the marriage of the Lamb.

  > "The scene here described is not the second advent of Christ to this earth, unless the Ancient of days is on this earth; for it is a coming to the Ancient of days.… The Son of man receives his kingdom before his return to this earth.… He receives the kingdom at the close of his priestly work in the sanctuary." — DAR 122.2-3.

- **Matthew 22:11-13** — "the king came in to see the guests" — the inspection of the wedding company for the wedding garment pictures the investigative judgment that examines every professed believer's record.

  > "As the last thing before the marriage, the King comes in to see the guests, to ascertain if all are properly arrayed in the wedding garment… But this question of fitness for the kingdom can be determined only by the investigative Judgment of the sanctuary." — DAR 599.1.

- **Revelation 14:7** — "Fear God, and give glory to him; for the hour of his judgment is come." The first angel's message announces that the very judgment of Daniel 7:9-10 has begun — the message of 1844.

  > "This period… began with the supremacy of the papacy, A.D. 538, and terminated in 1798." — GC 439.

  > "It will appear from the testimony of chapter 8:14, that this solemn work is even now transpiring in the sanctuary above." — DAR 121.1.

- **Acts 1:11** — "this same Jesus... shall so come in like manner as ye have seen him go." The coming of Daniel 7:13 (to the Father) and the coming of Acts 1:11 (to the earth) are distinct events — the first inaugurates the judgment, the second executes it.

  > "The Son of man receives his kingdom before his return to this earth. (See Luke 19:10-12 and onward.) This is a scene, therefore, which transpires in the heavenly temple… He receives the kingdom at the close of his priestly work in the sanctuary." — DAR 122.3.

### The verdict — the saints possess the kingdom

- **Daniel 7:22** — "Until the Ancient of days came, and judgment was given to the saints of the most High; and the time came that the saints possessed the kingdom." Three events in order: the Ancient of Days comes to judgment, the verdict is rendered in the saints' favor, and they receive the everlasting kingdom.

  > "In verse 22 three consecutive events seem to be brought to view.… (1) The coming of the Ancient of days… (2) The judgment that is given to the saints… (3) The time that the saints possess the kingdom; that is, the time of their entrance upon the possession of the new earth." — DAR 125.4.

- **Daniel 7:26** — "But the judgment shall sit, and they shall take away his dominion, to consume and to destroy it unto the end." The same judgment that vindicates the saints strips the little horn of its dominion and consumes it forever.

  > "The fourth terrible beast continues without change of character, and the little horn continues to utter its blasphemies… till the beast is given to the burning flame; and this is not its conversion, but its destruction.… Its career ends in the lake of fire, and it has no existence beyond." — DAR 121.2-5.

- **Daniel 7:27** — "the kingdom... under the whole heaven, shall be given to the people of the saints of the most High, whose kingdom is an everlasting kingdom, and all dominions shall serve and obey him." The vision closes where Daniel 2 closed — with the eternal kingdom of God filling the earth, after every beast and horn has passed.

  > "(3) The time that the saints possess the kingdom; that is, the time of their entrance upon the possession of the new earth. Then the last vestige of the curse, of sin, and of sinners, root and branch, will have been wiped away, and the territory so long misruled by the wicked powers of earth… will be taken by the righteous, to be held by them forever and ever." — DAR 125.

---

# 5. Daniel 8 — The Ram, the He-Goat, the Little Horn, and the Sanctuary

> _Daniel 8 retraces the same march of empires as chapters 2 and 7, but begins one
> kingdom later — dropping Babylon (already falling) and opening with Medo-Persia. Here
> the angel Gabriel names the powers outright: the ram is Media and Persia, the he-goat
> is Grecia, its great horn the first king. The "little horn" that follows is Rome in both
> its phases, pagan and papal — never Antiochus — and "the daily" together with "the
> transgression of desolation" fills the long 2300 days that reach to the cleansing of the
> sanctuary in 1844. The angel's own interpretation fixes the meaning beyond dispute.
> (DAR 145-182; TTHDS 1-2; GC 328.)_

### The setting and the day-for-a-year key

- **Daniel 8:1-2** — In Belshazzar's third year Daniel sees himself "at Shushan in the palace... by the river of Ulai." Shushan was the metropolis of Elam, then held by Babylon, where the king had a royal palace; soon it passed to the Medes and Persians — the very empire about to be unveiled.

  > "Shushan, as we learn from Prideaux, was the metropolis of the province of Elam. This was then in the hands of the Babylonians, and there the king of Babylon had a royal palace. Daniel, as minister of state, and employed about the king's business, was accordingly in that place. … Abradates, viceroy or prince of Shushan, revolted to Cyrus, and the province was joined to the Medes and Persians." — DAR 146.

- **Numbers 14:34** — "each day for a year" — the wilderness sentence makes a day stand for a year, the rule by which the 2300 days are read as 2300 years.

  > "In advance of any argument on the nature and application of these days, the position may be safely taken that they reach to the cleansing of the heavenly sanctuary, for the earthly was to be cleansed each year; and we make the prophet utter nonsense, if we understand him as saying that at the end of 2300 days … an event should take place which was to occur regularly every year." — DAR 178.

  > "Thus far every specification of the prophecies is strikingly fulfilled, and the beginning of the seventy weeks is fixed beyond question at 457 B.C., and their expiration in A.D. 34. From this data there is no difficulty in finding the termination of the 2300 days." — GC 328.

- **Ezekiel 4:6** — "I have appointed thee each day for a year" — the second witness establishing the year-day principle for all the time-prophecies of Daniel and Revelation.

  > "Respecting the 2300 days … The declaration respecting them is a part of a revelation which is given for the instruction of the people of God, and is consequently to be understood." — DAR 159.

### The ram — Medo-Persia (named by the angel)

- **Daniel 8:3-4** — A ram with two unequal horns, "the higher came up last," pushing westward, northward, and southward, doing "according to his will." The two horns are the two nationalities of the empire; the higher-and-later horn is Persia, which rose after Media and overshadowed it.

  > "The two horns represented the two nationalities of which the empire consisted. The higher came up last. This represented the Persian element, which, from being at first simply an ally of the Medes, came to be the leading division of the empire." — DAR 146.

  > "The distinguishing feature of the Persian empire, the union of the two nationalities which composed it, is represented by the two horns of the ram." — DAR 180.

- **Daniel 8:20** — "The ram which thou sawest having two horns are the kings of Media and Persia." The angel names it plainly; no conjecture is permitted. As the disciples said, "now speakest thou plainly, and speakest no proverb."

  > "As the disciples said to the Lord, so may we here say of the angel who spake to Daniel, 'Lo, now speakest thou plainly, and speakest no proverb.' This is an explanation of the vision in language as plain as need be given." — DAR 180.

### The he-goat — Grecia, and its notable horn (Alexander)

- **Daniel 8:5** — "an he goat came from the west... and touched not the ground: and the goat had a notable horn between his eyes." Grecia lay west of Persia; the goat that scarcely touches the ground pictures the marvelous swiftness of the Macedonian conquest — the same speed shown by the four wings of the leopard in chapter 7.

  > "The goat came from the west. Grecia lay west of Persia. … He 'touched not the ground.' Such was the marvelous celerity of his movements that he did not seem to touch the ground, but to fly from point to point with the swiftness of the wind; the same feature is brought to view by the four wings of the leopard in the vision of chapter 7." — DAR 148.

- **Daniel 7:6** — The third beast, "like a leopard, which had upon the back of it four wings of a fowl... and four heads" — the parallel symbol of Greece: swift (four wings) and four-fold after Alexander (four heads), confirming the goat.

  > "He 'touched not the ground.' Such was the marvelous celerity of his movements that he did not seem to touch the ground, but to fly from point to point with the swiftness of the wind; the same feature is brought to view by the four wings of the leopard in the vision of chapter 7." — DAR 148.

- **Daniel 8:6-7** — The goat runs at the ram "in the fury of his power," breaks his two horns, casts him down, and stamps upon him — the overthrow of Persia by Alexander at the Granicus, Issus, and Arbela (B.C. 331), with none to deliver.

  > "Alexander first vanquished the generals of Darius at the River Granicus in Phrygia; he next attacked and totally routed Darius at the passes of Issus in Cilicia, and afterward on the plains of Arbela in Syria. This last battle occurred B. C. 331, and marked the conclusion of the Persian empire." — DAR 148.

  > "The two horns were broken, and the ram was cast to the ground and stamped upon. Persia was subdued, the country ravaged, its armies cut to pieces and scattered … Thus the ram had no power to stand before the goat, and there was none that could deliver him out of his hand." — DAR 149.

- **Daniel 8:21** — "the rough goat is the king of Grecia: and the great horn that is between his eyes is the first king." That first king was Alexander the Great.

  > "The notable horn between his eyes. This is explained in verse 21 to be the first king of the Macedonian empire. This king was Alexander the Great." — DAR 148.

  > "Grecia attained its greatest glory as a unit under the leadership of Alexander the Great, a general as famous as the world has ever seen. … the one notable horn symbolized Alexander the Great." — DAR 180.

- **Daniel 2:39** — "another third kingdom of brass, which shall bear rule over all the earth" — the same Grecia of the image, here the goat that swept "on the face of the whole earth."

  > "The only natural inference would be that the little horn, the power which in this vision succeeds Grecia as an 'exceeding great' power, is also Rome. … in both those prophetic delineations we have found that the power which succeeded Grecia as the fourth great power, was Rome." — DAR 153.

### The four horns — the division of Greece

- **Daniel 8:8** — "the great horn was broken; and for it came up four notable ones toward the four winds of heaven." Alexander fell in the prime of his strength; his empire shattered and consolidated into four divisions.

  > "But Daniel saw it broken in the very prime of its strength and the hight of its power, when every beholder would have exclaimed, Surely, the kingdom is established, and nothing can overthrow it." — DAR 149.

  > "the family of Alexander being then extinct, the chief commanders of the army … assumed the title of kings. They thereupon fell to leaguing and warring with one another to such a degree that within the short space of fifteen years from Alexander's death, the number was reduced to … four — just the number specified in the prophecy." — DAR 150.

- **Daniel 8:22** — "four kingdoms shall stand up out of the nation, but not in his power." The fourfold division — none equal to the original kingdom; the angel's own commentary on the four horns.

  > "Upon his death, the kingdom fell into fragments, but almost immediately consolidated into four grand divisions … These divisions did not stand in his power. None of them possessed the strength of the original kingdom." — DAR 180-181.

### The little horn — Rome, not Antiochus

- **Daniel 8:9** — "out of one of them came forth a little horn, which waxed exceeding great, toward the south, and toward the east, and toward the pleasant land." This power "exceeding great" must surpass both Persia ("great") and Grecia ("very great"); Antiochus Epiphanes — one of twenty-six Syrian kings, who paid tribute to Rome and was evacuated from Egypt at Rome's command — cannot fit. The power that exceeds them all is Rome.

  > "Antiochus, then, was simply one of the twenty-six kings who constituted the Syrian horn of the goat. He was, for the time being, that horn. Hence he could not be at the same time a separate and independent power, or another and remarkable horn, as the little horn was." — DAR 151.

  > "The little horn, in comparison with the powers that preceded it, was exceeding great. Persia is simply called great … Grecia, being more extensive still, is called very great. Now the little horn, which waxed exceeding great, must surpass them both. How absurd, then, to apply this to Antiochus, who was obliged to abandon Egypt at the dictation of the Romans, to whom he paid enormous sums of money as tribute." — DAR 152.

- **Daniel 2:40** — "the fourth kingdom shall be strong as iron... shall it break in pieces and bruise." The same field as chapters 2 and 7: the fourth world-power after Grecia is Rome — therefore the horn after Grecia is Rome.

  > "The field of vision here is substantially the same as that covered by Nebuchadnezzar's image of chapter 2, and Daniel's vision of chapter 7. And in both those prophetic delineations we have found that the power which succeeded Grecia as the fourth great power, was Rome." — DAR 153.

- **Daniel 8:9 (south/east/pleasant land)** — Rome waxed great toward the south (Egypt a province B.C. 30), the east (Syria B.C. 65), and the pleasant land (Judea B.C. 63), eventually destroying Jerusalem and scattering the Jews — point by point fulfilled in Rome.

  > "The little horn waxed great toward the south. … Egypt was made a province of the Roman empire B. C. 30 … The little horn waxed great toward the east. … Rome conquered Syria B. C. 65 … The little horn waxed great toward the pleasant land. … The Romans made it a province of their empire, B. C. 63, and eventually destroyed the city and the temple, and scattered the Jews over the face of the whole earth." — DAR 154.

- **Daniel 8:10** — "it cast down some of the host and of the stars to the ground, and stamped upon them." Rome cast down illustrious persons and Jewish rulers — the same work as the dragon of Revelation 12:4, which is pagan Rome.

  > "The great red dragon (Revelation 12:4) is said to have cast down a third part of the stars of heaven to the ground. The dragon is there interpreted to symbolize pagan Rome, and the stars it cast to the ground were Jewish rulers. Evidently it is the same power and the same work that is here brought to view, which again makes it necessary to apply this growing horn to Rome." — DAR 154.

- **Daniel 8:11** — "he magnified himself even to the prince of the host, and by him the daily was taken away, and the place of his sanctuary was cast down." Rome alone magnified itself against the Prince — crucifying our Lord under its jurisdiction — and pagan Rome was "remodeled into papal Rome," its seat removed from the city of Rome by Constantine (A.D. 330) to Constantinople.

  > "'By him' (the papal form) 'the daily' (the pagan form) 'was taken away.' Pagan Rome was remodeled into papal Rome. And the place of his sanctuary, or worship, the city of Rome, was cast down. The seat of government was removed by Constantine in A. D. 330 to Constantinople." — DAR 154-155.

- **Daniel 8:24-25** — "his power shall be mighty, but not by his own power... he shall destroy the mighty and the holy people... he shall also stand up against the Prince of princes; but he shall be broken without hand." Rome conquered "not by his own power" (through allies and division), destroyed the "holy people" (1,100,000 slain at Jerusalem), stood up against the Prince of princes at the cross, and shall be "broken without hand" — the very phrase that links its end to the stone of chapter 2.

  > "'Mighty, but not by his own power.' The success of the Romans was owing largely to the aid of their allies, and divisions among their enemies … In taking Jerusalem, they slew eleven hundred thousand Jews, and made ninety-seven thousand captives. … And Rome, finally, in the person of one of its governors, stood up against the Prince of princes, by giving sentence of death against Jesus Christ. 'But he shall be broken without hand,' an expression which identifies the destruction of this power with the smiting of the image of chapter 2." — DAR 181-182.

- **Daniel 8:25 (Prince of princes)** — "he shall also stand up against the Prince of princes" — the Prince is Jesus Christ (cf. Dan. 9:25; Acts 3:15; Rev. 1:5); since Antiochus died 164 years before Christ was born, the prophecy cannot mean him — only Rome. Romanists apply it to Antiochus to escape the prophecy; the text forbids it.

  > "The little horn was to stand up against the Prince of princes. The Prince of princes here means, beyond controversy, Jesus Christ. … But Antiochus died one hundred and sixty-four years before our Lord was born. The prophecy cannot, therefore, apply to him … Romanists take that view to avoid the application of the prophecy to themselves." — DAR 153.

### "The daily" and "the transgression of desolation" — paganism and the papacy

- **Daniel 8:12** — "an host was given him against the daily by reason of transgression; and it cast down the truth to the ground; and it practised, and prospered." The barbarian converts became instruments of Rome's religious form; "by reason of transgression" — the working of the mystery of iniquity; the papacy cast the truth to the ground, practiced its deceptions, and prospered, making war on the saints.

  > "The barbarians that subverted the Roman empire … became converts to the Catholic faith, and the instruments of the dethronement of their former religion. … And this was brought about by reason of 'transgression;' that is, by the working of the mystery of iniquity. … And it has 'prospered.' It has made war with the saints, and prevailed against them." — DAR 155.

- **Daniel 8:13** — "How long shall be the vision concerning the daily, and the transgression of desolation, to give both the sanctuary and the host to be trodden under foot?" The word rendered "daily" (Hebrew _tamid_, "continual") occurs 102 times in the Old Testament and means _continual_ — never "sacrifice," which is a supplied word. It denotes a desolating power that endures a long series of years: the "continual" desolation is paganism; the "transgression of desolation" is the papacy. The two phases of opposition fill the whole vision.

  > "The word here rendered daily occurs in the Old Testament, according to the Hebrew Concordance, one hundred and two times, and is, in the great majority of instances, rendered continual or continually. The idea of sacrifice does not attach to the word at all. … By the 'continuance of desolation,' or the perpetual desolation, we must understand that paganism, through all its long history, is meant; and by 'the transgression of desolation' is meant the papacy." — DAR 157.

  > "From a religious point of view, the world has presented only these two phases of opposition against the Lord's work in the earth. … Medo-Persia was pagan; Grecia was pagan; Rome in its first phase was pagan; these all were embraced in the 'daily.' Then comes the papal form, — the 'transgression of desolation.'" — DAR 158.

- **Daniel 8:13 (the cry "How long?")** — the very question of the martyrs through every age (cf. Rev. 6:10). Heaven records both question and answer "which is prima-facie evidence that this is a matter which it was designed that the church should understand."

  > "But another saint asked this one that spake an important question: How long the vision? and both the question and the answer are placed upon record, which is prima-facie evidence that this is a matter which it was designed that the church should understand." — DAR 156.

  > "No wonder the cry has gone up from suffering martyrs, from age to age, How long, O Lord, how long?" — DAR 158.

### The 2300 days — to the cleansing of the sanctuary (1844)

- **Daniel 8:14** — "Unto two thousand and three hundred days; then shall the sanctuary be cleansed." This is the great time-prophecy of the chapter — the "main pillar on which rested the proclamation of time" in the Advent movement. By the year-day rule, 2300 years.

  > "It is well known that the main pillar on which rested the proclamation of time, was the 2300 days, of Daniel 8. In the vision of that chapter, four things were presented to the Prophet: the ram, the he goat, the little horn, and the period of 2300 days." — TTHDS 1-2.

  > "The appointed time was given, Daniel 8:14: 'Unto 2300 days, then shall the sanctuary be cleansed.' Again, he was expressly told when the resurrection of the just would take place, by his own time being measured." — MWV3 95.

- **Daniel 8:14 (which sanctuary?)** — Not the earth (never called the sanctuary), nor Canaan, nor the church, but the heavenly "true tabernacle, which the Lord pitched, and not man" (Heb. 8:1-2; 9:23-24). The earthly sanctuary was cleansed every year; a period of 2300 years must reach the cleansing of the _heavenly_ sanctuary.

  > "Several objects have been claimed by different ones as the sanctuary here mentioned: (1) The earth; (2) The land of Canaan; (3) The church; (4) The sanctuary, the 'true tabernacle, which the Lord pitched, and not man,' which is 'in the heavens,' and of which the Jewish tabernacle was a type, pattern, or figure. Hebrews 8:1, 2; 9:23, 24." — DAR 160.

  > "the position may be safely taken that they reach to the cleansing of the heavenly sanctuary, for the earthly was to be cleansed each year … The heavenly sanctuary is the one in which the decision of all cases is to be rendered." — DAR 178.

- **Daniel 8:13-14 (closing of the vision)** — "the vision of the evening and the morning... is true" (v. 26) — the 2300 evening-mornings; the sanctuary's cleansing answers the martyrs' "How long?" and centers the whole plan of salvation. The sanctuary "occupies the central position... in the great wheel of truth," and is "a key to the interpretation of the most important prophecies."

  > "'The vision of the evening and the morning,' is that of the 2300 days. In view of the long period of oppression, and the calamities which were to come upon his people, Daniel fainted, and was sick certain days. He was astonished at the vision, but did not understand it." — DAR 182.

  > "the sanctuary is a great central object in the plan of salvation. … In the great wheel of truth, the sanctuary occupies this central position. In it, the great truths of revelation find their focal point. … It is a key to the interpretation of the most important prophecies which are having their fulfillment at the present time." — STTHD 10-12.

- **Daniel 8:14 (the terminus)** — Beginning the 2300 years with the decree to restore Jerusalem in B.C. 457 (the 7th of Artaxerxes) and adding 2300 brings the period to A.D. 1844: "the commandment did not go forth until the middle of B.C. 457... which brings us to the 10th of the 7th month, 1844." The seventy weeks (490 years) cut off from the 2300, dated from 457 B.C. and ending A.D. 34, leave 1810 years that reach from 34 to 1844.

  > "If it be asked why the twenty-three hundred days ended on the 10th of the 7th month, 1844, we answer because the commandment did not go forth until the middle of B. C. 457, therefore the first six months of that year must be added to B. C. 1844, which brings us to the 10th of the 7th month, 1844, which two numbers add together, and the result is 2300 years, full and complete." — BP3 132.

  > "The seventy weeks—490 days—having been cut off from the 2300, there were 1810 days remaining. … From A.D. 34, 1810 years extend to 1844. Consequently the 2300 days of Daniel 8:14 terminate in 1844." — GC 328.

### The angel's interpretation — Gabriel commanded to make Daniel understand

- **Daniel 8:15-16** — "Gabriel, make this man to understand the vision." The interpreter is named; the higher voice that commands him is "probably no other than the archangel Michael, or Christ." Gabriel signifies "the mighty one"; he later announced the births of John and the Messiah (Luke 1:11, 26).

  > "The commandment given was, to make this man, Daniel, understand the vision. It was addressed to Gabriel, a name that signifies 'the mighty one.' … the one who here addressed him was evidently higher in rank, and had power to command and control his actions. This was probably no other than the archangel Michael, or Christ." — DAR 178-179.

- **Daniel 8:17-19** — "Understand, O son of man: for at the time of the end shall be the vision... I will make thee know what shall be in the last end of the indignation." The "indignation" is God's controversy with His covenant people, the long period the sanctuary and host are trodden under foot (cf. Eze. 21:25-27).

  > "The indignation must be understood to cover a period of time. … 'Remove the diadem, and take off the crown.... I will overturn, overturn, overturn it: and it shall be no more, until he come whose right it is; and I will give it him.' Ezekiel 21:25-27, 31." — DAR 179-180.

  > "Here is the period of God's indignation against his covenant people; the period during which the sanctuary and host are to be trodden under foot." — DAR 180.

- **Daniel 8:26-27** — "shut thou up the vision; for it shall be for many days... I was astonished at the vision, but none understood it." Gabriel explained the symbols plainly but left one point — _the time_ — unexplained; Daniel was overwhelmed, and the further instruction is deferred.

  > "Respecting the 2300 days, introduced for the first time in verse 14, there are no data in this chapter from which to determine their commencement and close … It is necessary, therefore, for the present, to pass them by." — DAR 159.

  > "Why did not Gabriel at this time fully carry out his instructions, and cause Daniel to understand the vision? — Because Daniel had received all that he could then bear. Further instruction is therefore deferred to a future time." — DAR 182.

- **Daniel 9:21 (the continuation)** — "the man Gabriel, whom I had seen in the vision at the beginning" — the same Gabriel returns in chapter 9 to finish his commission, supplying the one omitted point, _time_, and so unlocking the 2300 days. Thus "the mystery which hangs over these days in this chapter, is dispelled in the next."

  > "They are spoken of in the midst of a prophecy which the angel Gabriel was commanded to make Daniel understand … It will accordingly be found that the mystery which hangs over these days in this chapter, is dispelled in the next." — DAR 159-160.

  > "There was only one point which the Angel had omitted to mention; and that was, Time; hence that was what troubled Daniel … But Gabriel must explain this also; for he had received his commission, 'Make this man to understand the vision;' and he must fulfill it." — TTHDS 1-2.

---

# 6. Daniel 9 — The Seventy Weeks Unlock the 2300 Days (The Keystone)

> _This is the keystone of the whole historicist arch. In Daniel 8 the angel Gabriel
> explained ram, he-goat, and little horn, but left Daniel fainting and the 2300 days
> unexplained ("none understood it"). In chapter 9 Gabriel returns to "give skill and
> understanding" and finishes the unfinished vision. Seventy weeks — 490 years — are
> "cut off" (determined) from the 2300, dating from the decree to restore Jerusalem
> (Artaxerxes, 457 B.C.). Those 490 years measure off the Messiah to the day: 69 weeks
> (483 yrs) to His anointing at baptism (A.D. 27); cut off in the midst of the last week
> (A.D. 31 crucifixion); the 70 weeks closing A.D. 34 when the gospel turns to the
> Gentiles. Subtract 490 from 2300 and 1810 years remain past A.D. 34 — reaching the
> autumn of 1844. Locate the seventy weeks and the 2300 days are surely located.
> (DAR 192, 206-207; GC 326-328, 410.)_

### Gabriel returns to finish the unexplained vision of Daniel 8

- **Daniel 8:14** — "Unto two thousand and three hundred days; then shall the sanctuary be cleansed." The longest time-prophecy in Scripture — but in chapter 8 it is left without a starting date.

  > "The angel had been sent to Daniel for the express purpose of explaining to him the point which he had failed to understand in the vision of the eighth chapter, the statement relative to time — 'unto two thousand and three hundred days; then shall the sanctuary be cleansed.'" — GC 326.

  > "This must therefore have been the point which he did not understand; and as without this the other portions of the vision were of no avail, he could well say, while the application of this period was left in obscurity, that he did not understand the vision." — DAR 191.

- **Daniel 8:26-27** — The vision of the evening and morning "is true," yet shut up "for many days"; Daniel "fainted, and was sick," and "none understood it." The 2300-day number was given but its application was withheld — the loose end chapter 9 ties.

  > "Why did not Gabriel at this time fully carry out his instructions, and cause Daniel to understand the vision? — Because Daniel had received all that he could then bear. Further instruction is therefore deferred to a future time." — DAR 182.

  > "He could endure no more, and the angel left him for a time. Daniel 'fainted, and was sick certain days.' 'And I was astonished at the vision,' he says, 'but none understood it.'" — GC 325-326.

- **Daniel 9:1-2** — Daniel, "in the first year of Darius," understood "by books" Jeremiah's seventy years of captivity were ending — and so prayed for his people, mistaking those literal seventy years for the indignation.

  > "Daniel thought seventy years' captivity in Babylon was the indignation. Gabriel told him, not so. But 'seventy sevens are determined upon thy people and upon thy holy city, to finish the transgression and to make an end of sins.'" — PREX1 130.

- **Daniel 9:21** — "Even the man Gabriel, whom I had seen in the vision at the beginning" — the very same angel of chapter 8 — was "caused to fly swiftly" to Daniel "about the time of the evening oblation." The same messenger comes back to finish the same vision.

  > "If this view of the subject is correct, we should naturally expect, when the angel completed his explanation of the vision, that he would commence with the very point which had been omitted; namely, the time. And this we find to be true in fact." — DAR 191.

  > "That commission must be fulfilled. In obedience to it, the angel, some time afterward, returned to Daniel, saying: 'I am now come forth to give thee skill and understanding;' 'therefore understand the matter, and consider the vision.'" — GC 325.

- **Daniel 9:22-23** — Gabriel: "I am now come forth to give thee skill and understanding... therefore understand the matter, and consider the vision." He sends Daniel back to "the vision" — the only vision left unexplained, that of the 2300 days.

  > "There was one important point in the vision of chapter 8 which had been left unexplained, namely, that relating to time — the period of the 2300 days; therefore the angel, in resuming his explanation, dwells chiefly upon the subject of time." — GC 325.

### "Seventy weeks are cut off" — the seventy weeks are part of the 2300 days

- **Daniel 9:24** — "Seventy weeks are determined upon thy people and upon thy holy city" — 70 weeks of years = 490 years, to finish transgression, make an end of sins, make reconciliation, bring in everlasting righteousness, seal up vision and prophecy, and anoint the most Holy.

  > "After citing Daniel's attention back to the former vision in the most direct and emphatic manner, and assuring him that he had now come forth to give him understanding in the matter, he commences upon the very point there omitted, and says, 'Seventy weeks are determined upon thy people and upon thy holy city.'" — DAR 191.

  > "After bidding Daniel 'understand the matter, and consider the vision,' the very first words of the angel are: 'Seventy weeks are determined upon thy people and upon thy Holy City.' The word here translated 'determined' literally signifies 'cut off.'" — GC 326.

- **The Hebrew "determined" = "cut off."** The word _nehhtak_ (Dan. 9:24) "properly" means "to cut off" — Gesenius, Stockius, the Septuagint, Theodotion, and the Vulgate all carry the sense of cutting. The 490 years are cut off — but from what?

  > "This word Gesenius, in his Hebrew Lexicon, defines as follows: 'Properly, to cut off; tropically, to divide; and so to determine, to decree.'… Thus Chaldaic and Rabbinical authority, and that of the earliest versions, the Septuagint and Vulgate, give the single signification of cutting off, to this verb." — DAR 192.

- **There is only one period to cut from.** "There is no period from which the seventy weeks could be cut off but the 2300 days of the previous vision." The 70 weeks are the first 490 years of the 2300; therefore the two periods begin together.

  > "The language cannot be intelligibly referred to anything else;… there is no period from which the seventy weeks could be cut off but the 2300 days of the previous vision.… Cut off from what? — The 2300 days, most assuredly." — DAR 192.

  > "As the 2300 days was the only period of time mentioned in chapter 8, it must be the period from which the seventy weeks were cut off; the seventy weeks must therefore be a part of the 2300 days, and the two periods must begin together." — GC 326.

- **Numbers 14:34** — "each day for a year" — the wilderness sentence, God's own rule that a prophetic day stands for a literal year.

  > "The explanation of these prophetic periods is based on what is called the 'year-day principle;' that is, making each day stand for a year, according to the Scriptural rule for the application of symbolic time. Ezekiel 4:6; Numbers 14:34." — DAR 197.

- **Ezekiel 4:6** — "I have appointed thee each day for a year." The second witness to the year-day principle that turns 70 weeks (490 days) into 490 years and 2300 days into 2300 years.

  > "What is more conclusive than all else is the fact that the prophecies have actually been fulfilled on this principle, — a demonstration of its correctness from which there is no appeal." — DAR 197.

### The starting date: the decree to restore and build Jerusalem, 457 B.C.

- **Daniel 9:25** — Reckon "from the going forth of the commandment to restore and to build Jerusalem unto the Messiah the Prince" — seven weeks and threescore and two weeks (69 weeks); "the street shall be built again, and the wall, even in troublous times." The decree, not the captivity's end, is the anchor.

  > "The angel now gives to Daniel the event which is to mark the commencement of the seventy weeks. They were to date from the going forth of the commandment to restore and build Jerusalem.… If, now, we can find a date which will harmonize with all these events, we have, beyond a doubt, the true application." — DAR 196.

  > "The seventy weeks were declared by the angel to date from the going forth of the commandment to restore and build Jerusalem. If the date of this commandment could be found, then the starting point for the great period of the 2300 days would be ascertained." — GC 326.

- **Four candidate decrees — only one fits.** Cyrus (B.C. 536, Ezra 1) and Darius (B.C. 519, Ezra 6) concerned only the temple; Nehemiah's grant (B.C. 444, Neh. 2) was merely verbal and too late. Only Artaxerxes' decree to Ezra (B.C. 457, Ezra 7) restored the full civil and ecclesiastical state.

  > "There are but four events which can be taken as answering to the commandment to restore and build Jerusalem.… these decrees had reference principally to the restoration of the temple and the temple-worship of the Jews, and not to the restoration of their civil state and polity, all of which must be included in the expression, 'To restore and to build Jerusalem.'" — DAR 197-200.

- **Ezra 7:11-26** — The full decree of Artaxerxes to Ezra: silver and gold, temple service, and power to "set magistrates and judges... whether it be unto death, or to banishment" — a restoration of the whole Jewish polity, the only grant ample enough to "restore and build Jerusalem."

  > "It empowered him to ordain laws, set magistrates and judges, and execute punishment even unto death; in other words, to restore the Jewish state, civil and ecclesiastical, according to the law of God and the ancient customs of that people. Inspiration has seen fit to preserve this decree; and a full and accurate copy of it is given in the seventh chapter of the book of Ezra." — DAR 198.

  > "In the seventh chapter of Ezra the decree is found. Verses 12-26. In its completest form it was issued by Artaxerxes, king of Persia, 457 B.C." — GC 326.

- **Ezra 6:14** — The Jews built "according to the commandment of Cyrus, and Darius, and Artaxerxes" — three kings, but "the commandment" (singular). The three decrees are reckoned as one whole, perfected and "gone forth" only in the grant to Ezra.

  > "The decrees of these three kings are spoken of as one, — 'the commandment'… showing that they are all reckoned as a unit, the different decrees being but the successive steps by which the work was accomplished. And this decree could not be said to have 'gone forth,' as intended by the prophecy, till the last permission which the prophecy required was embodied in the decree." — DAR 206.

  > "These three kings, in originating, reaffirming, and completing the decree, brought it to the perfection required by the prophecy to mark the beginning of the 2300 years." — GC 326-327.

- **Nehemiah 2:1-8** — Nehemiah's request "in the twentieth year of Artaxerxes" (B.C. 444) was only spoken permission with travel-letters; he found Jerusalem's work already underway (Neh. 2:16) under Ezra's decree, finishing in 52 days — far short of the prophecy's seven weeks (49 years) for the building. Hence not the starting point.

  > "Permission was granted him, but we have no evidence that it was anything more than verbal.… When he arrived at Jerusalem, he found rulers and priests, nobles and people, already engaged in the work of building Jerusalem.… These were, of course, acting under the decree given to Ezra thirteen years before. And finally, Nehemiah, having arrived at Jerusalem, finished the work he came to accomplish, in fifty-two days." — DAR 199-200.

- **The 457 B.C. date is fixed by astronomy.** The canon of Ptolemy, "demonstrated by the concurrent agreement of more than twenty eclipses," places the seventh of Artaxerxes at B.C. 457 — a date that cannot be moved without unsettling all chronology.

  > "This canon places the seventh year of Artaxerxes in the year B.C. 457; and the accuracy of this canon is demonstrated by the concurrent agreement of more than twenty eclipses. This date we cannot change from B.C. 457, without first demonstrating the inaccuracy of Ptolemy's canon." — DAR 205.

### The 69 weeks (483 years): to Messiah the Prince — A.D. 27 anointing

- **Daniel 9:25** — "unto the Messiah the Prince shall be seven weeks, and threescore and two weeks" — 69 weeks = 483 years. From the autumn of 457 B.C., 483 years reach to the autumn of A.D. 27.

  > "Sixty-nine weeks, or 483 years, were to extend to Messiah the Prince. Dating from B.C. 457, they end in A.D. 27." — DAR 201.

  > "The decree of Artaxerxes went into effect in the autumn of 457 B.C. From this date, 483 years extend to the autumn of A.D. 27.… At that time this prophecy was fulfilled." — GC 327.

- **"Messiah" means "the Anointed One."** At A.D. 27 Christ was anointed — not crowned with oil but with the Spirit, at His baptism, exactly where the 483 years end.

  > "The word 'Messiah' signifies 'the Anointed One.' In the autumn of A.D. 27 Christ was baptized by John and received the anointing of the Spirit." — GC 327.

- **Matthew 3:16-17** — At His baptism "the Spirit of God" descended "like a dove" and the Father's voice declared, "This is my beloved Son." The visible anointing of the Messiah the Prince.

  > "What event then occurred? Luke thus informs us: 'Now when all the people were baptized,… the Holy Ghost descended in a bodily shape like a dove upon him, and a voice came from heaven, which said, Thou art my beloved Son; in thee I am well pleased.' Luke 3:21, 22; margin, A.D. 27." — DAR 201.

- **Acts 10:38** — "God anointed Jesus of Nazareth with the Holy Ghost and with power" — Peter naming the baptism as the anointing that made Him Messiah (the Anointed).

  > "The apostle Peter testifies that 'God anointed Jesus of Nazareth with the Holy Ghost and with power.' Acts 10:38." — GC 327.

- **Luke 4:18-19** — "The Spirit of the Lord is upon me, because he hath anointed me to preach the gospel... to preach the acceptable year of the Lord." Christ Himself claims the anointing the 69 weeks foretold.

  > "And the Saviour Himself declared: 'The Spirit of the Lord is upon Me, because He hath anointed Me to preach the gospel to the poor.' Luke 4:18." — GC 327.

- **Mark 1:14-15** — Straightway after His baptism Jesus came "preaching the gospel of the kingdom of God... The time is fulfilled." The only prophetic time then closing was the 69 weeks to Messiah the Prince — and the Messiah announces its fulfilment with His own lips.

  > "After this, Jesus came 'preaching the gospel of the kingdom of God, and saying, The time is fulfilled.' Mark 1:14, 15.… no prophetic period can be found then terminating, except the sixty-nine weeks of the prophecy of Daniel.… The Messiah had now come; and with his own lips he announced the termination of that period which was to be marked by his manifestation." — DAR 201-202.

  > "After His baptism He went into Galilee, 'preaching the gospel of the kingdom of God, and saying, The time is fulfilled.' Mark 1:14, 15." — GC 327.

- **Luke 3:1** — "In the fifteenth year of the reign of Tiberius Caesar" John's ministry — and soon Christ's baptism — is dated; the 15th of Tiberius corresponds to A.D. 27, the very year the 483 years expire. (The seeming clash with "about thirty years" is resolved by the four-year error in the Dionysian era — Christ was born about B.C. 4.)

  > "The answer to this question is found in the fact that Christ was born between three and four years before the beginning of the Christian era.… His birth is carried back to the latter part of U.C. 749, a little over three years before A.D. 1. Christ was therefore thirty years of age in A.D. 27." — DAR 201.

- **Galatians 4:4** — "When the fulness of the time was come, God sent forth his Son" — the "fulness of the time" is no vague phrase but the precise terminus of the prophetic 69 weeks.

  > "The decree of Artaxerxes went into effect in the autumn of 457 B.C. From this date, 483 years extend to the autumn of A.D. 27.… At that time this prophecy was fulfilled." — GC 327.

### The midst of the week: Messiah cut off — A.D. 31 crucifixion

- **Daniel 9:26** — "And after threescore and two weeks shall Messiah be cut off, but not for himself" — the Anointed One slain, His death substitutionary ("not for himself"), and the city and sanctuary doomed to "the prince that shall come" (Rome).

  > "After threescore and two weeks, according to the record, the Messiah was to be cut off. It is the same as if it had read: And after threescore and two weeks, in the midst of the seventieth week, shall Messiah be cut off, and cause the sacrifice and oblation to cease.… the crucifixion is definitely located in the middle of the seventieth week." — DAR 202-203.

  > "The prophet in the last part of verse 27 speaks of the soon-following destruction of Jerusalem by the Roman power; and finally of the destruction of that power itself, called in the margin 'the desolator.'" — DAR 211.

- **Daniel 9:27** — "He shall confirm the covenant with many for one week" (the last seven years, A.D. 27-34); "and in the midst of the week he shall cause the sacrifice and the oblation to cease." The middle of the 70th week — three and a half years after A.D. 27 — falls in the spring of A.D. 31.

  > "The Messiah was to confirm the covenant with many for one week. This would be the last week of the seventy, or the last seven years of the 490. In the midst of the week, the prophecy informs us, he should cause the sacrifice and oblation to cease. These Jewish ordinances, pointing to the death of Christ, could cease only at the cross." — DAR 202-203.

  > "'In the midst of the week He shall cause the sacrifice and the oblation to cease.' In A.D. 31, three and a half years after His baptism, our Lord was crucified." — GC 327-328.

- **Matthew 27:50-51** — When Jesus "yielded up the ghost... the veil of the temple was rent in twain from the top to the bottom." Heaven itself caused the sacrifice and oblation to cease at the cross; type met antitype, the shadow-service ended.

  > "These Jewish ordinances, pointing to the death of Christ, could cease only at the cross; and there they did virtually come to an end, though the outward observance was kept up till the destruction of Jerusalem, A.D. 70." — DAR 202.

  > "With the great sacrifice offered upon Calvary, ended that system of offerings which for four thousand years had pointed forward to the Lamb of God. Type had met antitype, and all the sacrifices and oblations of the ceremonial system were there to cease." — GC 328.

- **A.D. 31 fixed by His four Passovers.** Christ kept four Passovers (John 2:13; 5:1; 6:4; 13:1), giving a ministry of three and a half years from autumn A.D. 27 to spring A.D. 31 — "exactly... the midst of the seventieth week." Thirteen ancient authorities locate the crucifixion in the spring of A.D. 31.

  > "As he began his ministry in the autumn of A.D. 27, his first Passover would occur the following spring, A.D. 28; his second, A.D. 29; his third, A.D. 30; and his fourth and last, A.D. 31. This gives us three years and a half for his public ministry, and corresponds exactly to the prophecy that he should be cut off in the midst, or middle, of the seventieth week.… Here, then, are thirteen credible authorities locating the crucifixion of Christ in the spring of A.D. 31." — DAR 203-204.

### The 70 weeks close: A.D. 34 — the gospel turns to the Gentiles

- **Daniel 9:24** — The 70 weeks were "determined upon thy people" — allotted to the Jews as the special people of God; when the 490 years run out, that special standing ends. Going forward from the cross (A.D. 31) three and a half years reaches the autumn of A.D. 34.

  > "Going from the crucifixion forward three and a half years, we are brought to the autumn of A.D. 34, as the grand terminating point of the whole period of the seventy weeks.… these are just the events which one would expect to take place when that specified period which was cut off for the Jews, and allotted to them as a peculiar people, should fully expire." — DAR 204-205.

  > "The seventy weeks, or 490 years, especially allotted to the Jews, ended, as we have seen, in A.D. 34.… Then the message of salvation, no longer restricted to the chosen people, was given to the world." — GC 328.

- **Acts 1:8** — "Ye shall be witnesses unto me both in Jerusalem... and unto the uttermost part of the earth." The widening of the gospel beyond the Jews waits on the close of their seventy weeks.

  > "Then the message of salvation, no longer restricted to the chosen people, was given to the world. The disciples, forced by persecution to flee from Jerusalem, 'went everywhere preaching the word.'" — GC 328.

- **Acts 3:25** — "Ye are the children of the prophets, and of the covenant" — within the 70th week (A.D. 27-34) the offer of the covenant goes "to you first," to the Jews; only at A.D. 34 does it pass to all nations.

  > "During this time, extending from A.D. 27 to A.D. 34, Christ, at first in person and afterward by His disciples, extended the gospel invitation especially to the Jews. As the apostles went forth with the good tidings of the kingdom, the Saviour's direction was: 'Go not into the way of the Gentiles… but go rather to the lost sheep of the house of Israel.'" — GC 327-328.

- **The martyrdom of Stephen marks A.D. 34.** The Sanhedrin "sealed its rejection of the gospel by the martyrdom of Stephen and the persecution of the followers of Christ" (Acts 9:1-18); the scattered disciples "went everywhere preaching," and the apostles turned to the Gentiles — exactly the event due when the Jews' allotted period expired.

  > "This date is marked by the martyrdom of Stephen, the formal rejection of the gospel of Christ by the Jewish Sanhedrin in the persecution of his disciples, and the turning of the apostles to the Gentiles. Acts 9:1-18." — DAR 204-205.

  > "Through the action of the Jewish Sanhedrin, the nation sealed its rejection of the gospel by the martyrdom of Stephen and the persecution of the followers of Christ.… The disciples, forced by persecution to flee from Jerusalem, 'went everywhere preaching the word.'" — GC 328.

### The keystone arithmetic: 490 from 2300 → 1844

| Event                                             | Reference                 | Date                 |
| ------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------- | -------------------- |
| Decree to Ezra — "going forth of the commandment" | Ezra 7; Dan. 9:25         | autumn 457 B.C.      |
| 7 weeks (49 yrs): street & wall rebuilt           | Dan. 9:25                 | B.C. 408             |
| 69 weeks (483 yrs): Messiah anointed at baptism   | Dan. 9:25; Mark 1:15      | autumn A.D. 27       |
| Midst of 70th week: Messiah cut off (crucified)   | Dan. 9:26-27; Matt. 27:51 | spring A.D. 31       |
| 70 weeks (490 yrs) end: gospel to the Gentiles    | Dan. 9:24; Acts 9         | autumn A.D. 34       |
| Remainder of the 2300: 2300 − 490 = 1810 yrs      | Dan. 8:14                 | A.D. 34 + 1810       |
| 2300 days end: "the sanctuary shall be cleansed"  | Dan. 8:14                 | autumn **A.D. 1844** |

- **2300 − 490 = 1810.** "The seventy weeks are but the first 490 years of the 2300. Take 490 from 2300, and there remain 1810." The 490 ended in the autumn of A.D. 34.

  > "The seventy weeks are but the first 490 years of the 2300. Take 490 from 2300, and there remain 1810. The 490, as we have seen, ended in the autumn of A.D. 34." — DAR 206-207.

  > "The seventy weeks — 490 days — having been cut off from the 2300, there were 1810 days remaining. After the end of 490 days, the 1810 days were still to be fulfilled." — GC 328.

- **A.D. 34 + 1810 = autumn 1844.** "If to this date we now add the remaining 1810 years... we have the autumn of A.D. 1844. Thus speedily and surely do we find the termination of the 2300 days, when once the seventy weeks have been located."

  > "If to this date we now add the remaining 1810 years, we shall have the termination of the whole period. Thus, to A.D. 34, autumn, add 1810, and we have the autumn of A.D. 1844. Thus speedily and surely do we find the termination of the 2300 days, when once the seventy weeks have been located." — DAR 206-207.

  > "From A.D. 34, 1810 years extend to 1844. Consequently the 2300 days of Daniel 8:14 terminate in 1844. At the expiration of this great prophetic period, upon the testimony of the angel of God, 'the sanctuary shall be cleansed.'" — GC 328.

  > "The first 490 years of the 2300 having then ended, 1810 years would remain. From A.D. 34, 1810 years extend to 1844. 'Then,' said the angel, 'shall the sanctuary be cleansed.'" — GC 410.

- **The same chain in The Great Controversy.** "The seventy weeks — 490 days — having been cut off from the 2300, there were 1810 days remaining... From A.D. 34, 1810 years extend to 1844. Consequently the 2300 days of Daniel 8:14 terminate in 1844."

  > "The seventy weeks — 490 days — having been cut off from the 2300, there were 1810 days remaining.… From A.D. 34, 1810 years extend to 1844. Consequently the 2300 days of Daniel 8:14 terminate in 1844." — GC 328.

  > "The first 490 years of the 2300 having then ended, 1810 years would remain. From A.D. 34, 1810 years extend to 1844.… All the preceding specifications of the prophecy had been unquestionably fulfilled at the time appointed." — GC 410.

- **Miller's witness (1840s).** "The seventy weeks were evidently fulfilled in the year A.D. 33, beginning 457 years B.C., at the going forth of the commandment to Ezra to restore the law and the people to Jerusalem" — Gabriel was "so particular in defining the beginning and the end" precisely because definite time was meant.

  > "The seventy weeks were evidently fulfilled in the year A.D. 33, beginning 457 years B.C., at the going forth of the commandment to Ezra to restore the law and the people to Jerusalem.… Why should the man Gabriel be so particular in defining the beginning and the end of the seventy weeks, if indefinite time only is meant?" — MWV1 43.

- **Litch's witness.** Daniel's prayer over Jeremiah's seventy years is "the true connecting link between the vision of the ram and goat of the 8th chapter and the prophecy of seventy weeks"; Gabriel corrects Daniel — not seventy years of captivity, but seventy sevens "cut off" for the people.

  > "This prayer of Daniel is the true connecting link between the vision of the ram and goat of the 8th chapter and the prophecy of seventy weeks, or the seventy sevens.… Seventy weeks, or sevens, are determined, or cut off, for them to accomplish the national 'transgression, and make an end of sins.'" — PREX1 130.

- **Why it must be the autumn.** It takes 457 full years B.C. and 1843 full years A.D. to make 2300; since Ezra reached Jerusalem only in the fifth month and the work began in the seventh (autumn) of 457, the whole period runs to the seventh month — autumn, Jewish time — of 1844.

  > "It takes 457 full years before Christ, and 1843 full years after, to make 2300.… the period is to be dated, not from the starting of Ezra from Babylon, but from the actual commencement of the work at Jerusalem; which it is not probable could be earlier than the seventh month (autumn) of 457.… The whole period would therefore extend to the seventh month, autumn, Jewish time, of 1844." — DAR 207.

- **The whole arch stands or falls here.** As the pioneers said: "If the connection between the seventy weeks of Daniel 9 and the 2300 days of Daniel 8 does not exist, the whole system is shaken to its foundation; if it does exist, as we suppose, the system must stand." Every specification of Daniel 9 was "unquestionably fulfilled at the time appointed" — fixing 457 B.C. and, with it, 1844 beyond reasonable doubt.

  > "'If the connection between the seventy weeks of Daniel 9 and the 2300 days of Daniel 8 does not exist, the whole system is shaken to its foundation; if it does exist, as we suppose, the system must stand.' — Harmony of the Prophetic Chronology, p. 33." — DAR 195.

  > "All the preceding specifications of the prophecy had been unquestionably fulfilled at the time appointed.… To deny that the days ended at that time was to involve the whole question in confusion, and to renounce positions which had been established by unmistakable fulfillments of prophecy." — GC 410.

---

# 7. The Daily, and the 1290 and 1335 Days

> _The deeper time-lines of Daniel 11 and 12 hang upon one disputed word — "the daily" (Heb. tamid, the continual). It is not the Jewish sacrifice, but the continual desolating power, paganism, which had to be taken away to make room for the papal "abomination that maketh desolate." That removal was accomplished in A.D. 508; from it run the 1290 years to 1798 (508 + 1290) and the 1335 years to 1843/44 (508 + 1335). The "time of the end" opens in 1798 with the wounding of the papacy, and the blessing on him "that waiteth, and cometh to the thousand three hundred and five and thirty days" fell upon the advent believers of 1843-44. (DAR 154-157, 254-258, 313-316; GC 356, 439.)_

### "The Daily" — the continual abomination is paganism, not sacrifice

- **Daniel 8:11** — The little horn "magnified himself even to the prince of the host, and by him the daily was taken away." The word "sacrifice" is supplied by the translators; the act belongs to Rome in both phases, pagan and papal.

  > "This little horn must be understood to symbolize Rome in its entire history, including its two phases, pagan and papal. These two phases are elsewhere spoken of as the 'daily' (sacrifice is a supplied word) and the 'transgression of desolation;' the daily (desolation) signifying the pagan form, and the transgression of desolation, the papal.… 'By him' (the papal form) 'the daily' (the pagan form) 'was taken away.' Pagan Rome was remodeled into papal Rome." — DAR 154.

- **Daniel 8:12** — "An host was given him against the daily by reason of transgression." The barbarians who overran Rome were themselves converted to the Catholic faith and became "the instruments of the dethronement of their former religion," perpetuating the empire in another form.

  > "The barbarians that subverted the Roman empire in the changes, attritions, and transformations of those times, became converts to the Catholic faith, and the instruments of the dethronement of their former religion. Though conquering Rome politically, they were themselves vanquished religiously by the theology of Rome, and became the perpetrators of the same empire in another phase." — DAR 155.

- **Daniel 8:13** — "How long shall be the vision concerning the daily, and the transgression of desolation?" Two desolating powers are set side by side — the daily (paganism) and the transgression of desolation (the papacy) — and the question asks how long both shall tread down the sanctuary.

  > "These two verses close the vision proper of chapter 8; and they introduce the one remaining point which of all others would naturally be of the most absorbing interest to the prophet and to all the church; namely, the time the desolating powers previously brought to view were to continue. How long shall they continue their course of oppression against God's people, and of blasphemy against high Heaven?" — DAR 156.

  > "Then we have two desolating powers, which for a long period oppress, or desolate the church.… By the 'continuance of desolation,' or the perpetual desolation, we must understand that paganism, through all its long history, is meant; and by 'the transgression of desolation' is meant the papacy." — DAR 157.

- **The word itself** — "Daily" (tamid) occurs in the Old Testament about one hundred and two times, "and is, in the great majority of instances, rendered continual or continually. The idea of sacrifice does not attach to the word at all." Hence "the continual" = paganism, the continual desolating power.

  > "The word here rendered daily occurs in the Old Testament, according to the Hebrew Concordance, one hundred and two times, and is, in the great majority of instances, rendered continual or continually. The idea of sacrifice does not attach to the word at all. Nor is there any word in the text which signifies sacrifice; that is wholly a supplied word." — DAR 157.

### The taking away of the daily — A.D. 508

- **Daniel 11:31** — "And arms shall stand on his part, and they shall pollute the sanctuary of strength, and shall take away the daily, and they shall place the abomination that maketh desolate." The taking away of paganism is the historical key to both the 1290 and 1335 days.

  > "The power of the empire was committed to the carrying on of the work before mentioned. 'And they shall pollute the sanctuary of strength,' or Rome.… it would signify the removal of the seat of empire from Rome to Constantinople, which contributed its measure of influence to the downfall of Rome. The passage would then be parallel to Daniel 8:11 and Revelation 13:2." — DAR 254.

- **Conversion of Clovis, 496** — Until the conversion of Clovis, king of France, "the French and other nations of Western Rome were pagan; but subsequently to that event, the efforts to convert idolaters to Romanism were crowned with great success." This prepared the crisis of 508.

  > "As we approach the year A. D. 508, we behold a grand crisis ripening between Catholicism and the pagan influences still existing in the empire. Up to the time of the conversion of Clovis, king of France, A. D. 496, the French and other nations of Western Rome were pagan; but subsequently to that event, the efforts to convert idolaters to Romanism were crowned with great success." — DAR 255.

- **508 — paganism subdued** — "From the time when these successes were fully accomplished, namely, 508, the papacy was triumphant so far as paganism was concerned." In that very year a Christian king (Arthur) was crowned in Britain, and at Constantinople the Catholic party for the first time "waged a successful war against both the civil authority of the empire and the church of the East."

  > "From the time when these successes were fully accomplished, namely, 508, the papacy was triumphant so far as paganism was concerned.… In England, Arthur, the first Christian king, founded the Christian worship on the ruins of the pagan.… he was elected monarch of Britain in 508." — DAR 256.

  > "Let it be marked that in this year, 508, paganism had so far declined, and Catholicism had so far relatively increased in strength, that the Catholic Church for the first time waged a successful war against both the civil authority of the empire and the church of the East, which had for the most part embraced the Monophysite doctrine. The extermination of 65,000 heretics was the result." — DAR 257.

- **The mark of the year** — "In 508 the mine is sprung beneath the throne of the Eastern empire." Paganism, as a controlling power, was broken; the way lay open for the papal abomination to be set up thirty years later, in 538.

  > "In 508 the mine is sprung beneath the throne of the Eastern empire. The result of the confusion and strife it occasions is the humiliation of its rightful lord. Now the question is, At what time was paganism so far suppressed as to make room for its substitute and successor, the papal abomination?… Is there any other date for its being 'placed,' or 'set up,' in the room of paganism but 508?" — DAR 258.

### The 1290 days (years) — 508 to 1798

- **Daniel 12:11** — "And from the time that the daily shall be taken away, and the abomination that maketh desolate set up, there shall be a thousand two hundred and ninety days." The margin reads "to set up the abomination," fixing the start at the removal of paganism.

  > "The margin reads, 'To set up the abomination,' etc. With this reading the text would stand thus: 'And from the time that the daily sacrifice shall be taken away to set up [or in order to set up] the abomination that maketh desolate, there shall be a thousand two hundred and ninety days.'" — DAR 313.

- **The reckoning** — "The daily has already been shown to be... the daily or continual abomination, that is, paganism.... For the historical events showing how this was accomplished in 508, see on chapter 11:31.... Counting back, then, 1290 years from 1798, we have the year 508, where it has been shown that paganism was taken away, thirty years before the setting up of the papacy." 508 + 1290 = 1798.

  > "The daily has already been shown to be, not the daily sacrifice of the Jews, but the daily or continual abomination, that is, paganism. (See on chapter 8:13.) This had to be taken away to prepare the way for the papacy.… Counting back, then, 1290 years from 1798, we have the year 508, where it has been shown that paganism was taken away, thirty years before the setting up of the papacy." — DAR 313.

- **Both periods end together** — "The two periods, therefore, the 1290 and the 1260 days, terminate together in 1798, the one beginning in 538, and the other in 508, thirty years previous."

  > "The two periods, therefore, the 1290 and the 1260 days, terminate together in 1798, the one beginning in 538, and the other in 508, thirty years previous." — DAR 313-314.

- **Daniel 12:7** — The man clothed in linen sware "that it shall be for a time, times, and an half." This is the 1260 years of papal supremacy (538-1798); "this period... began with the supremacy of the papacy, A.D. 538, and terminated in 1798." Its close fixes the inner edge of the time of the end.

  > "The forty and two months are the same as the 'time and times and the dividing of time,' three years and a half, or 1260 days, of Daniel 7—the time during which the papal power was to oppress God's people. This period, as stated in preceding chapters, began with the supremacy of the papacy, A.D. 538, and terminated in 1798. At that time the pope was made captive by the French army, the papal power received its deadly wound." — GC 439.

- **Miller's summary** — "His 1290 days, beginning when the ten kings... should be converted to the Christian faith, and the daily sacrifice abomination taken out of the way, viz., A.D. 508, which would end us in 1798, when the Pope lost his power to reign over the kings... by being deprived of his civil power by Bonaparte."

  > "By the numbers given in Daniel 12:11-13, his 1290 days, beginning when the ten kings, represented by the ten toes in Nebuchadnezzar's dream, and ten horns in Daniel's vision, should be converted to the Christian faith, and the daily sacrifice abomination taken out of the way, viz, A. D. 508, which would end us in 1798, when the Pope lost his power to reign over the kings and trample on the holy people, and the abomination of desolation ceased his civil reign, by being deprived of his civil power by Bonaparte." — MWV2 113.

### The 1335 days (years) — 508 to 1843, and the blessing on those who wait

- **Daniel 12:12** — "Blessed is he that waiteth, and cometh to the thousand three hundred and five and thirty days." This period begins from the same point as the 1290 — 508 — for it is "spoken of in immediate connection with the 1290 years, which commenced... in 508." 508 + 1335 = 1843.

  > "The only clue we have to the solution of this question, is the fact that it is spoken of in immediate connection with the 1290 years, which commenced, as shown above, in 508.… 'Blessed is he that waiteth and cometh to the 1335 days.' From what point? — From the same point, undoubtedly, as that from which the 1290 date; namely, 508.… From this point they would extend to 1843; for 1335 added to 508 make 1843." — DAR 314.

- **Miller's reckoning** — "I then showed you that the number 1335 days, beginning at the same time as the 1290 days, viz. A.D. 508, would end in 1843.... this brought us to the same year that Daniel's whole number, 2300, brought us." The forty-five years' difference between 1290 and 1335 spans 1798 to 1843.

  > "I then showed you that the number 1335 days, beginning at the same time as the 1290 days, viz. A. D. 508, would end in 1843, at the resurrection, for Daniel would stand in his lot at the end of these days. And you have undoubtedly notice that this brought us to the same year that Daniel's whole number, 2300, brought us, which is forty-five years, the difference between the two numbers, 1290 and 1335." — MWV2 113.

  > "Therefore take 1335 years from 1810 years, would leave 475 years; and he could reckon from the end of the 70 weeks, or 490 years, to the end of pagan Rome, would be 475, from thence to the time he should stand in his lot, would be 1335 years." — MWV2 100.

- **The blessing identified** — The thing promised at the close of the 1335 days "is a blessing unto those who wait and come to that time; that is, those who are then living" — the joy of receiving the advent truth. "If a new and glorious truth was a blessing in the days of Christ to those who received it, why was it not equally so in A.D. 1843?"

  > "The only thing promised at the end of the 1335 days is a blessing unto those who wait and come to that time; that is, those who are then living. What is this blessing? Looking at the year 1843, when these years expired, what do we behold? — We see a remarkable fulfillment of prophecy in the great proclamation of the second coming of Christ." — DAR 314-315.

  > "If a new and glorious truth was a blessing in the days of Christ to those who received it, why was it not equally so in A. D. 1843?" — DAR 315.

- **Marked by a prophetic period** — The advent movement was worthy to be set off by a time prophecy, "since our Lord, in verse 14 of his great prophecy of Matthew 24, makes a special announcement of this movement; and since it is still further set forth in Revelation 14:6, 7, under the symbol of an angel flying through mid-heaven."

  > "Why not, since the period in which it was to occur; namely, the time of the end, is introduced by a prophetic period; since our Lord, in verse 14 of his great prophecy of Matthew 24, makes a special announcement of this movement; and since it is still further set forth in Revelation 14:6, 7, under the symbol of an angel flying through mid-heaven with a special announcement of the everlasting gospel to the inhabitants of the earth? Surely the Bible gives great prominence to this movement." — DAR 315-316.

- **Daniel 12:13** — "But go thou thy way till the end be: for thou shalt rest, and stand in thy lot at the end of the days." The "days" here reach back to the 2300 (Dan 8:14), which "terminated in 1844, and brought us to the cleansing of the sanctuary." Daniel stands in his lot when his case is decided in the judgment, "in the person of his Advocate, our great High Priest."

  > "The 2300 days, as has been already shown, terminated in 1844, and brought us to the cleansing of the sanctuary. How did Daniel at that time stand in his lot? Answer: In the person of his Advocate, our great High Priest, as he presents the cases of the righteous for acceptance to his Father.… And when Daniel's case comes up for examination, he is found righteous, stands in his lot, is assigned a place in the heavenly Canaan." — DAR 316.

### Matthew 24:15 — Christ's own seal on Daniel's "abomination"

- **Matthew 24:15** — "When ye therefore shall see the abomination of desolation, spoken of by Daniel the prophet, stand in the holy place... let him understand." Christ Himself points His church back to Daniel's prophecy of the desolating powers, vouching for the prophetic time-lines and bidding the reader understand.

  > "Daniel, in the ninth chapter, speaks of desolations and abominations, plural. More than one abomination, therefore, treads down the church; that is, so far as the church is concerned, both paganism and the papacy are abominations. But as distinguished from each other, the language is restricted, and one is the 'daily' desolation, and the other is pre-eminently the transgression or 'abomination' of desolation." — DAR 255.

  > "Unless they are to be reckoned from this point, it is impossible to locate them, and they must be excepted from the prophecy of Daniel when we apply to it the words of Christ, 'Whoso readeth, let him understand.' Matthew 24:15." — DAR 314.

### "The time of the end" — opened in 1798

- **Daniel 12:4** — "Shut up the words, and seal the book, even to the time of the end: many shall run to and fro, and knowledge shall be increased." The book of Daniel was sealed only "till the time of the end," after which it would be opened and understood.

  > "'Many running to and fro.' This is another important and evident sign of the end. Daniel 12:4, 'But thou, O Daniel, shut up; the words and seal the book, even to the time of the end. Many shall run to and fro.'… All must acknowledge, that this text is remarkably fulfilled in this day." — MWV2 289.

- **The end of the 1290 fixes the date** — Because the 1290 closes the long oppression of "the holy people" in 1798, that year opens "the time of the end." From it the book of Daniel was unsealed.

  > "Counting back, then, 1290 years from 1798, we have the year 508, where it has been shown that paganism was taken away, thirty years before the setting up of the papacy.… The two periods, therefore, the 1290 and the 1260 days, terminate together in 1798, the one beginning in 538, and the other in 508, thirty years previous." — DAR 313-314.
