  > "This period, as stated in preceding chapters, began with the supremacy of the papacy, A.D. 538, and terminated in 1798. At that time the pope was made captive by the French army, the papal power received its deadly wound." — GC 439.

- **GC: 1798 unsealing** — "Since 1798 the book of Daniel has been unsealed, knowledge of the prophecies has increased, and many have proclaimed the solemn message of the judgment near." Martin Luther had placed the judgment three centuries in his future; only after 1798 did the advent message go forth.

  > "The Reformers did not proclaim it. Martin Luther placed the judgment about three hundred years in the future from his day. But since 1798 the book of Daniel has been unsealed, knowledge of the prophecies has increased, and many have proclaimed the solemn message of the judgment near." — GC 356.

- **The two signs** — "Many shall run to and fro, and knowledge shall be increased." Miller marks both as evident signs of the end, "literally true; in this day of invention and improvement, knowledge increase[s]," fulfilled in the Bible, tract, and Sabbath-school societies and the worldwide advent awakening.

  > "If it means missionaries of the cross, no man can dispute the fulfillment. See the heralds of salvation crossing and re-crossing on every part of the habitable globe.… No man, unless he is wilfully ignorant, can deny that this sign is not actually and literally fulfilled." — MWV2 289.

  > "'Even to the time of the end many shall run to and fro, and knowledge shall be increased.' View this in any point you please, whether theological or scientifical, it is literally true; in this day of invention and improvement, knowledge increase. What of the fifty different moral societies, which have become general in the Christian world? Is there no increase of knowledge in our Bible societies, Sabbath schools, tract societies, temperance societies…?" — MWV2 290.

- **Daniel 12:9-10** — "The words are closed up and sealed till the time of the end.... none of the wicked shall understand; but the wise shall understand." The seal is not that the prophecy cannot be known, but that its full meaning waited for the time of the end, when the wise — the advent believers — would understand it.

  > "The time of the end was the time in which the Spirit of God was to break the seal from off this book; and consequently this was the time during which the wise should understand, while the wicked, lost to all sense of the value of eternal truth, with hearts callous and hardened in sin, would grow continually more wicked and more blind." — DAR 311-312.

  > "they are sealed, that is, made sure, unalterable, will stand until every word has its fulfillment… not, as some suppose, that Daniel's prophecy is sealed, closed up, out of sight, and cannot be understood.… 'But the wise shall understand,' says the angel. What shall the wise understand? They shall understand the vision." — MWV2 100.

### Daniel 12:1 — Michael standing up closes the line

- **Daniel 12:1** — "And at that time shall Michael stand up, the great prince... and there shall be a time of trouble, such as never was." "At that time" reaches back to the planting of the king of the north — the events following the time of the end. Michael is "the archangel" (Jude 9), "our Lord Jesus Christ."

  > "A DEFINITE time is introduced in this verse… 'At that time.' What time? — The time to which we are brought by the closing verse of the preceding chapter, — the time when the king of the north shall plant the tabernacles of his palace in the glorious holy mountain.… And when this event takes place, he is to come to his end; and then, according to this verse, we look for the standing up of Michael, the great prince." — DAR 293.

  > "Who, then, is Michael? and what is his standing up? — Michael is called, in Jude 9, the archangel. This means the chief angel, or the head over the angels.… The voice of the Son of God is the voice of the archangel; the archangel, then, is the Son of God. But the archangel is Michael; hence also Michael is the Son of God." — DAR 294.

- **The standing up = taking the kingdom** — As "there shall stand up yet three kings in Persia" means to reign, so Michael's standing up is His taking His own kingdom: "the work of mercy is done, and the probation of our race is ended." The deliverance of "every one that shall be found written in the book" closes the chain begun with the taking away of the daily.

  > "The key to the interpretation of this expression is furnished us in verses 2 and 3 of chapter 11: 'There shall stand up yet three kings in Persia;' 'A mighty king shall stand up, that shall reign with great dominion.'… They signify to take the kingdom, to reign.… At that time, Michael shall stand up, shall take the kingdom, shall commence his reign.… The work of mercy is done, and the probation of our race is ended." — DAR 295.

- **Daniel 12:2-3** — "Many of them that sleep in the dust of the earth shall awake... and they that be wise shall shine as the brightness of the firmament." The same "wise" who understand the unsealed book at the time of the end (12:10) are raised and glorified at its end — the reward of those who waited and came to the 1335 days.

  > "From this point they would extend to 1843; for 1335 added to 508 make 1843. Commencing in the spring of the former year, they ended in the spring of the latter." — DAR 314.

  > "would not Daniel's mind naturally turn to the principal and longest period, the 2300 days, rather than to any of its subdivisions? If this is so, the 2300 are the days intended." — DAR 316.

### The day-year principle that measures these periods

- **Numbers 14:34** — "After the number of the days... each day for a year, shall ye bear your iniquities." The divinely-given rule: a prophetic day stands for a literal year.

  > "We have here a new prophetic period introduced; namely, 1290 prophetic days, which would denote the same number of literal years." — DAR 313.

- **Ezekiel 4:6** — "I have appointed thee each day for a year." The same key, by which 1290 days = 1290 years and 1335 days = 1335 years.

  > "Still another prophetic period is here introduced, denoting 1335 years.… From that point there shall be, says the prophet, 1290 days. And the very next sentence reads, 'Blessed is he that waiteth and cometh to the 1335 days.'" — DAR 314.

| Period                            | Text                 | Begins   | Ends    | Event                                                 |
| --------------------------------- | -------------------- | -------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------- |
| 1260 / "time, times, and an half" | Dan. 12:7; Rev. 13:5 | 538      | 1798    | Papal supremacy / deadly wound                        |
| 1290 days                         | Dan. 12:11           | 508      | 1798    | Daily (paganism) taken away → time of the end         |
| 1335 days                         | Dan. 12:12           | 508      | 1843/44 | Blessing on the advent believers                      |
| 2300 days                         | Dan. 8:14            | 457 B.C. | 1844    | Cleansing of the sanctuary / Daniel stands in his lot |

# Part III — The Revelation

---

# 8. The Seven Churches of Revelation 1-3

> _The messages to the seven churches of Asia are not seven local letters merely, but a continuous prophecy: seven successive periods of the Christian church, running from apostolic Ephesus to the judgment-hour Laodicea. Each name, in its very meaning, fits the spiritual condition of its age; together they span the whole gospel dispensation, the number seven denoting fulness and completeness. This is no novel view — Bishop Newton, More, and Vitringa held it before us. (DAR 328-329, 345-347; GC 381-382.)_

### The seal of the seven: stars and candlesticks defined

- **Revelation 1:11** — "What thou seest, write in a book, and send it unto the seven churches which are in Asia; unto Ephesus, and unto Smyrna, and unto Pergamos, and unto Thyatira, and unto Sardis, and unto Philadelphia, and unto Laodicea." The seven are named in fixed order; that order is the order of history, apostolic to advent.

  > "Some reasons why the seven churches, or more properly the messages to them, should be regarded as prophetic, having their application to seven distinct periods covering the Christian age, have been given in the … remarks on chapter 1:4. It may here be added that this view is neither new nor local." — DAR 345.3.

- **Revelation 1:20** — "The seven stars are the angels of the seven churches: and the seven candlesticks which thou sawest are the seven churches." Scripture defines its own symbols: the lampstand is the church; the star is its messenger.

  > "The seven stars which the Son of man held in his right hand (verse 20), are declared to be the angels of the seven churches. The angels of the churches, doubtless all will agree, are the ministers of the churches." — DAR 328.3.

  > "John, looking into the Christian dispensation, saw only seven candlesticks, representing seven churches, in the midst of which stood the Son of man.… The whole gospel church in seven divisions, or periods, must be symbolized by them; and hence the seven churches must be applied in the same manner." — DAR 329.1.

- **Revelation 1:20** (the stars) — The seven stars held in Christ's right hand "are the angels of the seven churches," and an angel of a church is its minister; held in His right hand, they denote the upholding, guidance, and protection given to all true ministers of the whole gospel age.

  > "Their being held in the right hand of the Son of man denotes the upholding power, guidance, and protection vouchsafed to them.… May not, rather, all the true ministers of the whole gospel age derive from this representation the consolation of knowing that they are upheld and guided by the right hand of the great Head of the church?" — DAR 328.3.

- **Revelation 1:20** (the candlesticks) — John saw only seven lampstands, "and seven is a number denoting fulness and completeness"; so the whole gospel church, in seven divisions or periods, is symbolized — the Son of man standing in the midst to take cognizance of all His churches through the dispensation.

  > "The position of the Son of man in their midst must denote his presence with them, his watchcare over them, and his searching scrutiny of all their works.… Seven, as used in the Scriptures, is a number denoting fulness and completeness.… The whole gospel church in seven divisions, or periods, must be symbolized by them." — DAR 329.1.

- **Revelation 1:11; Revelation 1:20** (why prophetic) — "Many contend... that the seven epistles are prophetical of so many successive periods, or states, of the church, from the beginning to the conclusion of all" — the view is "neither new nor local."

  > "This view is neither new nor local. Benson quotes Bishop Newton as saying, 'Many contend, and among them such learned men as More and Vitringa, that the seven epistles are prophetical of so many successive periods, or states, of the church, from the beginning to the conclusion of all.'" — DAR 345.3.

### The seven periods in order, with the meaning of each name

| #   | Church       | Name signifies                                   | Period (approx.) | Condition                                       |
| --- | ------------ | ------------------------------------------------ | ---------------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| 1   | Ephesus      | "desirable"                                      | 31-100           | Apostolic purity; first love                    |
| 2   | Smyrna       | "myrrh"                                          | 100-313          | Persecuted; sweet-smelling savor                |
| 3   | Pergamos     | "height, elevation"                              | 313-538          | Worldly exaltation; mystery of iniquity         |
| 4   | Thyatira     | "sweet savor of labor / sacrifice of contrition" | 538-1798         | Papal supremacy; the long tribulation           |
| 5   | Sardis       | "that which remains / song of joy"               | 1798-1833        | The reformed churches; a name to live, yet dead |
| 6   | Philadelphia | "brotherly love"                                 | 1833-1844        | The Advent movement; the open door              |
| 7   | Laodicea     | "the judging of the people"                      | 1844-advent      | Judgment-hour church; lukewarm, counseled       |

_(Name-meanings and periods: DAR 347.1, 352.3-354.5, 359.1, 363.3, 366.3, 371.1.)_

### 1. Ephesus — apostolic purity (A.D. 31-100)

- **Revelation 2:1** — "These things saith he that holdeth the seven stars in his right hand, who walketh in the midst of the seven golden candlesticks." Christ opens to the first church as the One who upholds the ministry and watches over all the lampstands.

  > "The angel of a church must denote a messenger, or minister, of that church; and as these churches each cover a period of time, the angel of each church must denote the ministry, or all the true ministers of Christ during the period covered by that church." — DAR 347.2.

- **Revelation 2:2-3** — "I know thy works, and thy labour, and thy patience... thou hast tried them which say they are apostles, and are not, and hast found them liars." The early Christians had the doctrine of Christ in purity, the gifts of the Spirit, and tried and exposed false apostles. The name Ephesus, "desirable," well describes the church in its first state.

  > "The definition of the word Ephesus, is desirable, which may well be taken as a good descriptive term of the character and condition of the church in its first state. Those early Christians had received the doctrine of Christ in its purity. They enjoyed the benefits and blessings of the gifts of the Holy Spirit.… and they tried false apostles, searched out their true characters, and found them liars." — DAR 347.1.

- **Revelation 2:4** — "Nevertheless I have somewhat against thee, because thou hast left thy first love." Even the apostolic age cooled toward its close; the decline begins.

  > "'I have somewhat against thee,' says Christ, 'because thou hast left thy first love.'… 'The charge here is not that of falling from grace, nor that love is extinguished, but diminished. No zeal, no suffering can atone for the want of first love.'" — DAR 347.3-348.

- **Revelation 2:5** — "Repent... or else I will come unto thee quickly, and will remove thy candlestick out of his place." The threat to remove the lampstand confirms the candlestick is the church-period itself.
- **Revelation 2:6-7** — "Thou hatest the deeds of the Nicolaitans, which I also hate.... To him that overcometh will I give to eat of the tree of life." Reward to the overcomer of every age is Eden restored.

  > "The first church named is Ephesus. According to the application here made, this would cover the first, or apostolic age of the church.… In faithfulness to the pure principles taught by Christ, they could not bear those that were evil." — DAR 347.1.

### 2. Smyrna — the persecuted church (A.D. 100-313)

- **Revelation 2:8** — "These things saith the first and the last, which was dead, and is alive." The risen Christ presents Himself to a dying, persecuted people as the One who Himself conquered death.

  > "Smyrna signifies myrrh, fit appellation for the church of God while passing through the fiery furnace of persecution, and proving herself a 'sweet-smelling savor' unto him." — DAR 354.1.

- **Revelation 2:9** — "I know thy works, and tribulation, and poverty, (but thou art rich)." Smyrna signifies "myrrh," a fit name for the church passing through the fiery furnace of persecution and proving herself a "sweet-smelling savor" unto God.

  > "Smyrna signifies myrrh, fit appellation for the church of God while passing through the fiery furnace of persecution, and proving herself a 'sweet-smelling savor' unto him. But we soon reach the days of Constantine, when the church presents a new phase." — DAR 354.1.

- **Revelation 2:10** — "Ye shall have tribulation ten days: be thou faithful unto death, and I will give thee a crown of life." The "ten days" are prophetic: ten years (day-for-a-year), fulfilled in the last and bloodiest of the ten persecutions, under Diocletian, A.D. 302-312.

  > "As this message is prophetic, the time mentioned in it must also be regarded as prophetic, and would denote ten years. And it is a noticeable fact that the last and most bloody of the ten persecutions continued just ten years, under Diocletian, from A. D. 302 to A. D. 312." — DAR 352.3-353.

- **Revelation 2:11** — "He that overcometh shall not be hurt of the second death." To the martyr church, the promise that the worst the persecutor can do reaches only the first death.

### 3. Pergamos — worldly exaltation (A.D. 313-538)

- **Revelation 2:12** — "These things saith he which hath the sharp sword with two edges." To a church tempted to compromise, Christ comes as the One whose word divides truth from error.

  > "We now reach a period when influences began to work through which errors and evils were likely to creep into the church." — DAR 354.4.

- **Revelation 2:13** — "I know thy works, and where thou dwellest, even where Satan's seat is." Pergamos signifies "height, elevation" — the period from Constantine's professed conversion (A.D. 323) to the establishment of the papacy (A.D. 538), when the church was exalted into worldly favor.

  > "The word Pergamos signifies hight, elevation. The period covered by this church may be located from the days of Constantine, or perhaps, rather, from his professed conversion to Christianity, A. D. 323, to the establishment of the papacy, A. D. 538." — DAR 354.5.

- **Revelation 2:14-15** — "Them that hold the doctrine of Balaam... so hast thou also them that hold the doctrine of the Nicolaitans." Worldly policy, pride, and popularity bred false doctrine — the working of the "mystery of iniquity" that ripened into the papal man of sin.

  > "It was a period in which the true servants of God had to struggle against a spirit of worldly policy, pride, and popularity among the professed followers of Christ, and against the virulent workings of the mystery of iniquity, which finally resulted in the full development of the papal man of sin." — DAR 354.5.

- **Revelation 2:16-17** — "Repent; or else I will come unto thee quickly, and will fight against them with the sword of my mouth.... To him that overcometh will I give to eat of the hidden manna." Christ's remedy is the sword of His mouth — His word — against corrupting doctrine.

### 4. Thyatira — papal supremacy and the long tribulation (538-1798)

- **Revelation 2:18** — "These things saith the Son of God, who hath his eyes like unto a flame of fire, and his feet are like fine brass." Searching eyes for the longest and darkest period of the church.

  > "Thyatira signifies 'sweet savor of labor,' or 'sacrifice of contrition.' This would well describe the state of the church of Jesus Christ during the long period of papal triumph and persecution." — DAR 359.1.

- **Revelation 2:19** — "I know thy works, and charity, and service, and faith, and thy patience... and the last to be more than the first." Thyatira ("sweet savor of labor," "sacrifice of contrition") is the one church commended for spiritual _improvement_ — believers grew in grace amid the great tribulation such as never was.

  > "This age of such dreadful tribulation upon the church as never was … improved the religious condition of believers.… There had been an improvement in their condition, a growth in grace.… This church is the only one that is commended for an improvement in spiritual things." — DAR 359.1.

- **Revelation 2:20-23** — "Thou sufferest that woman Jezebel, which calleth herself a prophetess, to teach and to seduce my servants." Jezebel pictures the apostate, persecuting power tolerated within Christendom during the 1260 years of papal triumph (538-1798).

  > "As in the preceding church Antipas denoted, not an individual, but a class of persons, so, doubtless, Jezebel is here to be understood in the same sense." — DAR 359.2.

- **Revelation 2:24-25** — "Unto the rest in Thyatira... which have not known the depths of Satan... that which ye have already hold fast till I come." A faithful remnant is addressed through the whole supremacy; "till I come" carries this church across the centuries.
- **Revelation 2:26-28** — "He that overcometh, and keepeth my works unto the end, to him will I give power over the nations.... And I will give him the morning star." The overcomers of the dark ages are promised dominion and Christ Himself, the Morning Star.

### 5. Sardis — the reformed churches grown formal (1798-1833)

- **Revelation 3:1** — "These things saith he that hath the seven Spirits of God, and the seven stars; I know thy works, that thou hast a name that thou livest, and art dead." Sardis ("that which remains," "song of joy") is the reformed churches — having a name to live, but sunk into formality.

  > "Sardis signifies 'prince or song of joy,' or 'that which remains.' We then have before us, as constituting this church, the reformed churches.… The great fault found with this church is that it has a name to live, but is dead." — DAR 363.3-363.4.

- **Revelation 3:2-3** — "Be watchful, and strengthen the things which remain, that are ready to die.... If therefore thou shalt not watch, I will come on thee as a thief." A call to the Protestant bodies, from the era after the papal deadly wound (1798) to the great Advent awakening, to revive before probation closes.

  > "If the dates of the preceding churches have been correctly fixed, the period covered by the church of Sardis must commence about the year 1798.… [it constitutes] the reformed churches, from the date above named to the great movement which marked another era in the history of the people of God." — DAR 363.2-363.3.

- **Revelation 3:4-5** — "Thou hast a few names even in Sardis which have not defiled their garments; and they shall walk with me in white... I will not blot out his name out of the book of life." A faithful few remain undefiled; their names stand in the book of life unblotted.
- **Revelation 3:6** — "He that hath an ear, let him hear what the Spirit saith unto the churches." The same closing summons binds every period into one prophetic series.

### 6. Philadelphia — the Advent movement, the open door (1833-1844)

- **Revelation 3:7** — "These things saith he that is holy, he that is true, he that hath the key of David, he that openeth, and no man shutteth; and shutteth, and no man openeth." Christ holds the key — the opening of the most holy place of the heavenly sanctuary in 1844.

  > "A similar opening and shutting, or change of ministration, must be accomplished by Christ when the time comes for the cleansing of the heavenly sanctuary. And the time did come for this service to commence at the close of the 2300 days, in 1844.… the opening being the opening of his ministration in the most holy place, and the shutting, its cessation in the first apartment." — DAR 367.2.

- **Revelation 3:8** — "I have set before thee an open door, and no man can shut it: for thou hast a little strength, and hast kept my word." Philadelphia signifies "brotherly love," the position and spirit of those who received the Advent message up to the autumn of 1844, who laid aside party names and selfishness in one consecrated movement.

  > "The word Philadelphia signifies brotherly love, and expresses the position and spirit of those who received the Advent message up to the autumn of 1844. As they came out of the sectarian churches, they left party names and party feelings behind.… Selfishness and covetousness were laid aside, and a spirit of consecration and sacrifice was cherished." — DAR 366.3.

- **Revelation 3:9-10** — "I will make them of the synagogue of Satan... to come and worship before thy feet.... I also will keep thee from the hour of temptation, which shall come upon all the world." A promise of vindication and of preservation through the trial coming on the whole earth.

  > "Verse 9 probably applies to those who do not keep pace with the advancing light of truth, and who oppose those that do. Such shall yet be made to feel and confess that God loves those, who … continue to advance in the knowledge of his truth." — DAR 368.1.

  > "Those who now live in patient, faithful obedience to the commandments of God and the faith of Jesus, will be kept in the hour of temptation and peril just before us." — DAR 368.2.

- **Revelation 3:11-12** — "Behold, I come quickly: hold that fast which thou hast.... Him that overcometh will I make a pillar in the temple of my God." To the Advent believers, the imminent coming and a fixed, permanent place in God's temple — "he shall go no more out."

### 7. Laodicea — the judgment-hour church (1844-advent)

- **Revelation 3:14** — "These things saith the Amen, the faithful and true witness, the beginning of the creation of God." The last church meets Christ as the faithful and true Witness — fitting, for this is the period of the investigative Judgment.

  > "This is, then, the final message to the churches ere the close of probation. And though the description of their condition which he gives to the indifferent Laodiceans is fearful and startling, nevertheless it cannot be denied; for the Witness is 'faithful and true.'" — DAR 371.2.

- **Revelation 3:15-16** — "Thou art neither cold nor hot.... because thou art lukewarm... I will spue thee out of my mouth." Laodicea signifies "the judging of the people," or "a just people"; it is the last stage of the church, under the third angel's message, while the great day of atonement is transpiring.

  > "Laodicea signifies the judging of the people, or, according to Cruden, a just people.… It is the last stage of the church. It consequently applies to believers under the third message … while the great day of atonement is transpiring, and the investigative Judgment is going forward upon the house of God." — DAR 371.1.

- **Revelation 3:17** — "Thou sayest, I am rich, and increased with goods, and have need of nothing; and knowest not that thou art wretched, and miserable, and poor, and blind, and naked." The self-deceived, self-satisfied condition of the church living in the judgment hour.

  > "Such the Laodiceans think is their condition. They are not hypocrites, because they 'know not' that they are poor, miserable, blind, and naked." — DAR 374.1.

- **Revelation 3:18** — "I counsel thee to buy of me gold tried in the fire... and white raiment... and anoint thine eyes with eyesalve, that thou mayest see." The threefold remedy: tried faith and love, Christ's righteousness, and spiritual discernment.

  > "Buy of me, says the true Witness, gold tried in the fire, that thou mayest be rich, and white raiment, that thou mayest be clothed, and anoint thine eyes with eye-salve, that thou mayest see. This shows at once to the deceived Laodiceans the objects they lack, and the extent of their destitution." — DAR 374.2.

- **Revelation 3:19-20** — "As many as I love, I rebuke and chasten: be zealous therefore, and repent. Behold, I stand at the door, and knock." Even the lukewarm are loved; Christ still pleads at the door of the last church.

  > "The Token of Love. — This, strange as it may seem, is chastisement. 'As many as I love, I rebuke and chasten.' If we are without chastisement, we are not sons." — DAR 378.1.

  > "'Behold, I stand at the door, and knock.' Why does he? Not because he is without home elsewhere.… But he goes round from door to door in Laodicea. He stands at each, and knocks, because he came to seek and to save that which is lost." — DAR 378.3.

- **Revelation 3:21-22** — "To him that overcometh will I grant to sit with me in my throne.... He that hath an ear, let him hear what the Spirit saith unto the churches." The series closes as it ran — the overcomer enthroned with Christ, and the Spirit's call sounding to every age at once.

### The church as a woman — symbol confirmed

- **Revelation 1:20 with Revelation 12; Revelation 17** — A woman is the Bible symbol of a church: "a virtuous woman representing a pure church, a vile woman an apostate church." The lampstand-church of Revelation 1-3 is the same pure woman who, fallen, becomes Babylon.

  > "In Revelation 17 Babylon is represented as a woman—a figure which is used in the Bible as the symbol of a church, a virtuous woman representing a pure church, a vile woman an apostate church." — GC 381.1.

- **2 Corinthians 11:2** — "I have espoused you to one husband, that I may present you as a chaste virgin to Christ." The marriage figure binds Christ to His church through all seven periods; her unfaithfulness is spiritual adultery.

  > "In the Bible the sacred and enduring character of the relation that exists between Christ and His church is represented by the union of marriage.… And Paul employs the same figure in the New Testament when he says: 'I have espoused you to one husband, that I may present you as a chaste virgin to Christ.'" — GC 381.2.

  > "The unfaithfulness of the church to Christ in permitting her confidence and affection to be turned from Him, and allowing the love of worldly things to occupy the soul, is likened to the violation of the marriage vow." — GC 381.3-382.

---

# 9. The Seven Seals — the Providential History of the Church

> _Where the seven churches trace the inward spiritual state of God's people, the seven seals unroll the outward providence of the gospel and the church through the self-same Christian era — from apostolic purity to the great day of wrath. As the Lamb breaks each seal, John beholds in succession a white horse (gospel conquest), a red horse (persecution), a black horse (declension under Constantine), a pale horse (papal death), the souls of the martyrs, the literal signs of the sixth seal (the 1755 earthquake, the 1780 dark day, the 1833 falling stars), and at last silence in heaven when the King appears. The seven, being the number of completeness, embrace the whole history of the church from the opening of the Christian era to the coming of Christ. (DAR 402-434; GC 304-308, 333-334.)_

### The Lamb opens the book — a complete sevenfold history

- **Revelation 6:1** — "And I saw when the Lamb opened one of the seals... one of the four beasts saying, Come and see." The Lamb who alone was worthy to take the book now breaks its seals one by one; John is summoned to behold the scenes of each.

  > "HAVING taken the book, the Lamb proceeds at once to open the seals; and the attention of the apostle is called to the scenes that transpire under each seal." — DAR 402.

- **Revelation 5:9** — Christ was found worthy "to take the book, and to open the seals thereof." The opening of the seals is the unrolling of providential history that none but the slain Lamb could disclose.

  > "The view which to us seems most consistent is that the book contained a record of events which were to transpire; and when the seals were broken, and the record was brought to light, the scenes were presented before John... by a representation of what was described in the book being made to pass before his mind in living characters." — DAR 402.

- **Seven = completeness** — The number seven denotes in Scripture completeness and perfection; the seven seals therefore embrace the whole of a certain class of events, reaching down to the close of probationary time. The seals give the religious history of the church; the trumpets, running contemporaneously, give the political commotions of the nations.

  > "The number seven has already been noticed as denoting in the Scriptures completeness and perfection. The seven seals therefore embrace the whole of a certain class of events, reaching down to the close of probationary time.... The seals denote events of a religious character, and contain the history of the church from the opening of the Christian era to the coming of Christ." — DAR 402.2.

### First seal — the WHITE HORSE: apostolic purity and gospel conquest (A.D. 31–c.100)

- **Revelation 6:2** — "Behold a white horse: and he that sat on him had a bow; and a crown was given unto him: and he went forth conquering, and to conquer." The whiteness denotes the purity of the gospel in the apostolic age; the rider goes forth in triumph carrying the truth to all the world.

  > "And I saw, and behold a white horse; and he that sat on him had a bow; and a crown was given unto him: and he went forth conquering, and to conquer." — DAR 402.

- **Mark 16:15** — "Go ye into all the world, and preach the gospel to every creature." The commission that set the white horse riding forth conquering.

  > "And I saw when the Lamb opened one of the seals... and behold a white horse;... and he went forth conquering, and to conquer." — DAR 402.

- **Colossians 1:23** — The gospel "was preached to every creature which is under heaven" — the conquest of the first seal in the apostolic generation.

  > "John saw the characters which made up the various scenes, not fixed and motionless upon canvas, but living and moving, and engaging actively in the parts assigned them." — DAR 402.

- **Revelation 19:11** — A later "white horse," whose rider is "Faithful and True," confirms that the white horse is the symbol of pure, victorious righteousness.

  > "And behold a white horse; and he that sat on him had a bow; and a crown was given unto him: and he went forth conquering, and to conquer." — DAR 402.

### Second seal — the RED HORSE: persecution and bloodshed (c.100–c.323)

- **Revelation 6:4** — "There went out another horse that was red: and power was given to him that sat thereon to take peace from the earth, and that they should kill one another: and there was given unto him a great sword." The change of color is significant: if white is gospel purity, red is the bloodshed of the great pagan persecutions that followed.

  > "If the whiteness of the first horse denoted the purity of the gospel in the period which that symbol covers, the redness of the second horse would signify that in this period that original purity began to be corrupted.... Errors began to arise. Worldliness came in. The ecclesiastical power sought the alliance of the secular. Troubles and commotions were the result." — DAR 404.

- **Matthew 10:34** — "I came not to send peace, but a sword." The truth, faithfully held, drew out the sword of the persecutor; peace was taken from the earth.

  > "This state of things answers well to the declaration of the prophet that power was given to him that sat on the horse 'to take peace from the earth, and that they should kill one another: and there was given unto him a great sword.'" — DAR 404.

### Third seal — the BLACK HORSE: declension under Constantine (c.323–c.538)

- **Revelation 6:5** — "Lo a black horse; and he that sat on him had a pair of balances in his hand." Blackness marks the moral darkening of the church as paganism was incorporated into Christianity, the period in which the false system that resulted in the papacy rapidly developed.

  > "It is seen by this how paganism was incorporated into Christianity, and how, during this period, the false system which resulted in the establishment of the papacy, rapidly rounded out to its full outlines, and ripened into all its deplorable perfection of strength and stature." — DAR 407.

- **Revelation 6:6** — "A measure of wheat for a penny... and see thou hurt not the oil and the wine." The balances denote the union of religious and civil power in one person, beginning with Constantine; the scarcity-price denotes a church grasping after worldly goods, while the warning to spare "the oil and the wine" guards the graces of the Spirit, faith and love.

  > "The balances denoted that religion and civil power would be united in the person who would administer the executive power in the government.... The measures of wheat and barley for a penny denote that the members of the church would be eagerly engaged after worldly goods.... The Oil and Wine... 'denote the graces of the Spirit, faith and love.'" — DAR 407.

- **Constantine's union of church and state** — "It represents a secular period, or union of church and state. Constantine aided the clergy, and put them under obligations to him... called the Council of Nicaea." The prosperity Constantine procured for the church bred the corruptions that finally terminated in the apostasy.

  > "It represents a secular period, or union of church and state. Constantine aided the clergy, and put them under obligations to him. He legislated for the church, called the Council of Nicaea, and was most prominent in that Council.... Mosheim says of this period, 'There was continual war and trouble.'" — DAR 404.3.

  > "The discerning reader will easily perceive what detriment the church received from the peace and prosperity procured by Constantine, and from the imprudent methods employed to allure the different nations to embrace the gospel." — DAR 406.

### Fourth seal — the PALE HORSE: papal death and persecution (538–1798)

- **Revelation 6:8** — "Behold a pale horse: and his name that sat on him was Death, and Hell followed with him. And power was given unto them over the fourth part of the earth, to kill with sword, and with hunger, and with death." The pale (livid, corpse-like) color is unnatural — fit emblem of the death-dealing dominion of the papacy over its territory.

  > "The colors of the white, red, and black horses... are natural; but a pale color is unnatural.... The rider on this horse is named Death; and Hell (ᾅδης, the grave) follows with him.... It must refer to the time in which the papacy bore its unrebuked, unrestrained, and persecuting rule, commencing about A. D. 538, and extending to the time when the Reformers commenced their work." — DAR 408.

- **"Power was given unto him... namely, the papacy"** — The margin reads "him," the power personified by Death on the pale horse, the papacy; the "fourth part of the earth" is the territory over which Rome held jurisdiction, where it slew with sword, hunger, death, and the beasts of the earth.

  > "'And power was given unto them' — him, says the margin; that is, the power personified by Death on the pale horse; namely, the papacy. By the fourth part of the earth is doubtless meant the territory over which this power had jurisdiction; while the words sword, hunger, death... and beasts of the earth, are figures denoting the means by which it has put to death its martyrs." — DAR 408.3.

### Fifth seal — the SOULS UNDER THE ALTAR: the martyrs cry "How long?"

- **Revelation 6:9** — "I saw under the altar the souls of them that were slain for the word of God, and for the testimony which they held." The martyrs of the foregoing seals, slain for their fidelity, are figuratively seen beneath the altar — their blood, like Abel's, crying from the ground; this is no proof of conscious souls in death but symbolic, as Abel's blood "crieth."

  > "This representation is popularly regarded as a strong proof of the doctrine of the disembodied and conscious state of the dead.... This view of the passage is inadmissible, for several reasons: (1) The popular view places these souls in heaven; but the altar of sacrifice on which they were slain, and beneath which they were seen, cannot be there." — DAR 410.

  > "The word souls may be regarded as here meaning simply the martyrs, those who had been slain.... Lying there, victims of papal bloodthirstiness and oppression, they cried to God for vengeance, in the same manner that Abel's blood cried to him from the ground. Genesis 4:10." — DAR 412.

- **Revelation 6:10** — "How long, O Lord, holy and true, dost thou not judge and avenge our blood on them that dwell on the earth?" The cry for vindication against the persecuting power.

  > "These souls pray that their blood may be avenged, — an article which the immaterial soul, as popularly understood, is not supposed to possess." — DAR 412.

- **Revelation 6:11** — "White robes were given unto every one of them; and it was said unto them, that they should rest yet for a little season." The white robes are the partial answer — the vindication of their misrepresented characters — until the full number of martyrs should be completed.

  > "The White Robes. — These were given as a partial answer to their cry, 'How long, O Lord, dost thou not judge and avenge our blood?'... They had gone down to the grave in the most ignominious manner. Their lives had been misrepresented, their reputations tarnished, their names defamed." — DAR 412.2.

### Sixth seal — the LITERAL SIGNS in earth and heaven

- **Revelation 6:12** — "And I beheld when he had opened the sixth seal, and, lo, there was a great earthquake; and the sun became black as sackcloth of hair, and the moon became as blood." Here the language turns from the highly figurative to the strictly literal: a great earthquake, the darkened sun, the bloodied moon.

  > "Between the fifth and sixth seals there seems to be a sudden and entire change in the language, from the highly figurative to the strictly literal.... By no principle of interpretation can the language of the preceding seals be made to be literal, nor can the language of this any more easily be made to be figurative." — DAR 414.2-3.

- **Revelation 6:13** — "And the stars of heaven fell unto the earth, even as a fig tree casteth her untimely figs, when she is shaken of a mighty wind." The falling stars of the sixth seal — the meteoric shower.

  > "And the Stars of Heaven Fell. — The voice of history still is, Fulfilled!... We refer to the great meteoric shower of Nov. 13, 1833." — DAR 422.7.

- **THE GREAT EARTHQUAKE — Lisbon, Nov. 1, 1755** — The first sign, the great earthquake, was fulfilled in the Lisbon earthquake of November 1, 1755, which "extended over a tract of at least 4,000,000 square miles," pervading the greater part of Europe, Africa, and America — no recorded earthquake combining so great an extent with such violence.

  > "The Great Earthquake. — The first event under this seal... is a great earthquake. As the more probable fulfillment of this prediction, we refer to the great earthquake of Nov. 1, 1755, known as the earthquake of Lisbon.... 'The great earthquake of Nov. 1, 1755, extended over a tract of at least 4,000,000 square miles.... It pervaded the greater portion of Europe, Africa, and America.'" — DAR 414.3-418.

  > "In fulfillment of this prophecy there occurred, in the year 1755, the most terrible earthquake that has ever been recorded. Though commonly known as the earthquake of Lisbon, it extended to the greater part of Europe, Africa, and America.... It pervaded an extent of not less than four million square miles." — GC 304-305.

- **THE DARKENED SUN — May 19, 1780** — "The sun became black as sackcloth of hair" was fulfilled in the Dark Day of May 19, 1780, "a most unaccountable darkening of the whole visible heavens and atmosphere in New England," not caused by any eclipse, and standing alone as the most mysterious phenomenon of its kind.

  > "The Darkening of the Sun. — Following the earthquake, it is announced that 'the sun became black as sackcloth of hair.'... This portion of the prediction has also been fulfilled.... the wonderful darkening of the sun, May 19, 1780.... 'Almost, if not altogether alone, as the most mysterious and yet unexplained phenomenon of its kind in nature's diversified range of events... stands the dark day of May 19th, 1780, — a most unaccountable darkening of the whole visible heavens and atmosphere in New England.'" — DAR 418.3-420.

  > "Twenty-five years later appeared the next sign mentioned in the prophecy—the darkening of the sun and moon.... 'Almost, if not altogether alone, as the most mysterious and as yet unexplained phenomenon of its kind, ... stands the dark day of May 19, 1780,—a most unaccountable darkening of the whole visible heavens and atmosphere in New England.'" — GC 306-308.

- **THE MOON AS BLOOD — night of May 19, 1780** — That same night the darkness was "as gross as has ever been observed," and whenever the full moon (which had fulled the day before, proving no eclipse) did appear, it had the appearance of blood — exactly as the prophecy foretold.

  > "'The darkness of the following evening was probably as gross as has ever been observed since the Almighty fiat gave birth to light.'... 'The darkness was as uncommon in the night as it was in the day, as the moon had fulled the day before.' This statement respecting the phase of the moon proves the impossibility of an eclipse.... And whenever on this memorable night the moon did appear... it had, according to this prophecy, the appearance of blood." — DAR 422.1-6.

- **THE FALLING STARS — Nov. 13, 1833** — "And the stars of heaven fell" was fulfilled in the great meteoric shower of November 13, 1833 — "the most universal and wonderful which has ever been recorded, the whole firmament, over all the United States, being then, for hours, in fiery commotion." On each occasion the people instinctively cried, "The Judgment has come."

  > "'Extensive and magnificent showers of shooting stars have been known to occur at various places in modern times; but the most universal and wonderful which has ever been recorded, is that of the 13th of November, 1833, the whole firmament, over all the United States, being then, for hours, in fiery commotion.'... They were each instinctively associated... with the great day of which they were the forerunners. And on each occasion the cry was raised, 'The Judgment has come; the world's at an end.'" — DAR 422.7-426.

  > "This prophecy received a striking and impressive fulfillment in the great meteoric shower of November 13, 1833. That was the most extensive and wonderful display of falling stars which has ever been recorded; 'the whole firmament, over all the United States, being then, for hours, in fiery commotion!'" — GC 333-334.

- **Matthew 24:29** — "Immediately after the tribulation of those days shall the sun be darkened, and the moon shall not give her light, and the stars shall fall from heaven, and the powers of the heavens shall be shaken." Christ's own sign-list matches the sixth seal exactly and fixes its time — "after the tribulation," i.e., after the 1260-year papal persecution had begun to abate.

  > "Our Lord has not only told us that such an event should occur as a sign of his coming, but he has told us also when it should occur. 'Immediately after the tribulation of those days,' says Matthew.... The days are the days of papal supremacy, the 1260 years, from 538 to 1798; the tribulation is the oppression of Christians by the Catholic power till restrained by the work of the Reformation. The tribulation may be said to have ceased about the middle of the eighteenth century." — DAR 428.3-430.

  > "Prophecy not only foretells the manner and object of Christ's coming, but presents tokens by which men are to know when it is near.... 'The sun shall be darkened, and the moon shall not give her light, and the stars of heaven shall fall, and the powers that are in heaven shall be shaken. And then shall they see the Son of man coming in the clouds with great power and glory.' Mark 13:24-26." — GC 304.

  > "This prophecy received a striking and impressive fulfillment in the great meteoric shower of November 13, 1833. That was the most extensive and wonderful display of falling stars which has ever been recorded." — GC 333.

- **Joel 2:31** — "The sun shall be turned into darkness, and the moon into blood, before the great and the terrible day of the LORD come." The Old-Testament prophet foretold the very signs of 1780; the eyewitness descriptions of the Dark Day are "but an echo of the words of the Lord, recorded by the prophet Joel."

  > "The description of this event, as given by eyewitnesses, is but an echo of the words of the Lord, recorded by the prophet Joel, twenty-five hundred years previous to their fulfillment: 'The sun shall be turned into darkness, and the moon into blood, before the great and terrible day of the Lord come.' Joel 2:31." — GC 308.1.

- **Luke 21:25-26** — "There shall be signs in the sun, and in the moon, and in the stars... the powers of heaven shall be shaken." The parallel discourse confirms these as the appointed tokens that the King is near, even at the doors.

  > "Prophecy not only foretells the manner and object of Christ's coming, but presents tokens by which men are to know when it is near. Said Jesus: 'There shall be signs in the sun, and in the moon, and in the stars.' Luke 21:25." — GC 304.

### The sixth seal reaches to the great day of wrath

- **Revelation 6:14** — "And the heaven departed as a scroll when it is rolled together; and every mountain and island were moved out of their places." Having beheld the past signs fulfilled, we now stand between verses 13 and 14, looking to the still-future rolling-up of the heavens at the voice of God.

  > "And the Heaven Departed as a Scroll. — In this event our minds are turned to the future. From looking at the past, and beholding the word of God fulfilled, we are now called to look at events in the future.... Here is our position, unmistakably defined. We stand between the 13th and 14th verses of this chapter. We wait for the heavens to depart as a scroll when it is rolled together." — DAR 432.2-3.

- **Isaiah 34:4** — "The heavens shall be rolled together as a scroll: and all their host shall fall down, as the leaf falleth off from the vine." The prophets describe the same closing scene of the sixth seal — the departing heaven.

  > "It is the voice of God, as he speaks in terrible majesty from his throne in heaven, that causes this fearful commotion in earth and sky.... He is to speak again, and not only the earth will shake, but the heavens also.... Isaiah (24: 19, 20) says: 'The earth is utterly broken down, the earth is clean dissolved, the earth is moved exceedingly.'" — DAR 432.3-4.

- **Revelation 6:15-16** — Kings, great men, and bondmen alike hide "in the dens and in the rocks of the mountains," crying, "Fall on us, and hide us from the face of him that sitteth on the throne, and from the wrath of the Lamb." The largest and last prayer-meeting ever held — addressed not to God but to the rocks, in despair.

  > "Many now affect to despise the institution of prayer; but at one time or another all men will pray. Those who will not now pray to God in penitence, will then pray to the rocks and mountains in despair; and this will be the largest prayer-meeting ever held." — DAR 434.1-2.

- **Revelation 6:17** — "For the great day of his wrath is come; and who shall be able to stand?" The sixth seal closes by ushering in the great day of wrath, just short of Christ's personal appearing, which is reserved for the next seal.

  > "The involuntary language of their anguished hearts is, 'The great day of his wrath is come, and who shall be able to stand?'" — DAR 434.

  > "That seal does not bring us to the second advent, although it embraces events that transpire in close connection therewith.... But the seal stops just short of that event. The personal appearing of Christ must therefore be allotted to the next seal." — DAR 452.4.

### Revelation 7 — the sealing falls within the sixth seal (parenthesis)

- **Revelation 7:1** — "I saw four angels standing on the four corners of the earth, holding the four winds of the earth, that the wind should not blow." The four winds — strife, war, and commotion — are restrained while the work of God goes forward; chapter 7 is thrown in parenthetically between the sixth and seventh seals.

  > "The sixth chapter closed with the events of the sixth seal, and the seventh seal is not mentioned until we reach the opening of chapter 8. The whole of chapter 7 is therefore thrown in here parenthetically. Why is it thus thrown in at this point? — Evidently for the purpose of stating additional particulars concerning the sixth seal." — DAR 435.2.

  > "The seventh chapter stands parenthetically between the sixth and seventh seals, from which it appears that the sealing work of that chapter belongs to the sixth seal." — DAR 452.3.

- **Revelation 7:2** — "I saw another angel ascending from the east, having the seal of the living God." The sealing message arises in the closing scenes of the sixth seal — the very era in which we now live.

  > "It comes in, therefore, between the 13th and 14th verses of Revelation 6; but there, as already shown, is just where we now stand. Hence the first part of Revelation 7 relates to a work the accomplishment of which may be looked for at the present time." — DAR 435.

- **Revelation 7:3** — "Hurt not the earth... till we have sealed the servants of our God in their foreheads." The winds of destruction are held until God's people are sealed; this sealing work belongs to the sixth seal, just before the great day of wrath.

  > "The seventh chapter stands parenthetically between the sixth and seventh seals, from which it appears that the sealing work of that chapter belongs to the sixth seal." — DAR 452.3.

- **Revelation 14:1** — The 144,000 stand "on the mount Sion... having his Father's name written in their foreheads" — the sealed company of Revelation 7, the fruit of the sealing held back by the four angels.

  > "It must transpire before the departing of the heavens as a scroll; for after that event there is no place for such a work as this. And it must take place subsequently to the signs in the sun, moon, and stars; for these signs have been fulfilled, and such a work has not yet been accomplished." — DAR 435.

### Seventh seal — SILENCE IN HEAVEN: the King appears

- **Revelation 8:1** — "And when he had opened the seventh seal, there was silence in heaven about the space of half an hour." The series is here resumed and concluded: the sixth seal stopped just short of the second advent, so the personal appearing of Christ is allotted to the seventh. When the Lord descends with all the holy angels — when the heavenly harpers leave the courts above to come down with their Lord — there is silence in heaven.

  > "The series of seven seals is here resumed and concluded.... But the seal stops just short of that event. The personal appearing of Christ must therefore be allotted to the next seal.... And when all the heavenly harpers leave the courts above to come down with their divine Lord, as he descends to gather the fruit of his redeeming work, will there not be silence in heaven?" — DAR 452.2-4.

- **Matthew 25:31** — "When the Son of man shall come in his glory, and all the holy angels with him." The emptying of heaven's courts at the advent is the cause of the half-hour silence of the seventh seal.

  > "But when the Lord appears, he comes with all the holy angels with him. Matthew 25:31. And when all the heavenly harpers leave the courts above to come down with their divine Lord... will there not be silence in heaven?" — DAR 452.4.

### The seals view the era as PROVIDENCE, not spiritual state

- **Zechariah 6:1-8** — The four chariots with red, black, white, and "grisled and bay" horses — "the four spirits of the heavens, which go forth from standing before the Lord of all the earth" — show that horses of differing color are a Scriptural symbol of successive providential agencies sent forth over the earth.

  > "Perhaps the first noticeable feature in these symbols is the contrast in the color of the horses. This is doubtless designed to be significant." — DAR 404.

- **Seals vs. churches** — The seven churches (Rev. 2–3) describe the inward spiritual condition of God's people; the seven seals describe the outward providence — the church's fortunes and the world's history — over the very same Christian centuries. The trumpets, again, run contemporaneously but trace political war among the nations.

  > "The trumpets denote a series of events which transpire contemporaneously with the events of the seals, but of an entirely different character. A trumpet is a symbol of war; hence the trumpets denote great political commotions to take place among the nations during the gospel age. The seals denote events of a religious character, and contain the history of the church from the opening of the Christian era to the coming of Christ." — DAR 402.2.

---

# 10. The Seven Trumpets — the Political Judgments on the Nations

> _Where the seven churches trace the inner life of the gospel church and the seven seals her outward fortunes, the seven trumpets sound the political and military judgments God brought upon the nations that had to do with His church. The first four overthrew Western Rome by the barbarian floods (Goths under Alaric, Vandals under Genseric, Huns under Attila, and the final extinction of the Western emperor); the fifth woe loosed the Saracens of Mohammed, the sixth the Ottoman Turks. The sixth trumpet's "hour, day, month, and year" measured 391 years and 15 days, running from July 27, 1449 to August 11, 1840 — the very day Litch had publicly fixed two years beforehand, openly vindicating the year-day rule and giving "a mighty impetus to the great Advent movement." (DAR 452-487; GC 334-335; PREX2 183-188.)_

### The trumpets as a distinct series — political judgments, not the seals

- **Revelation 8:1** — "When he had opened the seventh seal, there was silence in heaven about the space of half an hour." The seventh seal opens here; the trumpets are therefore a new and separate series, beginning where the seals close. The silence is the heavens emptied as Christ and all the angels descend.

  > "But when the Lord appears, he comes with all the holy angels with him. Matthew 25:31. And when all the heavenly harpers leave the courts above to come down with their divine Lord, as he descends to gather the fruit of his redeeming work, will there not be silence in heaven?" — DAR 452.

- **Revelation 8:2** — "And I saw the seven angels which stood before God; and to them were given seven trumpets." Seven angels, seven blasts — symbols of successive wars and overthrows by which God judged the nations touching His people, the political counterpart to the seals.

  > "This verse introduces a new and distinct series of events. In the seals we have had the history of the church during what is called the gospel dispensation. In the seven trumpets, now introduced, we have the principal political and warlike events which were to transpire during the same time." — DAR 453.

- **Revelation 8:3-5** — An angel with a golden censer offers "much incense, with the prayers of all saints" at the golden altar; then he fills the censer with "fire of the altar," casts it into the earth, and there follow "voices, and thunderings, and lightnings, and an earthquake." Intercession at the altar precedes the sounding — the judgments fall in answer to the saints' cause.

  > "The seven angels with their warlike trumpets had been introduced; terrible scenes were to transpire under their sounding; but before they commence, the people of God are pointed to the work of mediation in their behalf above, and their source of help and strength during this time…. they were to know that their great High Priest still ministered for them in the sanctuary in heaven, and that thither they could direct their prayers, and have them offered, with incense, to their Father in heaven." — DAR 454.

- **Revelation 8:6** — "And the seven angels which had the seven trumpets prepared themselves to sound." The signal that the historical series is about to open upon the Roman world.

  > "The subject of the seven trumpets is here resumed…. The seven angels prepare themselves to sound. Their sounding comes in as a complement to the prophecy of Daniel 2 and 7, commencing with the breaking up of the old Roman empire into its ten divisions, of which, in the first four trumpets, we have a description." — DAR 454.

### The first four trumpets — the barbarian floods that broke Western Rome

> _Uriah Smith follows Keith and Gibbon: "Little or nothing is left for the professed interpreter to do but to point to the pages of Gibbon." The first four trumpets are the four successive invasions that subverted the Western Empire, A.D. 395-493._

| Trumpet | Verse   | Symbol                            | Historical fulfilment                                | Leader / date                |
| ------- | ------- | --------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| First   | 8:7     | Hail, fire, blood on the earth    | Gothic invasions of the empire                       | Alaric, c. 395 onward        |
| Second  | 8:8-9   | Burning mountain into the sea     | Naval war on the maritime coasts; Africa & Italy     | Genseric the Vandal, 428-468 |
| Third   | 8:10-11 | Blazing star "Wormwood" on rivers | Desolating wars on the Alpine river-sources of Italy | Attila the Hun               |
| Fourth  | 8:12    | Sun, moon, stars darkened         | Extinction of the Western imperial line              | Odoacer / Theodoric, 476-493 |

- **Revelation 8:7** — "Hail and fire mingled with blood... cast upon the earth: and the third part of trees was burnt up." "Hail" = the northern origin of the invaders; "fire" = the burning of city and country; "blood" = the slaughter — the Gothic war under Alaric, "the first sore and heavy judgment which fell on Western Rome," from the close of the fourth century.

  > "The terrible effects of this Gothic invasion are represented as 'hail,' from the fact of the northern origin of the invaders; 'fire,' from the destruction by flame of both city and country; and 'blood,' from the terrible slaughter of the citizens of the empire by the bold and intrepid warriors." — DAR 455.

- **Revelation 8:8-9** — "As it were a great mountain burning with fire was cast into the sea: and the third part of the sea became blood... the third part of the ships were destroyed." A maritime warfare: Genseric the Vandal (A.D. 428-468), whose conquests "were, for the most part, naval," preyed on Roman commerce from Carthage and destroyed the fleets sent against him.

  > "His conquests were, for the most part, naval; and his triumphs were 'as it were a great mountain burning with fire, cast into the sea.' What figure would better, or even so well, illustrate the collision of navies, and the general havoc of war on the maritime coasts?… From the port of Carthage he repeatedly made piratical sallies, and preyed on the Roman commerce, and waged war with that empire." — DAR 458.

  > "A last and desperate attempt to dispossess Genseric of the sovereignty of the seas, was made in the year 468 by Leo, the emperor of the East…. After the failure of this great expedition, Genseric again became the tyrant of the sea." — DAR 459.

- **Revelation 8:10-11** — "There fell a great star from heaven, burning as it were a lamp... upon the third part of the rivers, and upon the fountains of waters... the name of the star is called Wormwood." A brilliant meteoric chieftain whose ravages fell on the river-sources of the Alps and Italy — Attila and his Huns, "the scourge of God."

  > "In the manner of his appearance, he strongly resembled a brilliant meteor flashing in the sky. He came from the East gathering his Huns, and poured them down… with the rapidity of a flashing meteor, suddenly on the empire…. As a matter of fact, the principal operations of Attila were on the regions of the Alps, and on the portions of the empire whence the rivers flow down into Italy." — DAR 461.

  > "These words… recall us for a moment to the character of Attila, to the misery of which he was the author or the instrument, and to the terror that was inspired by his name…. He styled himself, 'The Scourge of God.'" — DAR 462.

- **Revelation 8:12** — "The third part of the sun was smitten, and the third part of the moon, and the third part of the stars; so as the third part of them was darkened." The "sun" of Roman imperial power was extinguished when the Western line of emperors ended (476-493); the "moon and stars" — consulship and senate — lingered, then expired "star by star," till a Gothic king reigned over Rome.

  > "The imperial Roman power, of which either Rome or Constantinople had been jointly or singly the seat, whether in the West or the East, was no longer recognized in Italy, and the third part of the sun was smitten, till it emitted no longer the faintest rays. The power of the Caesars was unknown in Italy; and a Gothic king reigned over Rome…. yet the moon and the stars still shone, or glimmered, for a little longer in the Western empire." — DAR 464.

  > "'She saw her glories star by star expire,' till not even a single star remained, to glimmer on the vacant and dark night." — DAR 467.

- **Revelation 8:13** — "Woe, woe, woe, to the inhabiters of the earth by reason of the other voices of the trumpet of the three angels, which are yet to sound!" The remaining three trumpets are so much heavier than the barbarian floods that they are singled out as woes — the first incursions were "but as the preliminary drops of a shower before the torrent."

  > "Fearful as were the calamities brought upon the empire by the first incursions of these barbarians, they were comparatively light as contrasted with the calamities which were to follow. They were but as the preliminary drops of a shower before the torrent which was soon to fall upon the Roman world. The three remaining trumpets are overshadowed with a cloud of woe." — DAR 467.

### The fifth trumpet — first woe: the Saracens of Mohammed

> _"There is scarcely so uniform an agreement among interpreters... as respecting the application of the fifth and sixth trumpets, or the first and second woes, to the Saracens and Turks." (DAR 469, quoting Keith.)_

- **Revelation 9:1-2** — "A star fall from heaven unto the earth: and to him was given the key of the bottomless pit. And he opened the bottomless pit; and there arose a smoke... and the sun and the air were darkened." The fallen star = the overthrow and death of Chosroes, king of Persia (628), which opened the way for Mohammed; the "smoke" darkening sun and air = the false religion of Islam spreading its darkness over the Eastern empire. The "bottomless pit" (Gr. _abussos_) fits the trackless Arabian desert whence the Saracens swarmed.

  > "Chosroes, after his entire discomfiture and loss of empire, was murdered in the year 628; and the year 629 is marked by 'the conquest of Arabia,' and 'the first war of the Mohammedans against the Roman empire.'… The bottomless pit needed but a key to open it, and that key was the fall of Chosroes…. In this instance it may appropriately refer to the unknown wastes of the Arabian desert, from the borders of which issued the hordes of Saracens, like swarms of locusts." — DAR 472.

- **Revelation 9:3-4** — "There came out of the smoke locusts upon the earth... and it was commanded them that they should not hurt the grass... but only those men which have not the seal of God in their foreheads." The Saracen hosts, like locusts from the desert, were charged (under Abubeker) to spare the verdure — and were directed against an apostate church that "had not the seal of God," having torn away the Sabbath.

  > "The Roman empire declined, as it arose, by conquest; but the Saracens and the Turks were the instruments by which a false religion became the scourge of an apostate church; and hence, instead of the fifth and sixth trumpets, like the former, being designated by that name alone, they are called woes." — DAR 469.

- **Revelation 9:5-6** — They were "not to kill them, but that they should be tormented five months." Their commission was to harass, not destroy — torment, not conquest of the Greek empire — for a literal prophetic "five months."

  > "'And their power was to hurt men five months.' Thus far their commission extended, to torment by constant depredations, but not politically to kill them. 'Five months,' thirty days to a month, give us one hundred and fifty days; and these days, being symbolic, signify one hundred and fifty years. Commencing July 27, 1299, the one hundred and fifty years reach to 1449." — DAR 479.

- **Revelation 9:7-10** — "The shapes of the locusts were like unto horses prepared unto battle; and on their heads were as it were crowns like gold... hair as the hair of women... teeth as the teeth of lions... breastplates of iron... tails like unto scorpions." Smith applies each detail to the Arabs: the turbans ("crowns like gold"), the long uncut hair, the lion-like ferocity, the cuirasses ("breastplates of iron"), and the swift cavalry whose charge was "as the sound of chariots of many horses running to battle."

  > "The turbans of the Saracens, like unto a coronet, were their ornament and their boast…. The Arabs, unlike other men, had their hair as the hair of women, or uncut…. for, as denoting their ferocity and strength to devour, their teeth were as the teeth of lions…. 'The cuirass (or breastplate) was in use among the Arabs in the days of Mohammed.'… 'With a touch of the hand, the Arab horses darted away with the swiftness of the wind.'" — DAR 477.

- **Revelation 9:11** — "They had a king over them... whose name in the Hebrew tongue is Abaddon, but in the Greek tongue hath his name Apollyon" — that is, _Destroyer_, fittingly the spirit of the destroying Mohammedan power.

  > "In Hebrew, 'Abaddon,' the destroyer; in Greek, 'Apollyon,' one that exterminates, or destroys. Having two different names in two languages, it is evident that the character, rather than the name of the power, is intended to be represented. If so, as expressed in both languages, he is a destroyer. Such has always been the character of the Ottoman government." — DAR 479.

- **Revelation 9:12** — "One woe is past; and, behold, there come two woes more hereafter." The Saracen woe closes when the five months end and the work passes to the Turk.

  > "The first woe was to continue from the rise of Mohammedanism until the end of the five months. Then the first woe was to end, and the second to begin. And when the sixth angel sounded, it was commanded to take off the restraints which had been imposed on the nation, by which they were restricted to the work of tormenting men, and their commission was enlarged so as to permit them to slay the third part of men." — DAR 480.

### The sixth trumpet — second woe: the Ottoman Turks (391 years 15 days → Aug 11, 1840)

> _Here is the great public test of the historicist method: a date computed from "an hour, and a day, and a month, and a year" and fixed two years in advance — fulfilled to the very letter._

- **Revelation 9:13-14** — "The sixth angel sounded... Loose the four angels which are bound in the great river Euphrates." The "four angels" = the four chief Ottoman sultanies of Aleppo, Iconium, Damascus, and Bagdad, in the country of the Euphrates; previously restrained, now loosed.

  > "The Four Angels. — These were the four principal sultanies of which the Ottoman empire was composed, located in the country watered by the great river Euphrates. These sultanies were situated at Aleppo, Iconium, Damascus, and Bagdad. Previously they had been restrained; but God commanded, and they were loosed." — DAR 480.

- **Revelation 9:15** — "The four angels were loosed, which were prepared for an hour, and a day, and a month, and a year, for to slay the third part of men." By the year-day rule: a prophetic year = 360 years, a month = 30 years, a day = 1 year, and an hour = 15 days — totaling **391 years and 15 days**, now to slay (overthrow the Greek empire) rather than merely torment.

  > "And when the sixth angel sounded, it was commanded to take off the restraints which had been imposed on the nation… and their commission was enlarged so as to permit them to slay the third part of men." — DAR 480.

  > "The four angels were loosed for an hour, a day, a month, and a year, to slay the third part of men. This period amounts to three hundred and ninety-one years and fifteen days; during which Ottoman supremacy was to exist in Constantinople." — PREX2 183.1.

  > "The specified time for the continuance of Turkish or Mahometan supremacy over the Greeks, was an hour, day, month, and year. A prophetic year, three hundred and sixty days; a month, thirty days; one day; and an hour, or the twenty-fourth part of a day…. Three hundred and ninety-one years and fifteen days." — PREX2 188.2.

- **Day-for-a-year — the rule that fixes the date.** **Numbers 14:34** — "each day for a year, shall ye bear your iniquities." **Ezekiel 4:6** — "I have appointed thee each day for a year." The same divine measure that turns 391 prophetic days into 391 historical years.

  > "And when the sixth angel sounded, it was commanded to take off the restraints which had been imposed on the nation… and their commission was enlarged so as to permit them to slay the third part of men. This command came from the four horns of the golden altar." — DAR 480.

  > "A prophetic year, three hundred and sixty days; a month, thirty days; one day; and an hour, or the twenty-fourth part of a day. Three hundred and sixty, the number of days in a prophetic year, divided by twenty-four, the number of hours in a day, gives us fifteen days. Three hundred and ninety-one years and fifteen days." — PREX2 188.

- **Revelation 9:16-19** — "The number of the army of the horsemen were two hundred thousand thousand... breastplates of fire, and of jacinth, and brimstone... and out of their mouths issued fire and smoke and brimstone." The vast Turkish cavalry, and "fire, smoke, and brimstone" = the gunpowder artillery by which Mahomet II battered down the walls of Constantinople in 1453 — Gibbon, "the unconscious commentator," attributing the city's fall to the Ottoman cannon.

  > "It was to 'the fire and the smoke and the sulphur,' to the artillery and firearms of Mahomet, that the killing of the third part of men, i. e., the capture of Constantinople, and by consequence the destruction of the Greek empire, was owing…. It well deserves remark, how Gibbon, always the unconscious commentator on the Apocalyptic prophecy, puts this new instrumentality of war into the foreground of his picture." — DAR 483.

- **The commencement and the close.** The Ottoman supremacy over the Greeks began when Constantine XIII (Deacozes/Palaeologus) accepted the throne only "by permission of Amurath, the Turkish sultan" — virtually saying, "I cannot reign unless you permit." The 150 years ending July 27, 1449, the 391 years and 15 days then began, and "the period would end Aug. 11, 1840."

  > "But he would not venture to ascend the throne without the consent of Amurath, the Turkish sultan. He therefore sent ambassadors to ask his consent, and obtained it before he presumed to call himself sovereign…. The authority and supremacy of the Turkish power was acknowledged when Constantine virtually said, 'I cannot reign unless you permit.'" — DAR 480.

  > "This supremacy of the Mohammedans over the Greeks was to continue, as already noticed, three hundred and ninety-one years and fifteen days. Commencing when the one hundred and fifty years ended, July 27, 1449, the period would end Aug. 11, 1840." — DAR 485.

- **Revelation 9:18** — "By these three was the third part of men killed" — the destruction of the Greek (Eastern) empire, capital fallen, "her religion trampled in the dust by the Moslem conquerors."

  > "how the Turks at length 'rushing through the breaches,' 'Constantinople was subdued, her empire subverted, and her religion trampled in the dust by the Moslem conquerors.'… For what is it but a comment on the words of our prophecy? 'By these three was the third part of men killed, by the fire, and by the smoke, and by the sulphur, which issued out of their mouths.'" — DAR 483.

- **The fulfilment — Aug 11, 1840.** Just as the supremacy began by a voluntary surrender, so it ended by one: embroiled with Mehemet Ali of Egypt, the sultan accepted the intervention of the four powers (England, Russia, Austria, Prussia), and on **August 11, 1840** the ultimatum was placed by Rifat Bey into the pasha's hands — the sultan's independence "GONE," the empire thenceforth existing "only by the sufferance of these Christian powers." "Thus was the prophecy fulfilled to the very letter."

  > "Judging from the manner of the commencement of the Ottoman supremacy… we should naturally conclude that the fall or departure of the Ottoman independence would be brought about in the same way; that at the end of the specified period, that is, on the 11th of August, 1840, the sultan would voluntarily surrender his independence into the hands of the Christian powers." — DAR 485.

  > "From the first publication of the calculation of this matter in 1838… the time set for the fulfillment of the prophecy — Aug. 11, 1840 — was watched by thousands with intense interest. And the exact accomplishment of the event predicted… gave a mighty impetus to the great Advent movement then beginning to attract the attention of the world." — DAR 487.

- **Litch's prediction, made in 1838.** Two years beforehand, Josiah Litch published this calculation; "only a few days previous to its accomplishment he wrote" that the 391 years 15 days "will end on the 11th of August, 1840, when the Ottoman power in Constantinople may be expected to be broken." At the very time specified, Turkey placed herself under the control of the Christian nations: "The event exactly fulfilled the prediction."

  > "Two years before, Josiah Litch, one of the leading ministers preaching the second advent, published an exposition of Revelation 9, predicting the fall of the Ottoman Empire…. and only a few days previous to its accomplishment he wrote: '…it will end on the 11th of August, 1840, when the Ottoman power in Constantinople may be expected to be broken. And this, I believe, will be found to be the case.'" — GC 334-335.

  > "At the very time specified, Turkey, through her ambassadors, accepted the protection of the allied powers of Europe, and thus placed herself under the control of Christian nations. The event exactly fulfilled the prediction. (See Appendix.) When it became known, multitudes were convinced of the correctness of the principles of prophetic interpretation adopted by Miller and his associates, and a wonderful impetus was given to the advent movement." — GC 335.

  > "But, although the four angels were thus loosed by the voluntary submission of the Greeks, yet another doom awaited the seat of empire. Amurath, the sultan to whom the submission of Deacozes was made… soon after died, and was succeeded in the empire, in 1451, by Mahomet II., who set his heart on Constantinople, and determined to make it a prey." — PREX2 183.

- **Revelation 9:20-21** — "The rest of the men which were not killed by these plagues yet repented not... that they should not worship devils, and idols of gold, and silver... Neither repented they of their murders, nor of their sorceries, nor of their fornication, nor of their thefts." The Saracen and Turkish scourges were sent upon apostate Christendom, yet the surviving Roman world learned no lesson — saint-worship, image-worship, and pretended miracles ("sorceries") continued.

  > "The worship of devils (demons, dead men deified) and of idols of gold, silver, brass, stone, and wood, may find a fulfillment in the saint worship and image worship of the Roman Catholic Church; while of murders, sorceries (pretended miracles through the agency of departed saints), fornications, and thefts, in countries where the Roman religion has prevailed, there has been no lack." — DAR 487.
