### The seventh trumpet — third woe: the kingdom delivered to Christ

- **Revelation 11:14** — "The second woe is past; and, behold, the third woe cometh quickly." With Aug. 11, 1840, the second woe closes; the third woe (the seventh trumpet) follows quickly — the events of the last days.

  > "And the exact accomplishment of the event predicted, showing, as it did, the right application of the prophecy, gave a mighty impetus to the great Advent movement then beginning to attract the attention of the world." — DAR 487.

  > "At the very time specified, Turkey, through her ambassadors, accepted the protection of the allied powers of Europe… The event exactly fulfilled the prediction. (See Appendix.)… a wonderful impetus was given to the advent movement." — GC 335.

- **Revelation 11:15** — "The seventh angel sounded; and there were great voices in heaven, saying, The kingdoms of this world are become the kingdoms of our Lord, and of his Christ; and he shall reign for ever and ever." The final blast carries us past all earthly judgments to the kingdom given to Christ — the goal toward which the whole series of trumpets has pointed.

  > "The hordes of Saracens and Turks were let loose as a scourge and punishment upon apostate Christendom. Men suffered the punishment, but learned therefrom no lesson." — DAR 487.

### Why this trumpet mattered to the Advent movement

- **The year-day rule, publicly vindicated.** That "the time set for the fulfillment... — Aug. 11, 1840 — was watched by thousands with intense interest," and that "the exact accomplishment of the event predicted... gave a mighty impetus to the great Advent movement," proved the soundness of the prophetic method by which the same students reckoned the 2300 days to 1844.

  > "From the first publication of the calculation of this matter in 1838… the time set for the fulfillment of the prophecy — Aug. 11, 1840 — was watched by thousands with intense interest. And the exact accomplishment of the event predicted, showing, as it did, the right application of the prophecy, gave a mighty impetus to the great Advent movement." — DAR 487.

  > "When it became known, multitudes were convinced of the correctness of the principles of prophetic interpretation adopted by Miller and his associates, and a wonderful impetus was given to the advent movement. Men of learning and position united with Miller, both in preaching and in publishing his views, and from 1840 to 1844 the work rapidly extended." — GC 335.

- **One method, two dates.** The day-for-a-year principle (Num. 14:34; Eze. 4:6) that closed the sixth trumpet on Aug 11, 1840 is the same principle by which Daniel's 2300 evening-mornings reach to 1844 — so the Ottoman fulfilment, openly verified by history, stands as God's seal upon the chronology of the cleansing of the sanctuary.

  > "At the very time specified, Turkey, through her ambassadors, accepted the protection of the allied powers of Europe, and thus placed herself under the control of Christian nations. The event exactly fulfilled the prediction…. multitudes were convinced of the correctness of the principles of prophetic interpretation adopted by Miller and his associates." — GC 335.

  > "This supremacy of the Mohammedans over the Greeks was to continue, as already noticed, three hundred and ninety-one years and fifteen days. Commencing when the one hundred and fifty years ended, July 27, 1449, the period would end Aug. 11, 1840." — DAR 485.

# 11. Revelation 10 — The Little Book and the Bittersweet Disappointment

> _The mighty angel of Revelation 10 comes down with a little book OPEN in his hand — the prophecy of Daniel, sealed "to the time of the end" (Dan. 12:4, 9) and now at last unsealed. Setting one foot on the sea and one on the land, he lifts his hand and swears that "time shall be no longer" — that no prophetic period reaches beyond the close of the 2300 days in 1844. The book is honey-sweet in the mouth (the blessed hope of Christ's near coming) but bitter in the belly (the great disappointment when, at the appointed time, the Lord did not come). To the disappointed the angel says, "Thou must prophesy again" — the renewed proclamation of the third angel that follows. (DAR 488-496; GC 374, 404, 423; SSP 187-190.)_

### The mighty angel with the OPEN book (Daniel unsealed)

- **Revelation 10:1-2** — "Another mighty angel come down from heaven, clothed with a cloud: and a rainbow upon his head, and his face as the sun... and he had in his hand a little book OPEN." A book once shut is now spread open at the time of the end — the symbol of the prophecy of Daniel made plain.

  > "And I saw another mighty angel come down from heaven, clothed with a cloud: and a rainbow was upon his head, and his face was as it were the sun, and his feet as pillars of fire. 2. And he had in his hand a little book open: and he set his right foot upon the sea, and his left foot on the earth." — DAR 488.1.

  > "There is no book spoken of as closed up and sealed except the book of Daniel's prophecy; and there is no account of the opening of that book, unless it be here in the 10th of Revelation. … Nothing more could be required … to prove that the little book which the angel had in his hand open, was the book of the prophecy of Daniel." — DAR 488.4.

- **Daniel 12:4** — "Shut up the words, and seal the book, even to the time of the end: many shall run to and fro, and knowledge shall be increased." Daniel's book was sealed only UNTIL the time of the end; therefore at the time of the end it must be opened — and there is no record of its opening save here in Revelation 10.

  > "Since this book was closed up only till the time of the end, it follows that at the time of the end the book would be opened; and as its closing was mentioned in prophecy, it would be but reasonable to expect that in the predictions of events to take place at the time of the end, the opening of this book would also be mentioned." — DAR 488.4.

  > "There the time of the end commenced, and the book was opened. And since that time, many have run to and fro, and knowledge on these prophetic subjects has marvelously increased." — DAR 489.1.

- **Daniel 12:9** — "Go thy way, Daniel: for the words are closed up and sealed till the time of the end." The same sealed book, the same appointed unsealing — the little book in the angel's hand is Daniel's prophecy laid open.

  > "There is no book spoken of as closed up and sealed except the book of Daniel's prophecy; and there is no account of the opening of that book, unless it be here in the 10th of Revelation." — DAR 488.4.

  > "That we are in the time of the end, when the book of this prophecy should no longer be sealed, but be open and understood, is shown by Revelation 10:1, 2, where a mighty angel is seen to come down from heaven with a little book in his hand open." — DAR 309.4.

- **Daniel 8:26** — "Shut thou up the vision; for it shall be for many days." The 2300-day vision was likewise sealed for "many days," to be understood only when the long period drew near.

  > "We read in Daniel of a book which was closed up and sealed to a certain time… Since this book was closed up only till the time of the end, it follows that at the time of the end the book would be opened." — DAR 488.4.

- **Daniel 8:17** — "At the time of the end shall be the vision." The vision belongs to the time of the end; only then would the book be opened and the chronology of the angel's message be understood.

  > "That we are in the time of the end, when the book of this prophecy should no longer be sealed, but be open and understood, is shown by Revelation 10:1, 2, where a mighty angel is seen to come down from heaven with a little book in his hand open." — DAR 309.4.

### The angel's posture, voice, and the sealed thunders

- **Revelation 10:2-3** — He set his right foot on the sea and his left on the earth, and "cried with a loud voice, as when a lion roareth." A world-wide proclamation — sea and land — uttered with the loud voice of a great message.

  > "The position of this angel, one foot upon the sea and the other on the land, denotes the wide extent of his proclamation by sea and by land. … he has one foot upon the sea, from which we may infer that his message would cross the ocean, and extend to the various nations and divisions of the globe." — DAR 490.1.

- **Revelation 10:3-4** — "Seven thunders uttered their voices... Seal up those things which the seven thunders uttered, and write them not." What the thunders spoke John was forbidden to write; we leave them where John left them — sealed, unwritten, and to us unknown.

  > "It would be vain to speculate to any great length upon the seven thunders, in hope of gaining a definite knowledge of what they uttered. We must acquiesce in the directions given to John concerning them, and leave them where he left them, sealed up, unwritten, and consequently to us unknown." — DAR 491.2.

### "Time shall be no longer" — no prophetic period beyond 1844

- **Revelation 10:5-6** — The angel "lifted up his hand to heaven, and sware by him that liveth for ever and ever... that there should be time no longer." Not that time itself should cease — the very next verse speaks of days yet to come — but that no more PROPHETIC TIME should be measured; the longest prophetic period reaches only to 1844.

  > "It must therefore mean prophetic time; for there is no other to which it can refer. Prophetic time shall be no more … but no prophetic period should extend beyond this message; those that reach to the latest point would all close there." — DAR 492.2.

- **Revelation 10:7** — "But in the days of the voice of the seventh angel... the mystery of God should be finished, as he hath declared to his servants the prophets." The seventh trumpet begins to sound when prophetic time is at an end (1844); the "mystery of God" is the gospel, to be finished in these closing days.

  > "In the days when he shall begin to sound, the mystery of God shall be finished. Not in the day when he shall begin to sound … but in the early years of his sounding, the mystery of God shall be finished." — DAR 493.2.

  > "It is the same, then, as if the angel had declared, In the days of the voice of the seventh angel, when he shall begin to sound, the gospel shall be finished. … It will be finished when the number of God's people is made up, mercy ceases to be offered, and probation closes." — DAR 494.1.

  > "The seventh trumpet began to sound when prophetic time was at an end, that is, in 1844. The mystery of God is the Gospel of Jesus Christ; the sacrifice of the Lamb of God." — SSP 187.1.

- **Daniel 8:14** — "Unto two thousand and three hundred days; then shall the sanctuary be cleansed." This longest of the prophetic lines, the 2300 years, terminating in the autumn of 1844, is the very "time" beyond which no prophetic period extends.

  > "The close of the prophetic period was 1844, so that the 'quickly' would be the time between 1840 and 1844, and the seventh trumpet began to sound when prophetic time was at an end, that is, in 1844." — SSP 187.1.

  > "It was ascertained that the decree was given near the close of the year 457 B. C., and therefore the prophetic period must reach to the fall of the year 1844." — CET 50.1.

- **Numbers 14:34** — "Each day for a year." The day-for-a-year rule (with Eze. 4:6) is the key that turns the 2300 days into 2300 years, reaching from 457 B.C. to A.D. 1844.

  > "The language cannot be intelligibly referred to anything else; for the word here rendered determined signifies 'cut off;' and there is no period from which the seventy weeks could be cut off but the 2300 days of the previous vision." — DAR 192.1.

  > "It was not at first perceived that if the decree did not go forth at the beginning of the year 457 B. C., the 2300 years would not be completed at the close of 1843. But it was ascertained that the decree was given near the close of the year 457 B. C., and therefore the prophetic period must reach to the fall of the year 1844." — CET 50.1.

- **Amos 3:7** — "Surely the Lord GOD will do nothing, but he revealeth his secret unto his servants the prophets." The mystery to be finished is the same the prophets foretold — God hides nothing from His servants.

  > "Here God's purpose to gather together all in Christ is called the 'mystery' of his will. This is accomplished through the gospel. … Here the gospel is declared plainly to be a mystery." — DAR 493.4.

  > "In view of these testimonies, few will be disposed to deny that the mystery of God is the gospel." — DAR 494.1.

### One angel, one message — Revelation 10 = the first angel of Revelation 14

- **Revelation 14:6-7** — "Another angel... having the everlasting gospel to preach... Saying with a loud voice, Fear God, and give glory to him; for the hour of his judgment is come." Both angels have a special message, both cry with a loud voice, both proclaim time and judgment — the angel of Revelation 10 and the first angel of Revelation 14 are the same advent proclamation.

  > "The points of identity between them are easily seen: (1) They both have a special message to proclaim; (2) they both utter their proclamation with a loud voice; (3) they both use similar language, referring to the great Creator … and (4) they both proclaim time, one swearing that time should be no more, and the other proclaiming that the hour of God's Judgment has come." — DAR 490.1.

  > "The sixth and seventh verses of the tenth chapter of Revelation are parallel with the sixth and seventh verses of the fourteenth chapter." — SSP 187.2.

- **Revelation 11:18** — "The time of the dead, that they should be judged... reward unto thy servants the prophets, and to the saints." The seventh trumpet brings in the judgment hour announced by the angel — the same investigative work begun in 1844.

  > "From the events to take place under the sounding of the seventh trumpet, its commencement may be located with sufficient definiteness at the close of the prophetic periods in 1844. Not many years from that date, then, the mystery of God is to be finished." — DAR 493.3.

### Eat the book — sweet in the mouth (the type of the prophets)

- **Revelation 10:8-9** — "Go and take the little book which is open... Take it, and eat it up; and it shall make thy belly bitter, but it shall be in thy mouth sweet as honey." John, standing as the representative of the church, is bidden to eat — to study the open prophecy until its meaning is comprehended.

  > "In verse 8, John himself is brought in to act a part as a representative of the church … This could most appropriately be set forth in the person of some member of the human family; hence John is himself called upon to act a part in this symbolic representation." — DAR 495.2.

  > "John, symbolizing God's people, was bidden to take it out of the angel's hand. … he said, 'Take it, and eat it up.' Eating the Word of God implies a careful study until the meaning is fully comprehended." — SSP 188.1.

- **Ezekiel 2:8-10** — "Open thy mouth, and eat that I give thee... a roll of a book... written within and without... lamentations, and mourning, and woe." The prophet's parallel: he too must eat a book before he proclaims.

  > "He said unto me, Take it, and eat it up… Eating the Word of God implies a careful study until the meaning is fully comprehended. Jesus often used the figure in a spiritual sense, referring to His body and the 'bread of life.'" — SSP 188.1.

- **Ezekiel 3:1-3** — "Eat this roll, and go speak... and it was in my mouth as honey for sweetness." Ezekiel ate the same sweet roll before his commission — the exact figure renewed in Revelation 10.

  > "He said unto me, Take it, and eat it up; and it shall make thy belly bitter, but it shall be in thy mouth sweet as honey. … Eating the Word of God implies a careful study until the meaning is fully comprehended." — SSP 188.1.

- **Jeremiah 15:16** — "Thy words were found, and I did eat them; and thy word was unto me the joy and rejoicing of mine heart." The honey-like sweetness of the advent truth was Jeremiah's joy in eating God's words — the same experience set under the same figure.

  > "There are not a few now living who have in their own experience met a striking fulfillment of these verses, in the joy with which they received the message of Christ's immediate second coming, the honey-like sweetness of the precious truths then brought out. … (See the same facts brought to view under a similar figure in Jeremiah 15:16-18.)" — DAR 495.3.

  > "Thy words were found, and I did eat them; and thy word was unto me the Joy and rejoicing of mine heart: for I am called by thy name, O Lord God of hosts. Jeremiah 15:16." — SSP 188.1.

### Bitter in the belly — the great disappointment of 1844

- **Revelation 10:10** — "I took the little book... and ate it up; and it was in my mouth sweet as honey: and as soon as I had eaten it, my belly was bitter." The blessed hope was honey to the taste; the passing of the appointed time turned it to wormwood — the disappointment, not the truth, was bitter.

  > "What had been so like honey to their taste, suddenly became like wormwood and gall. But those who had patience to endure … soon learned that the mistake was only in the event, not in the time." — DAR 495.3.

  > "The autumn of 1844 came and went, and the intensity of the disappointment was beyond description. No earthly inducement ever seemed so sweet as the message of His coming; no disappointment was ever so bitter as that experienced by the believers in the second coming of Christ." — SSP 189.1.

- **Habakkuk 2:3** — "The vision is yet for an appointed time, but at the end it shall speak, and not lie: though it tarry, wait for it; because it will surely come, it will not tarry." The "tarrying time" was foreseen; when the spring of 1844 passed, it was found the vision had not failed — the decree of 457 B.C. carried the period to the autumn.

  > "Therefore the vision of time did not tarry, though it had seemed to do so. We learned to rest upon the language of the prophet: 'The vision is yet for an appointed time, but at the end it shall speak, and not lie: though it tarry, wait for it; because it will surely come, it will not tarry.' Habakkuk 2:3." — CET 50.1.

  > "Those who had been disappointed were not long left in darkness; for in searching the prophetic periods with earnest prayer the error was discovered, and the tracing of the prophetic pencil down through the tarrying time." — CET 50.3.

  > "We are placed in a position which God foresaw his children would be placed in at the end of the vision; and for which he made provision, by the prophet Habakkuk … 'For the vision is yet for an appointed time, but at the end [of the prophetic periods] it shall speak and not lie: though it tarry [beyond their apparent termination], wait for it.'" — GSAM 153.2.

- **Habakkuk 2:1** — "I will stand upon my watch, and set me upon the tower, and will watch to see what he will say... when I am reproved." God provided beforehand for the disappointed watchers through Habakkuk's tower — patient waiting through the apparent delay.

  > "We are placed in a position which God foresaw his children would be placed in at the end of the vision; and for which he made provision, by the prophet Habakkuk, when he says, 'I will stand upon my watch, and set me upon the tower, and will watch to see what he will say unto me, and what I shall answer when I am reproved.'" — GSAM 153.2.

- **Daniel 8:27** — "I Daniel fainted, and was sick certain days... and I was astonished at the vision, but none understood it." Even the prophet was sickened and dismayed at the vision he could not yet comprehend — a foretaste of the bitterness of those who ate the book in 1844.

  > "What had been so like honey to their taste, suddenly became like wormwood and gall. But those who had patience to endure, so to speak, the digesting process, soon learned that the mistake was only in the event, not in the time." — DAR 495.3.

- **Luke 24:21** — "We trusted that it had been he which should have redeemed Israel." The disciples' bitter disappointment at the cross was the very type of 1844 — "We looked for Him to save us, but He has not come."

  > "Yet this disappointment was not so great as was that experienced by the disciples at the time of Christ's first advent. … The disciples were accomplishing the purpose of God; yet they were doomed to a bitter disappointment." — GC 404.2.

  > "The disciples, weeping at the tomb over a crucified Saviour, seemed to drain the cup of bitterness, but a potion no less galling was drunk by the disciples in 1844. 'We thought that it was He that would save Israel,' was echoed eighteen hundred years later in the words, 'We looked for Him to save us, but He has not come.'" — SSP 189.1.

- **Acts 1:6-7** — "Lord, wilt thou at this time restore again the kingdom to Israel?... It is not for you to know the times or the seasons." As the first disciples mistook the EVENT while God's purpose stood, so the advent believers mistook the event of 1844, not the time.

  > "When Jesus rode triumphantly into Jerusalem, His followers believed that He was about to ascend the throne of David and deliver Israel from her oppressors. … Their expectations had not been realized in a single particular, and their hopes died with Jesus." — GC 404.2.

  > "Those who had patience to endure, so to speak, the digesting process, soon learned that the mistake was only in the event, not in the time, and that what the angel had given them was not unto death, but to their nourishment and support." — DAR 495.3.

### The sanctuary — the key that explained the bitterness

- **Daniel 8:14** — "Then shall the sanctuary be cleansed." When the cloud of disappointment was pierced, the truth of the heavenly sanctuary was discovered: the event of 1844 was not the second advent but the opening of the investigative judgment — Christ's entrance within the veil.

  > "The subject of the sanctuary was the key which unlocked the mystery of the disappointment of 1844. It opened to view a complete system of truth, connected and harmonious, showing that God's hand had directed the great advent movement." — GC 423.1.

  > "When the cloud was pierced by eyes which searched for Christ, the truth in regard to the heavenly sanctuary was discovered. … In 1844 the antitypical work of the day of atonement was begun in the heavenly sanctuary. That is, Christ at that time passed within the veil … The investigative judgment was opened." — SSP 187.2-188.

- **Revelation 11:18** — "The time of the dead, that they should be judged." The sanctuary truth showed that the hour of judgment, not the end of the world, had come — the mistake was in the event, never in the time.

  > "As the disciples of Jesus after the terrible night of their anguish and disappointment were 'glad when they saw the Lord,' so did those now rejoice who had looked in faith for His second coming. … Now in the holy of holies they again beheld Him, their compassionate High Priest, soon to appear as their king and deliverer." — GC 423.1.

  > "From the events to take place under the sounding of the seventh trumpet, its commencement may be located with sufficient definiteness at the close of the prophetic periods in 1844." — DAR 493.3.

### "Thou must prophesy again" — the post-disappointment commission

- **Revelation 10:11** — "And he said unto me, Thou must prophesy again before many peoples, and nations, and tongues, and kings." After the first and second messages ceased as leading proclamations, a renewed message was foretold — the third angel's message, now in process of fulfilment.

  > "Another message is to go forth after the time when the first and second messages, as leading proclamations, ceased. In other words, we have here a prophecy of the third angel's message, now, as we believe, in process of fulfilment. … it is to go before 'many peoples, and nations, and tongues, and kings.'" — DAR 496.2.

  > "Many expected that those who passed through the disappointment, would forever sink out of sight, but the angel said, 'Thou must prophesy again, before many peoples, and nations, and tongues, and kings.' This foretells the third message of Revelation 14:9-12, which will go to all the world, increasing as it goes, until it swells into a loud cry." — SSP 190.1.

- **Revelation 14:6-7** — "The everlasting gospel... to every nation, and kindred, and tongue, and people." The renewed proclamation goes again to the same world-wide audience — peoples, nations, tongues, kings — swelling at last into the loud cry.

  > "Many peoples, the nations of earth, representatives from every tongue, rich and poor, even kings on their thrones, will hear this last message of mercy which goes to the earth in the beginning of the sounding of the seventh trumpet. … what is begun on earth, mingles with the song of the redeemed on the other shore." — SSP 190.2.

- **Habakkuk 2:3** — "It will surely come, it will not tarry." The vision that seemed to tarry will yet speak and not lie; the disappointed are commanded to wait and to prophesy again, for the promise stands sure.

  > "For the vision is yet for an appointed time, but at the end [of the prophetic periods] it shall speak and not lie: though it tarry [beyond their apparent termination], wait for it; because it will surely come, [in the fullness of the prophetic times, beyond which] it will not tarry.' Habakkuk 2:1-3." — GSAM 153.2.

  > "For yet a little while, and he that shall come will come, and will not tarry. Now the just shall live by faith; but if any man draw back, my soul shall have no pleasure in him." — GSAM 153.3.

  > "Those who had been disappointed were not long left in darkness; for in searching the prophetic periods with earnest prayer the error was discovered, and the tracing of the prophetic pencil down through the tarrying time." — CET 50.3.

### Summary: the bittersweet message of Revelation 10

| Symbol in Revelation 10         | Historicist meaning                       | Fulfilment                        |
| ------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| Little book OPEN (v.2)          | Daniel's prophecy unsealed (Dan. 12:4, 9) | Understood at the time of the end |
| Foot on sea, foot on land (v.2) | World-wide proclamation                   | The 1840-44 advent movement       |
| "Time no longer" (v.6)          | No prophetic period beyond 1844           | The 2300 days end Oct. 22, 1844   |
| Sweet in the mouth (v.9-10)     | The blessed hope of Christ's coming       | Joy of the advent believers       |
| Bitter in the belly (v.10)      | The great disappointment                  | The Lord did not come in 1844     |
| "Prophesy again" (v.11)         | The renewed message                       | The third angel's message         |

_(DAR 488-496; GC 374.1, 404.2, 423.1; SSP 187.1-190.2; CET 50.1-50.4; GSAM 153.2.)_

---

# 12. The 1843 & 1850 Charts, the Tarrying Time, and the True Midnight Cry

> _Habakkuk 2 is the hub of the whole Advent experience: "Write the vision, make it plain upon tables" was fulfilled in the prophetic chart of Fitch and Hale (1842-43) and its corrected successor (1850) — the vision made plain on tables, afterward endorsed as "directed by the hand of the Lord." "Though it tarry, wait for it" foretold the tarrying time that followed the spring-1844 disappointment; and the parable of the ten virgins maps the first cry, the slumber, and the TRUE midnight cry — Samuel Snow's seventh-month movement, "Behold, the bridegroom cometh," fixing the tenth day of the seventh month, Oct. 22, 1844, as the cleansing of the sanctuary. The disappointment was resolved when it was seen that the Bridegroom came, not to earth, but to the Ancient of Days (Dan. 7:13) to receive His kingdom. (GC 392-408, 426-432; EW 74, 232-238; TRMC, Aug. 22, 1844.)_

### The vision made plain upon tables — the prophetic chart

- **Habakkuk 2:1** — "I will stand upon my watch, and set me upon the tower, and will watch to see what he will say unto me." The watchman on the tower is the believer who studies prophecy and waits for its word; Bates titled his tract on this very pathway "Second Advent Way Marks and High Heaps," urging the people to "stand continually upon the watch tower in the day time."

  > "Set thee up waymarks, make thee high heaps; set thy heart towards the highway, even the way that thou wentest." Jeremiah 31:21. — BP2 45.

  > "But, bless the Lord, there are some that are fully determined to correct their dead reckoning, by watching every opportunity for an observation of the Sun of Righteousness, and by faith cling fast to all the promises, doing as the Prophet has shown them - 'Stand continually upon the watch tower in the day time, and set in their ward every night,' watching for all the terrestrial and celestial land marks and heaps in their pathway…" — BP2 46.

  > "Among these prophecies was that of Habakkuk 2:1-4: 'I will stand upon my watch, and set me upon the tower, and will watch to see what He will say unto me…'" — GC 392.

- **Habakkuk 2:2** — "Write the vision, and make it plain upon tables, that he may run that readeth it." As early as 1842 this command suggested to Charles Fitch the preparation of a prophetic chart to illustrate Daniel and the Revelation; its publication was regarded as a literal fulfillment of Habakkuk, the vision "made plain upon tables."

  > "As early as 1842 the direction given in this prophecy to 'write the vision, and make it plain upon tables, that he may run that readeth it,' had suggested to Charles Fitch the preparation of a prophetic chart to illustrate the visions of Daniel and the Revelation. The publication of this chart was regarded as a fulfillment of the command given by Habakkuk." — GC 392.

- **Habakkuk 2:2** — The chart of 1843 (Fitch & Hale) was afterward declared inspired in its providential use: "I have seen that the 1843 chart was directed by the hand of the Lord, and that it should not be altered; that the figures were as He wanted them; that His hand was over and hid a mistake in some of the figures, so that none could see it, until His hand was removed." The corrected 1850 chart then displayed the figures rightly.

  > "I have seen that the 1843 chart was directed by the hand of the Lord, and that it should not be altered; that the figures were as He wanted them; that His hand was over and hid a mistake in some of the figures, so that none could see it, until His hand was removed." — EW 74.

- **Daniel 8:14** — "Unto two thousand and three hundred days; then shall the sanctuary be cleansed." This was the central line of the chart — the great prophetic period whose terminus the tables were "made plain" to proclaim.

  > "Then I saw in relation to the 'daily' (Daniel 8:12) that the word 'sacrifice' was supplied by man's wisdom, and does not belong to the text, and that the Lord gave the correct view of it to those who gave the judgment hour cry." — EW 74.

  > "That which led to this movement was the discovery that the decree of Artaxerxes for the restoration of Jerusalem, which formed the starting point for the period of the 2300 days, went into effect in the autumn of the year 457 B.C.… Reckoning from the autumn of 457, the 2300 years terminate in the autumn of 1844." — GC 398-399.

### The day-for-a-year rule the chart was built upon

- **Numbers 14:34** — "Each day for a year, shall ye bear your iniquities" — the inspired key that a prophetic day stands for a literal year, the rule by which the 2300 days were reckoned as 2300 years on the chart.

  > "Following his rule of making Scripture its own interpreter, Miller learned that a day in symbolic prophecy represents a year (Numbers 14:34; Ezekiel 4:6); he saw that the period of 2300 prophetic days, or literal years, would extend far beyond the close of the Jewish dispensation…" — GC 324.

  > "Seventy weeks, then, or 490 days of the 2300, were cut off upon, or allotted to, Jerusalem and the Jews… if these seventy weeks are fulfilled as weeks of years, then the 2300 days, of which these are a part, are so many years." — DAR 193.

- **Ezekiel 4:6** — "I have appointed thee each day for a year" — the second witness to the year-day principle, doubly establishing the measure of the prophetic periods.

  > "Following his rule of making Scripture its own interpreter, Miller learned that a day in symbolic prophecy represents a year (Numbers 14:34; Ezekiel 4:6)…" — GC 324.

  > "By the events given to transpire in the seventy weeks, the prophecy is tested… if these seventy weeks are fulfilled as weeks of years, then the 2300 days, of which these are a part, are so many years. Thus the events of the seventy weeks furnish a key to the whole vision." — DAR 193.

### Though it tarry, wait for it — the tarrying time

- **Habakkuk 2:3** — "For the vision is yet for an appointed time... though it tarry, wait for it; because it will surely come, it will not tarry." No one noticed before the disappointment that an apparent delay — a tarrying time — stood in the same prophecy; after the spring of 1844 this scripture became very significant.

  > "No one, however, then noticed that an apparent delay in the accomplishment of the vision—a tarrying time—is presented in the same prophecy. After the disappointment, this scripture appeared very significant: 'The vision is yet for an appointed time…though it tarry, wait for it; because it will surely come, it will not tarry.'" — GC 392.

- **Habakkuk 2:3** — The disappointed but faithful ones, searching again, "discovered a tarrying time — 'Though it tarry, wait for it.' In their love for Christ's immediate coming, they had overlooked the tarrying of the vision, which was calculated to manifest the true waiting ones." The hand of the Lord was now removed from the figures, and the periods were seen to reach to 1844, not 1843.

  > "The hand of the Lord was removed from the figures, and the mistake was explained. They saw that the prophetic periods reached to 1844… Light from the Word of God shone upon their position, and they discovered a tarrying time—'Though it [the vision] tarry, wait for it.' In their love for Christ's immediate coming, they had overlooked the tarrying of the vision, which was calculated to manifest the true waiting ones." — EW 236.

- **Habakkuk 2:4** — "The just shall live by his faith." The believers held fast through the dark interval by faith, not by sight, exactly as the prophecy that foretold the tarrying also prescribed the remedy.

  > "After the disappointment, this scripture appeared very significant: 'The vision is yet for an appointed time…though it tarry, wait for it; because it will surely come, it will not tarry.... The just shall live by his faith.'" — GC 392.

- **Hebrews 10:36-37** — "Ye have need of patience... For yet a little while, and he that shall come will come, and will not tarry." The apostle's own echo of Habakkuk — patience through delay, then certain coming — strengthened the waiting Advent band.

  > "The waiting ones rejoiced, believing that He who knows the end from the beginning had looked down through the ages and, foreseeing their disappointment, had given them words of courage and hope. Had it not been for such portions of Scripture, admonishing them to wait with patience and to hold fast their confidence in God's word, their faith would have failed in that trying hour." — GC 392-393.

- **Hebrews 10:38** — "Now the just shall live by faith: but if any man draw back, my soul shall have no pleasure in him." The tarrying separated those who lived by faith from those who drew back when the Lord did not come at the expected hour.

  > "And now to take up again the heavy burden of life's cares and perplexities, and to endure the taunts and sneers of a scoffing world, was a terrible trial of faith and patience." — GC 404.

### The two cries — the parable of the ten virgins

- **Matthew 25:1** — "Then shall the kingdom of heaven be likened unto ten virgins, which took their lamps, and went forth to meet the bridegroom." The lamps are the Bible; the going forth is the widespread reformation under the first angel's message proclaiming Christ's soon coming.

  > "The coming of Christ, as announced by the first angel's message, was understood to be represented by the coming of the bridegroom. The widespread reformation under the proclamation of His soon coming, answered to the going forth of the virgins… All had taken their lamps, the Bible, and by its light had gone forth to meet the Bridegroom." — GC 393-394.

- **Matthew 25:2-4** — The wise took oil in their vessels; the foolish took none. The wise had the grace of the Spirit and a personal faith that disappointment could not overthrow; the foolish had only a theory of the truth and good emotions.

  > "The latter class had received the grace of God, the regenerating, enlightening power of the Holy Spirit… These had a personal experience, a faith in God and in His word, which could not be overthrown by disappointment and delay. Others 'took their lamps, and took no oil with them.'… satisfied with the flickering light of good emotions, without a thorough understanding of the truth or a genuine work of grace in the heart." — GC 394.

- **Matthew 25:5** — "While the bridegroom tarried, they all slumbered and slept." The tarrying of the bridegroom is the passing of the spring-1844 time of expectation — the disappointment and seeming delay — during which all, wise and foolish alike, slumbered.

  > "'While the bridegroom tarried, they all slumbered and slept.' By the tarrying of the bridegroom is represented the passing of the time when the Lord was expected, the disappointment, and the seeming delay… 'They all slumbered and slept;' one class in unconcern and abandonment of their faith, the other class patiently waiting till clearer light should be given." — GC 394.

- **Matthew 25:6** — "And at midnight there was a cry made, Behold, the bridegroom cometh; go ye out to meet him." In the summer of 1844, midway between the spring expectation and the autumn fulfilment, the message was proclaimed in the very words of Scripture — this is the TRUE midnight cry, the seventh-month movement.

  > "In the summer of 1844, midway between the time when it had been first thought that the 2300 days would end, and the autumn of the same year, to which it was afterward found that they extended, the message was proclaimed in the very words of Scripture: 'Behold, the Bridegroom cometh!'" — GC 398.

  > "This was the midnight cry, which was to give power to the second angel's message. Angels were sent from heaven to arouse the discouraged saints and prepare them for the great work before them… Angels were sent to the humble, devoted ones, and constrained them to raise the cry, 'Behold, the Bridegroom cometh; go ye out to meet Him!'" — EW 238.

- **Matthew 25:7** — "Then all those virgins arose, and trimmed their lamps." The midnight cry roused the whole waiting body; like a tidal wave it swept the land, fanaticism vanishing before it "like early frost before the rising sun."

  > "Like a tidal wave the movement swept over the land. From city to city, from village to village, and into remote country places it went, until the waiting people of God were fully aroused. Fanaticism disappeared before this proclamation like early frost before the rising sun." — GC 400.

- **Matthew 25:10** — "They that were ready went in with him to the marriage: and the door was shut." Those who, by the testimony of Scripture, followed Christ by faith into the most holy place "went in" to the marriage; this took place in heaven while the saints were yet on earth.

  > "At the appointed time the Bridegroom came, not to the earth, as the people expected, but to the Ancient of Days in heaven, to the marriage… 'They that were ready went in with Him to the marriage: and the door was shut.' They were not to be present in person at the marriage; for it takes place in heaven, while they are upon the earth." — GC 427.

- **Matthew 25:13** — "Watch therefore, for ye know neither the day nor the hour wherein the Son of man cometh." The watching command frames the whole parable — and the disappointment proved that the date of the event, not the spiritual watch, was what God had appointed to test His people.

  > "Chapter 25 opens with the words: 'Then shall the kingdom of heaven be likened unto ten virgins.' Here is brought to view the church living in the last days… In this parable their experience is illustrated by the incidents of an Eastern marriage." — GC 393.

  > "Miller and his associates fulfilled prophecy and gave a message which Inspiration had foretold should be given to the world, but which they could not have given had they fully understood the prophecies pointing out their disappointment… The first and second angels' messages were given at the right time and accomplished the work which God designed to accomplish by them." — GC 405.

### The seventh-month movement — Snow's argument from the types

- **Leviticus 23:27** — "On the tenth day of this seventh month there shall be a day of atonement... ye shall afflict your souls." Snow reasoned from the Mosaic types that, as the spring feasts (Passover, firstfruits, Pentecost) had met their antitype to the very day at Christ's first advent, so the autumn type — the Day of Atonement on the tenth of the seventh month — must meet its antitype in the cleansing of the sanctuary that very year.

  > "Thus we see that those types that pointed to events connected with our Lord's first coming, were fulfilled exactly at the time of their observance. And every one who is not willingly blind must see, and feel too, that those which remain unfulfilled, will be fulfilled with an equally strict regard to time… Those types which were to be observed in the 7th month, have never yet had their fulfilment in the antitype." — TRMC, Aug. 22, 1844, p. 4.

  > "Arguments drawn from the Old Testament types also pointed to the autumn as the time when the event represented by the 'cleansing of the sanctuary' must take place. This was made very clear as attention was given to the manner in which the types relating to the first advent of Christ had been fulfilled." — GC 399.

- **Leviticus 16:29-30** — "In the seventh month, on the tenth day... ye shall afflict your souls... for on that day shall the priest make an atonement for you, to cleanse you." The high priest entered the most holy place once a year on this day to cleanse the sanctuary — the exact type of the antitypical cleansing of Daniel 8:14, which Snow placed on the tenth day of the seventh month, 1844.

  > "Another type is given in Leviticus 23:26-32, that is, the day of atonement or reconciliation, on the tenth day of the 7th month, in which the high priest went into the most holy place of the tabernacle, presenting the blood of the victim before the mercy-seat, after which on the same day be came out and blessed the waiting congregation of Israel… As this was on the tenth day of the 7th month, so on that day Jesus will certainly come, because not a single point of the law is to fail." — TRMC, Aug. 22, 1844, p. 4.

  > "Under the Mosaic system the cleansing of the sanctuary, or the great Day of Atonement, occurred on the tenth day of the seventh Jewish month (Leviticus 16:29-34), when the high priest, having made an atonement for all Israel, and thus removed their sins from the sanctuary, came forth and blessed the people." — GC 399.

  > "In the examination of the sanctuary, on chapter 8:14, we saw that a time came when the earthly sanctuary gave place to the heavenly, and the priestly ministration was transferred to that… The last event, therefore, of the seventy weeks, here brought to view, is the anointing of the heavenly tabernacle, or the opening of the ministration there." — DAR 193.

- **Leviticus 16:33-34** — "He shall make an atonement for the holy sanctuary... And this shall be an everlasting statute... to make an atonement for the children of Israel for all their sins once a year." The yearly, fixed-date character of the type fixed the antitype's date: "the tenth day of the seventh Jewish month... which in the year 1844 fell upon the twenty-second of October."

  > "The tenth day of the seventh month, the great Day of Atonement, the time of the cleansing of the sanctuary, which in the year 1844 fell upon the twenty-second of October, was regarded as the time of the Lord's coming. This was in harmony with the proofs already presented that the 2300 days would terminate in the autumn, and the conclusion seemed irresistible." — GC 399.

- **Leviticus 23:29** — "Whatsoever soul it be that shall not be afflicted in that same day, he shall be cut off from among his people." The solemnity of afflicting the soul on that day underscored, for the movement, the searching, heart-humbling character of the autumn cry.

  > "There was little ecstatic joy, but rather deep searching of heart, confession of sin, and forsaking of the world. A preparation to meet the Lord was the burden of agonizing spirits. There was persevering prayer and unreserved consecration to God." — GC 400.

  > "A mighty work was accomplished by the midnight cry. The message was heart-searching, leading the believers to seek a living experience for themselves. They knew that they could not lean upon one another." — EW 238.

- **Leviticus 25:9** — "Then shalt thou cause the trumpet of the jubile to sound on the tenth day of the seventh month, in the day of atonement." Snow joined the jubilee trumpet to the Day of Atonement: as the jubilee proclaimed liberty and redemption to all the land on the tenth of the seventh month, so the antitypical jubilee would bring "the glorious deliverance of the people of God."

  > "In Leviticus 25:8-13, 23, 24, we find that on the tenth day of the seventh month, in the fiftieth year, the jubilee trumpet was always to be blown, and redemption granted to all the land. Let any man read carefully the connection of this subject, and he must surely see that this is a most striking type of the glorious deliverance of the people of God…" — TRMC, Aug. 22, 1844, p. 4.

### The seventh-month date — Oct. 22, 1844

- **Daniel 8:14** — "Unto two thousand and three hundred days; then shall the sanctuary be cleansed." The discovery that the decree of Artaxerxes (the starting point) went into effect in the autumn of 457 B.C. — not at the year's beginning — moved the terminus of the 2300 years to the autumn of 1844, harmonizing prophecy with the type.

  > "That which led to this movement was the discovery that the decree of Artaxerxes for the restoration of Jerusalem, which formed the starting point for the period of the 2300 days, went into effect in the autumn of the year 457 B.C., and not at the beginning of the year, as had been formerly believed. Reckoning from the autumn of 457, the 2300 years terminate in the autumn of 1844." — GC 398.

- **Leviticus 23:32** — "In the ninth day of the month at even... from even unto even, shall ye celebrate your sabbath." The Day of Atonement was reckoned by the actual Karaite (not Rabbinic) new moon, which placed the tenth day of the seventh month in 1844 on October 22 — the precise date of the true midnight cry.

  > "The tenth day of the seventh month, the great Day of Atonement, the time of the cleansing of the sanctuary, which in the year 1844 fell upon the twenty-second of October, was regarded as the time of the Lord's coming." — GC 399-400.

- **Hebrews 10:37** — "He that shall come will come, and will not tarry." After the long uncertainty, the seventh-month cry came with the force of a definite, scripturally-fixed point of time, and "the conclusion seemed irresistible."

  > "This was in harmony with the proofs already presented that the 2300 days would terminate in the autumn, and the conclusion seemed irresistible." — GC 399-400.

### Why the time CAN be known — answering "no man knoweth"

- **Amos 3:7** — "Surely the Lord GOD will do nothing, but he revealeth his secret unto his servants the prophets." God hides nothing of His great purposes from His waiting people; the prophetic periods were given to be understood, not concealed.

  > "'The secret things,' says Moses, 'belong unto the Lord our God: but those things which are revealed belong unto us and to our children forever;' and the Lord declares by the prophet Amos, that He 'will do nothing, but He revealeth His secret unto His servants the prophets.'… The students of God's word may, then, confidently expect to find the most stupendous event to take place in human history clearly pointed out in the Scriptures of truth." — GC 324.

  > "The Old Testament contains the testimony of the Father concerning his Son, and concerning the time of both his first and second comings. Therefore the time is to be understood." — TRMC, p. 1.

- **Matthew 24:36** — "Of that day and hour knoweth no man, no, not the angels... but my Father only." Snow showed that since this declares the same of the Son as of angels and men, it cannot mean the time is forever unknowable; rather, "none but God the Father maketh known" the time — and through His word He does make it known.

  > "But if it prove this, it likewise proves that the Son of God himself is never to know the time: for the passage declares precisely the same concerning him, that it does concerning angels and men… So in the passage first quoted, it is declared that none but God the Father maketh known the day and hour, that is, the definite time of the second coming of his Son. And this necessarily implies that God makes the time known." — TRMC, Aug. 22, 1844, p. 1.

- **Daniel 12:10** — "None of the wicked shall understand; but the wise shall understand." The wise were promised understanding of the very prophetic time the careless could not see — the warrant of the whole movement.

  > "See Daniel 12:10, 'Many shall be purified, and made white, and tried; but the wicked shall do wickedly; and none of the wicked shall understand; but the wise shall understand.'… It is by the teaching of his word, as we are led therein by the Holy Spirit, that we are to understand the time of the coming of our Glorious King." — TRMC, p. 1.

### The disappointment resolved — the Bridegroom came to the Ancient of Days

- **Daniel 7:13** — "One like the Son of man came with the clouds of heaven, and came to the Ancient of days, and they brought him near before him." This coming — to the Ancient of Days in heaven, NOT to the earth — is the same event as the bridegroom going in to the marriage in the parable. The disappointment lay not in the date but in mistaking the event.

  > "The coming of Christ as our high priest to the most holy place, for the cleansing of the sanctuary, brought to view in Daniel 8:14; the coming of the Son of man to the Ancient of Days, as presented in Daniel 7:13… are descriptions of the same event; and this is also represented by the coming of the bridegroom to the marriage, described by Christ in the parable of the ten virgins, of Matthew 25." — GC 426.

- **Daniel 7:14** — "There was given him dominion, and glory, and a kingdom... his dominion is an everlasting dominion." The marriage of Matthew 25 is Christ's reception of His kingdom from the Ancient of Days; "the bridegroom came... not to the earth, as the people expected, but to the Ancient of Days in heaven, to the marriage, the reception of His kingdom."

  > "The marriage represents the reception by Christ of His kingdom… Christ, as stated by the prophet Daniel, will receive from the Ancient of Days in heaven, 'dominion, and glory, and a kingdom;' He will receive the New Jerusalem, the capital of His kingdom, 'prepared as a bride adorned for her husband.'" — GC 426-427.

- **Daniel 8:14** — The true event of Oct. 22, 1844, was the beginning of the cleansing of the heavenly sanctuary — Christ's entrance into the most holy place — which Daniel 8:14, Daniel 7:13, and Matthew 25 all describe as one. The TIME was right; only the EVENT had been misread.

  > "The coming of Christ as our high priest to the most holy place, for the cleansing of the sanctuary, brought to view in Daniel 8:14; the coming of the Son of man to the Ancient of Days, as presented in Daniel 7:13… are descriptions of the same event; and this is also represented by the coming of the bridegroom to the marriage… of Matthew 25." — GC 426.

  > "Now the important point in this type is the completion of the reconciliation at the coming of the high priest out of the holy place. The high priest was a type of Jesus our High Priest; the most holy place a type of heaven itself…" — TRMC, Aug. 22, 1844, p. 4.

- **Habakkuk 2:20** — "But the LORD is in his holy temple: let all the earth keep silence before him." The answer to the disappointment was that the Lord had entered the holy temple above to begin His closing ministry — exactly the prophet's own conclusion to the vision of the appointed time.

  > "At the appointed time the Bridegroom came, not to the earth, as the people expected, but to the Ancient of Days in heaven, to the marriage, the reception of His kingdom… But they are to understand His work, and to follow Him by faith as He goes in before God." — GC 427.

### The everlasting gospel that drove the chart and the cry

- **Revelation 14:6** — "I saw another angel fly in the midst of heaven, having the everlasting gospel to preach unto them that dwell on the earth." The chart, the tarrying, and the midnight cry were all the outworking of this first angel's worldwide proclamation.

  > "I saw that God was in the proclamation of the time in 1843. It was His design to arouse the people and bring them to a testing point, where they should decide for or against the truth… Ministers laid aside their sectarian views and feelings and united in proclaiming the coming of Jesus. Wherever the message was given, it moved the people." — EW 232.

  > "It is a pleasing feature of the prophetic word that the people of God are never brought into positions of trial and difficulty, and there abandoned… but it takes them through to the end, and shows the issue in every conflict." — DAR 582.

- **Revelation 14:7** — "Fear God, and give glory to him; for the hour of his judgment is come." Miller and the pioneers "sounded far and near the cry, 'Fear God, and give glory to Him; for the hour of His judgment is come'" — the judgment-hour message that the cleansing of the sanctuary, begun Oct. 22, 1844, fulfilled.

  > "He ceased not to preach the everlasting gospel to crowds wherever he was invited, sounding far and near the cry, 'Fear God, and give glory to Him; for the hour of His judgment is come.'" — EW 232.

  > "A great religious awakening under the proclamation of Christ's soon coming is foretold in the prophecy of the first angel's message of Revelation 14… 'With a loud voice' he proclaims the message: 'Fear God, and give glory to Him; for the hour of His judgment is come.'" — GC 355.

### Two errors corrected

| Error                                                                                                                                                                    | Correction                                                                                                                                                                      | Reference                    |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| The periods were thought to end in **1843** (the year-day count was reckoned without allowing for the autumn-to-autumn span and the missing year between B.C. and A.D.). | Reckoning from the **autumn of 457 B.C.**, the 2300 years terminate in the **autumn of 1844** (Oct. 22).                                                                        | _(GC 398; EW 236; DAR 192.)_ |
| The **event** was thought to be Christ's coming **to the earth**.                                                                                                        | The event was Christ's entrance **into the most holy place** of the heavenly sanctuary — the Bridegroom going in to the marriage, the Son of man coming to the Ancient of Days. | _(GC 426-427; DAR 222.)_     |

---

# 13. The Woman Clothed in White (Revelation 12) — the True Church

> _In prophecy a pure woman is the symbol of the true church, and a corrupt woman of an apostate church (Eze. 23; Rev. 17). The woman of Revelation 12 is the church of all ages: clothed with the sun of the gospel, the moon of the Mosaic types beneath her feet, crowned with the twelve apostles. She brings forth the man child, Christ; flees into the wilderness for 1260 years (538-1798) while the papacy reigns; is preserved through the dark ages; and at the last the dragon makes war on the remnant of her seed who keep the commandments of God and have the testimony of Jesus. (DAR 509-519; GC 381-382, 433-439.)_

### The woman is the church — the bride espoused to Christ

- **Revelation 12:1** — "A woman clothed with the sun, and the moon under her feet, and upon her head a crown of twelve stars." A pure woman is the church; a corrupt woman (Rev. 17) an apostate church. By parity of reasoning the pure woman here is the true church.

  > "“A woman,” the true church. A corrupt woman is used to represent an apostate or corrupt church. Ezekiel 23:2-4; Revelation 17:3-6, 15, 18. By parity of reasoning, a pure woman, as in this instance, would represent the true church." — DAR 509.3.

- **2 Corinthians 11:2** — "I have espoused you to one husband, that I may present you as a chaste virgin to Christ." The church is the betrothed virgin; Paul's figure fixes the symbol.

  > "In the Bible the sacred and enduring character of the relation that exists between Christ and His church is represented by the union of marriage… And Paul employs the same figure in the New Testament when he says: “I have espoused you to one husband, that I may present you as a chaste virgin to Christ.” 2 Corinthians 11:2." — GC 381.2.

- **Jeremiah 6:2** — "I have likened the daughter of Zion to a comely and delicate woman." God's own likeness for His people is a woman — Zion the church.

  > "“A woman,” the true church… By parity of reasoning, a pure woman, as in this instance, would represent the true church." — DAR 509.3.

- **Isaiah 54:5-6** — "Thy Maker is thine husband... the LORD hath called thee as a woman forsaken... a wife of youth." The covenant between God and His people is a marriage; the church is His wife.

  > "The Lord has joined His people to Himself by a solemn covenant, He promising to be their God, and they pledging themselves to be His and His alone… And, again: “I am married unto you.” Jeremiah 3:14." — GC 381.2.

- **Hosea 2:19-20** — "I will betroth thee unto me for ever... in righteousness, and in judgment... and thou shalt know the LORD." The marriage figure of Christ and His church, quoted to establish the woman-symbol.

  > "He declares: “I will betroth thee unto Me forever; yea, I will betroth thee unto Me in righteousness, and in judgment, and in loving-kindness, and in mercies.” Hosea 2:19." — GC 381.2.

- **Ephesians 5:25-27** — "Christ also loved the church, and gave himself for it... that he might present it to himself a glorious church, not having spot, or wrinkle... holy and without blemish." The pure woman is the church Christ cleanses and presents spotless.

  > "In the Bible the sacred and enduring character of the relation that exists between Christ and His church is represented by the union of marriage. The Lord has joined His people to Himself by a solemn covenant, He promising to be their God, and they pledging themselves to be His and His alone." — GC 381.2.

- **Isaiah 62:5** — "As the bridegroom rejoiceth over the bride, so shall thy God rejoice over thee." The relation of God to His church is that of bridegroom to bride.

  > "In the Bible the sacred and enduring character of the relation that exists between Christ and His church is represented by the union of marriage." — GC 381.2.

- **Galatians 4:26** — "Jerusalem which is above is free, which is the mother of us all." The woman, the church, is the mother who brings forth her children — and the man child.

  > "Verses 1 and 2 cover a period of time commencing just previous to the opening of the present dispensation, when the church was earnestly longing for and expecting the advent of the Messiah, and extending to the time of the full establishment of the gospel church with its crown of twelve apostles. Luke 2:25, 26, 38." — DAR 510.1.

- **Revelation 19:7-8** — "The marriage of the Lamb is come, and his wife hath made herself ready... arrayed in fine linen, clean and white: for the fine linen is the righteousness of saints." The woman's white raiment is the imputed righteousness of Christ — the same sun-clothing of Revelation 12.

  > "In the Bible the sacred and enduring character of the relation that exists between Christ and His church is represented by the union of marriage." — GC 381.2.

### Her clothing — sun, moon, and crown of stars

- **Revelation 12:1** (clause) — "clothed with the sun." The sun is the light and glory of the gospel dispensation; the church is clothed with the righteousness of the gospel just risen.

  > "“The sun,” the light and glory of the gospel dispensation." — DAR 509.4.

  > "The woman, the church, had the moon under her feet; that is, the Mosaic dispensation had just ended, and the woman was clothed with the light of the gospel sun, which had just risen." — DAR 510.2.

- **Malachi 4:2** — "The Sun of righteousness arise with healing in his wings." Christ Himself is the Sun whose light clothes the woman — the antitype and substance.

  > "“The moon,” the Mosaic dispensation. As the moon shines with a borrowed light derived from the sun, so the former dispensation shone with a light borrowed from the present. There they had the type and shadow; here we have the antitype and substance." — DAR 509.5.

  > "The Mosaic dispensation shone with a light borrowed from the Christian dispensation, just as the moon shines with light borrowed from the sun. How appropriate, therefore, to represent the former by the moon, and the latter by the sun." — DAR 510.2.

- **Revelation 12:1** (clause) — "the moon under her feet." The moon is the Mosaic dispensation: as the moon shines with borrowed light from the sun, so the former dispensation shone with light borrowed from the gospel — type and shadow beneath the antitype.

  > "“The moon,” the Mosaic dispensation. As the moon shines with a borrowed light derived from the sun, so the former dispensation shone with a light borrowed from the present. There they had the type and shadow; here we have the antitype and substance." — DAR 509.5.

  > "The woman, the church, had the moon under her feet; that is, the Mosaic dispensation had just ended, and the woman was clothed with the light of the gospel sun, which had just risen." — DAR 510.2.

- **Song of Solomon 6:10** — "Fair as the moon, clear as the sun, and terrible as an army with banners." The bride is described in exactly these heavenly symbols — sun and moon — that crown the woman of Revelation 12.

  > "“The sun,” the light and glory of the gospel dispensation. … “The moon,” the Mosaic dispensation. As the moon shines with a borrowed light derived from the sun, so the former dispensation shone with a light borrowed from the present." — DAR 509.4-5.

- **Psalms 89:36-37** — "His seed shall endure for ever... It shall be established for ever as the moon, and as a faithful witness in heaven." Sun and moon are the standing prophetic emblems of God's enduring covenant and church.

  > "No symbols more fitting and impressive could be found than are here employed. The Mosaic dispensation shone with a light borrowed from the Christian dispensation, just as the moon shines with light borrowed from the sun." — DAR 510.2.

- **Revelation 12:1** (clause) — "a crown of twelve stars." The crown of twelve stars is the twelve apostles, the foundation of the New Testament church.

  > "“A crown of twelve stars,” the twelve apostles." — DAR 509.6.

  > "By the figure of prolepsis, the church is represented as fully organized, with its twelve apostles, before the man child, Christ, appeared upon the scene." — DAR 510.2.

- **Revelation 22:16** — "I am the root and the offspring of David, and the bright and morning star." The stars are her teachers and leaders, and Christ the chief Star — light reflected from the apostolic foundation.

  > "“A crown of twelve stars,” the twelve apostles." — DAR 509.6.

### The man child and the dragon — first advent and pagan Rome

- **Revelation 12:2** — "She being with child cried, travailing in birth, and pained to be delivered." Verses 1-2 cover the time just before the gospel dispensation, when the church longed and watched for the Messiah (Luke 2:25, 26, 38).

  > "Verses 1 and 2 cover a period of time commencing just previous to the opening of the present dispensation, when the church was earnestly longing for and expecting the advent of the Messiah, and extending to the time of the full establishment of the gospel church with its crown of twelve apostles. Luke 2:25, 26, 38." — DAR 510.1.

- **Revelation 12:3** — "A great red dragon, having seven heads and ten horns, and seven crowns upon his heads." The dragon is pagan Rome — the red dragon was the principal standard of the Roman legions, painted red.

  > "“A great red dragon,” pagan Rome. (See under verses 4 and 5.)" — DAR 509.7.

  > "And it may be a fact worth mentioning that during the second, third, fourth, and fifth centuries of the Christian era, next to the eagle the dragon was the principal standard of the Roman legions; and that dragon was painted red, as though, in faithful response to the picture held up by the seer of Patmos, they would exclaim to the world, We are the nation which that picture represents." — DAR 511.3-512.1.

- **Revelation 12:4** — "His tail drew the third part of the stars of heaven... and the dragon stood before the woman... for to devour her child as soon as it was born." A third part of the Jewish rulers — the kings — were swept away by Rome; and Rome, in Herod, sought to slay the new-born Christ.

  > "The Jews had three classes of rulers, — kings, priests, and the Sanhedrim. A third of these, the kings, were taken away by the Roman power." — DAR 510.4.

  > "No formal answer to this question need be given to any one who has read how Herod, in a fiendish effort to destroy the infant Jesus, sent forth and slew all the children in Bethlehem, from two years old and under. But who was Herod? — A Roman governor. From Rome, Herod derived his power." — DAR 511.3-512.1.

- **Revelation 12:5** — "She brought forth a man child, who was to rule all nations with a rod of iron: and her child was caught up unto God, and to his throne." The man child is Christ alone — none other was caught up to God's throne (Ps. 2:7-9; Heb. 8:1; Rev. 3:21).

  > "The testimony concerning the “man child” which the dragon seeks to destroy, is applicable to only one being that has appeared in this world, and that is our Lord Jesus Christ. No other one has been caught up to God and his throne; but he has been thus exalted. Ephesians 1:20, 21; Hebrews 8:1; Revelation 3:21." — DAR 511.1-2.

- **Genesis 3:15** — "I will put enmity between thee and the woman, and between thy seed and her seed; it shall bruise thy head." The first promise: the woman's Seed, Christ, who bruises the serpent — the war of Revelation 12 in germ.

  > "The dragon stood before the woman, to devour her child… The testimony concerning the “man child” which the dragon seeks to destroy, is applicable to only one being that has appeared in this world, and that is our Lord Jesus Christ." — DAR 511.1.

### War in heaven — Michael's victory at the first advent

- **Revelation 12:7-8** — "There was war in heaven: Michael and his angels fought against the dragon... And prevailed not; neither was their place found any more in heaven." Michael is Christ (Jude 9; 1 Thess. 4:16); the war is His personal contest with Satan at His first advent, in which Satan "prevailed not."

  > "And to what point are we carried back? — Evidently to the commencement of Christ’s ministry here upon earth. To prove that Michael is Christ, see Jude 9; 1 Thessalonians 4:16; John 5:28, 29; and that this was a special time of warfare between him and Satan need not be argued." — DAR 513.2.

  > "The war mentioned in the scripture now before us is between the Devil and Michael, Christ. The great effort of the former against the latter, personally, was during his mission here on earth; and Christ’s great personal victory over him was in that very contest." — DAR 515.1.

- **Revelation 12:9** — "The great dragon was cast out, that old serpent, called the Devil, and Satan... cast out into the earth." Here John names a second actor — Satan himself — distinct from pagan Rome; Satan is humiliated and cast down at the cross.

  > "Another symbol is here introduced, and John hastens to tell us what this symbol represents. It is the Devil and Satan. But this is not the same as the dragon of verses 3 and 4… It was because Rome, as a universal empire, was then the only possible general agent to carry out Satan’s will in the earth. But there is no occasion to confound the two symbols." — DAR 513.3-514.1.

- **Revelation 12:10** — "Now is come salvation, and strength, and the kingdom of our God... for the accuser of our brethren is cast down." The song is sung prospectively: Christ's victory made salvation forever sure.

  > "But this song was sung prospectively. These things were made sure. The great victory had been won by Christ which put the question of their establishment forever at rest." — DAR 516.1.

- **Revelation 12:11** — "They overcame him by the blood of the Lamb, and by the word of their testimony; and they loved not their lives unto the death." The faithful "brethren" overcome through all the ages by the Lamb's blood and their testimony — the martyr church.

  > "The prophet then glances rapidly over the working of Satan from that time to the end (verses 11, 12), during which time the faithful “brethren” overcome him by the blood of the Lamb and the word of their testimony, while his wrath increases as his time grows short." — DAR 516.2.

- **Revelation 12:12** — "Woe to the inhabiters of the earth and of the sea! for the devil is come down unto you, having great wrath, because he knoweth that he hath but a short time." Baffled and hopeless, Satan turns his rage upon the woman, the church.

  > "Henceforth the church (the woman) is the object of his malice, and he resorts to all those nefarious means against her that would naturally characterize a baffled and hopeless rage." — DAR 515.2.

  > "The prophet then glances rapidly over the working of Satan from that time to the end (verses 11, 12)… while his wrath increases as his time grows short." — DAR 516.2.

### The woman in the wilderness — 1260 years, 538-1798

- **Revelation 12:6** — "The woman fled into the wilderness... that they should feed her there a thousand two hundred and threescore days." The church fled when the papacy was established in 538, nourished by the word of God and the ministry of angels through 1260 years.

  > "And the church fled into the wilderness at the time the papacy was established, in 538, where it was nourished by the word of God and the ministration of angels during the long, dark, and bloody rule of that power, 1260 years." — DAR 512.2.

  > "The 1260 years of papal supremacy began in A.D. 538, and would therefore terminate in 1798." — GC 266.3.

- **Revelation 12:14** — "To the woman were given two wings of a great eagle, that she might fly into the wilderness... where she is nourished for a time, and times, and half a time." The eagles' wings are the haste and the help God gave her (Ex. 19:4); the "time, times, and half a time" equals the 1260 days.

  > "The eagles’ wings given her appropriately signify the haste with which the true church was obliged to provide for her own safety when the man of sin was installed in power, together with the assistance God provided her to this end. The like figure is used to describe God’s dealings with ancient Israel… “I bare you on eagles’ wings, and brought you unto myself.” Exodus 19:4." — DAR 517.2-3.

- **Daniel 7:25** — "They shall be given into his hand until a time and times and the dividing of time." The identical phrase fixes the little horn's persecution and the woman's wilderness to one and the same 1260 years of papal supremacy.

  > "The mention of the period during which the woman is nourished in the wilderness as “a time and times and half a time,” the exact phraseology used in Daniel 7:25, furnishes a key for the explanation of the latter passage… making in all 1260 days; and this, being symbolic, signifies 1260 literal years." — DAR 517.3.

  > "The forty and two months are the same as the “time and times and the dividing of time,” three years and a half, or 1260 days, of Daniel 7—the time during which the papal power was to oppress God’s people. This period… began with the supremacy of the papacy, A.D. 538, and terminated in 1798." — GC 439.2.

- **Revelation 13:5** — "Power was given unto him to continue forty and two months." Forty-two months = 1260 days = the time the church suffered oppression from Rome, A.D. 538 to 1798.

  > "The periods here mentioned—“forty and two months,” and “a thousand two hundred and threescore days”—are the same, alike representing the time in which the church of Christ was to suffer oppression from Rome. The 1260 years of papal supremacy began in A.D. 538, and would therefore terminate in 1798." — GC 266.3.

  > "“Power was given unto him to continue forty and two months.” … The forty and two months are the same as the “time and times and the dividing of time,” three years and a half, or 1260 days, of Daniel 7—the time during which the papal power was to oppress God’s people." — GC 439.2.

- **Numbers 14:34** — "Each day for a year." The year-day principle: each prophetic day stands for a literal year, turning 1260 days into 1260 years.

  > "This shows that a “time” is one year, 360 days; two “times,” two years, or 720 days; and “half a time,” half a year, or 180 days, making in all 1260 days; and this, being symbolic, signifies 1260 literal years." — DAR 517.3.

- **Ezekiel 4:6** — "I have appointed thee each day for a year." The second witness to the year-day rule by which the wilderness period is computed.

  > "This shows that a “time” is one year, 360 days; two “times,” two years, or 720 days; and “half a time,” half a year, or 180 days, making in all 1260 days; and this, being symbolic, signifies 1260 literal years." — DAR 517.3.

### The wilderness state, the flood, and the earth's help

- **Revelation 12:13** — "When the dragon saw that he was cast unto the earth, he persecuted the woman which brought forth the man child." Satan, defeated at the cross, turns with tenfold fury on the true church.

  > "Here we are again carried back to the time when Satan became fully aware that he had utterly failed in all his attempts against the Lord of glory in his earthly mission; and seeing this, he turned with tenfold fury, as already noticed, upon the church which Christ had established." — DAR 517.1.

- **Revelation 12:14** (clause) — "into the wilderness, into her place." The wilderness is seclusion and concealment: the true church hidden in caves and the valleys of the Piedmont (the Waldenses), God watching over and nourishing her.

  > "This must denote a state of seclusion from the public gaze, and of concealment from her foes… the caves and the hidden recesses of the valleys of the Piedmont may be taken as representative places, where the truth of the gospel was sacredly cherished from the rage of its foes. Here God watched over his church, and by his providence protected and nourished her." — DAR 517.1.

  > "Behind the lofty bulwarks of the mountains—in all ages the refuge of the persecuted and oppressed—the Waldenses found a hiding place. Here the light of truth was kept burning amid the darkness of the Middle Ages. Here, for a thousand years, witnesses for the truth maintained the ancient faith." — GC 65.3.

- **Revelation 12:15** — "The serpent cast out of his mouth water as a flood after the woman, that he might cause her to be carried away of the flood." The flood is the deluge of papal persecution — fifty to a hundred million carried away — yet the days were shortened for the elect's sake (Matt. 24:22).

  > "Through it Satan could hurl a mighty flood of persecution against the church in every direction; and this he was not slow to do… From fifty to one hundred million were carried away by the flood; but the church was not entirely swallowed up; the days were shortened for the elect’s sake. Matthew 24:22." — DAR 518.1.

- **Revelation 12:16** — "The earth helped the woman, and the earth opened her mouth, and swallowed up the flood." The Reformation: God raised up Luther and his colaborers; Protestant soil in Germany, Switzerland, Holland, England, and Scandinavia absorbed the flood of papal fury.

  > "“The earth helped the woman” by opening its mouth and swallowing up the flood. The Reformation of the sixteenth century began its work. God raised up the noble Luther and his colaborers… And soon there was enough Protestant soil found in Switzerland, Germany, Holland, England, Norway, and Sweden, to swallow up the flood of papal fury, and rob it of its power to harm the church." — DAR 518.2-519.

### The remnant of her seed — the link to the last-day church

- **Revelation 12:17** — "The dragon was wroth with the woman, and went to make war with the remnant of her seed, which keep the commandments of God, and have the testimony of Jesus Christ." The remnant is the last generation of the true church; the war is against those who keep all ten commandments — including the Sabbath — and hold the spirit of prophecy.

  > "Verse 17 brings to view another and a final outburst of his wrath, this time against the last generation of Christians to live on the earth… for the war of the dragon is directed against the remnant of the woman’s seed; that is, the remnant of the seed, or individuals, that constitute the true church; and no generation but the last can truthfully be represented by the remnant." — DAR 519.1-2.

  > "The line of prophecy in which these symbols are found begins with Revelation 12, with the dragon that sought to destroy Christ at His birth. The dragon is said to be Satan (Revelation 12:9); he it was that moved upon Herod to put the Saviour to death." — GC 438.

- **Revelation 14:12** — "Here is the patience of the saints: here are they that keep the commandments of God, and the faith of Jesus." The same commandment-keeping remnant, defined again under the third angel — the Sabbath reform of the last days.

  > "This remnant is characterized by their keeping of the commandments of God, and having the testimony of Jesus Christ. This points to a Sabbath reform to be accomplished in the last days… This is more particularly brought to view in the message of Revelation 14:9-12." — DAR 519.2.
