  > "Babylon is said to be 'the mother of harlots.' By her daughters must be symbolized churches that cling to her doctrines and traditions, and follow her example of sacrificing the truth and the approval of God, in order to form an unlawful alliance with the world." — GC 382-383.

- **Hosea 2:2-5** — "Plead with your mother... for she is not my wife... for she said, I will go after my lovers." Spiritual harlotry = chasing other gods and the world's favor instead of the Lord.

  > "In the New Testament, language very similar is addressed to professed Christians who seek the friendship of the world above the favor of God. Says the apostle James: 'Ye adulterers and adulteresses, know ye not that the friendship of the world is enmity with God?'" — GC 382.

### The Vision of the Woman on the Beast (Revelation 17:1-6)

- **Revelation 17:1-2** — "The judgment of the great whore that sitteth upon many waters: with whom the kings of the earth have committed fornication, and the inhabitants of the earth have been made drunk with the wine of her fornication." The illicit union of church and state, and the world intoxicated with her false doctrines.

  > "Between this church and the kings of the earth there has been illicit connection, and with the wine of her fornication, or her false doctrines, the inhabitants of the earth have been made drunk." — DAR 657.

- **Revelation 17:3** — "I saw a woman sit upon a scarlet coloured beast, full of names of blasphemy, having seven heads and ten horns." The church (woman) seated upon and controlling the civil power (beast) — the prophecy "distinguishes between church and state." She rides and guides it "to her own ends."

  > "This prophecy is more definite than others applicable to the Roman power, in that it distinguishes between church and state. We here have the woman, the church, seated upon a scarlet-colored beast, the civil power, by which she is upheld, and which she controls and guides to her own ends, as a rider controls the animal upon which he is seated." — DAR 657.3.

- **Revelation 17:4** — "Arrayed in purple and scarlet colour, and decked with gold and precious stones and pearls, having a golden cup in her hand full of abominations." Purple and scarlet are "the chief colors in the robes of popes and cardinals"; the golden cup — symbol of pure doctrine — pours out only abominations.

  > "Purple and scarlet are the chief colors in the robes of popes and cardinals.… And from the golden cup in her hand, — symbol of purity of doctrine and profession, which should have contained only that which is unadulterated and pure, … — there came forth only abominations, and wine of her fornication." — DAR 658.1.

- **Revelation 17:5** — "Upon her forehead a name written, MYSTERY, BABYLON THE GREAT, THE MOTHER OF HARLOTS AND ABOMINATIONS OF THE EARTH." Her own brand identifies her: not merely a harlot, but the mother — proof of apostate daughters belonging "to the same great family."

  > "This woman is explicitly called Babylon.… she is called the mother of harlots, as already noticed, which shows that there are other independent religious organizations that constitute the apostate daughters, and belong to the same great family." — DAR 658.2.

- **Revelation 17:6** — "I saw the woman drunken with the blood of the saints, and with the blood of the martyrs of Jesus." John wondered because this persecutor was no longer pagan Rome but a power professing the name of Christ — the church drunk on the blood of the faithful.

  > "It was not strange that pagans should persecute Christ's followers; but when he looked forward, and saw a church professedly Christian persecuting the followers of the Lamb, and drunken with their blood, he could but wonder with great amazement." — DAR 658.3-4.

  > "And no other power could be so truly declared 'drunken with the blood of the saints' as that church which has so cruelly persecuted the followers of Christ." — GC 382.

### Seven Heads = Seven Mountains = Rome (Revelation 17:7-11)

- **Revelation 17:9** — "Here is the mind which hath wisdom. The seven heads are seven mountains, on which the woman sitteth." The seven heads are first explained as seven mountains, then as seven kings — "forms of government." The seat is Rome.

  > "The seven heads are explained to be, first, seven mountains, and then seven kings, or forms of government.… The seven forms of government that have existed in the Roman empire are usually enumerated as follows: (1) kingly; (2) consular; (3) decemvirate; (4) dictatorial; (5) triumvirate; (6) imperial; and (7) papal." — DAR 659.3.

- **Revelation 17:10** — "There are seven kings: five are fallen, and one is, and the other is not yet come." The heads are successive in time — not literal hills, which are not successive. So they denote the successive ruling forms of the Roman power.

  > "'Five are fallen,' says the angel, or passed away; 'one is;' the sixth was then reigning; another was to come, and continue for a short space.… Kings, consuls, decemvirs, dictators, and triumvirs had passed away in John's day. He was living under the imperial form." — DAR 659.3.

  > "The seven heads are evidently successive in order of time; for we read, 'Five are fallen, and one is, and the other is not yet come.' Revelation 17. But the seven hills on which Rome is built, are not successive, and it would be absurd to apply such language to them." — DAR 602.3-5.

- **Revelation 17:11** — "The beast that was, and is not, even he is the eighth, and is of the seven, and goeth into perdition." Rome viewed "in three phases" — the same persecuting power reappearing "in its bloody and blasphemous form," doomed to perdition.

  > "Thus Rome in its pagan form was a persecuting power in its relation to the people of God, during which time it constituted the beast that was.… Time passed on, and it degenerated into popery, and again assumed its bloodthirsty and oppressive character, and then it constituted the beast that 'yet is.'" — DAR 659.2-3.

- **Revelation 13:1** — The kindred beast rises "having seven heads and ten horns" with "the name of blasphemy" — the same Roman power, the seven heads marking it across history. The wounded head "cannot be a literal mountain; for it would be folly to speak of wounding a mountain to death."

  > "We are informed in chapter 13 that one of the seven heads was wounded to death. This head therefore cannot be a literal mountain; for it would be folly to speak of wounding a mountain to death." — DAR 602.3.

  > "The head that was wounded to death was the papal head.… whatever is said of the dragon applies to Rome only in its pagan form, and whatever is said of the leopard beast applies to Rome only in its professedly Christian form." — DAR 525.3.

- **Daniel 7:23** — "The fourth beast shall be the fourth kingdom upon earth... and shall devour the whole earth." Rome — the fourth world-kingdom — is the beast the harlot rides; the prophets agree on the empire that carries her.

  > "In the explanation in verse 23, the angel said, 'The fourth beast shall be the fourth kingdom upon the earth.' These beast are therefore symbols of four great kingdoms." — DAR 113.

### The Waters, the Horns, and the Great City (Revelation 17:12-18)

- **Revelation 17:12-13** — "The ten horns... are ten kings... these have one mind, and shall give their power and strength unto the beast." The kingdoms of Europe were once "unanimous in giving their support to the papacy."

  > "This language must refer to the past, when the kingdoms of Europe were unanimous in giving their support to the papacy, and upholding it in all its pretensions." — DAR 661.1-2.

- **Revelation 17:14** — "These shall make war with the Lamb, and the Lamb shall overcome them: for he is Lord of lords, and King of kings." The final battle — the title "King of kings" belongs to the second coming.

  > "Here we are carried into the future, to the time of the great and final battle; for at this time the Lamb has assumed the title of King of kings and Lord of lords, a title which he does not assume till his second coming." — DAR 661.3.

- **Revelation 17:15** — "The waters which thou sawest, where the whore sitteth, are peoples, and multitudes, and nations, and tongues." Scripture defines its own symbol: the "many waters" of verse 1 are the multitudes over whom Babylon reigns.

  > "In verse 15 we have a plain definition of the Scripture symbol of waters; they denote peoples, multitudes, nations, and tongues." — DAR 662.1.

- **Revelation 17:16** — "The ten horns... these shall hate the whore, and shall make her desolate and naked... and burn her with fire." The same civil powers that upheld her at last turn and destroy her — her judgment.

  > "The angel told John, while calling his attention to this subject, that he would show him the judgment of this great harlot. In verse 16 that judgment is specified." — DAR 662.1.

- **Revelation 17:18** — "The woman which thou sawest is that great city, which reigneth over the kings of the earth." The woman = the city = "the great Roman power" — papal Rome ruling over the kings of the earth.

  > "What city? (See chapter 17:18: 'And the woman which thou sawest is that great city which reigneth over the kings [kingdoms] of the earth.') That city is the papal Roman power." — DAR 504.2.

  > "'The seven heads are seven mountains,' and 'The woman is that great city;' and it will not be difficult to show that the mountains and the city are both used symbolically." — DAR 602.2.

### Babylon: Her Name, Her Cup, Her Fall (Revelation 14:8; 18:1-5)

- **Genesis 11:9** — "Therefore is the name of it called Babel; because the LORD did there confound the language." "Babylon" is derived from "Babel" and "signifies confusion" — the fit name for apostate, sect-divided religion.

  > "The term 'Babylon' is derived from 'Babel,' and signifies confusion. It is employed in Scripture to designate the various forms of false or apostate religion." — GC 381.

- **Jeremiah 51:7** — "Babylon hath been a golden cup in the LORD's hand, that made all the earth drunken: the nations have drunken of her wine." The golden cup and the intoxicating wine of Revelation 17 are drawn straight from ancient Babylon — the type of the spiritual harlot.

  > "And from the golden cup in her hand, — symbol of purity of doctrine and profession, which should have contained only that which is unadulterated and pure, … — there came forth only abominations, and wine of her fornication, fit symbol of her abominable doctrines, and still more abominable practices." — DAR 657-658.

- **Revelation 14:8** — "Babylon is fallen, is fallen, that great city, because she made all nations drink of the wine of the wrath of her fornication." The second angel's message: a moral fall — the spreading of false doctrine to all nations.

  > "In Revelation 14 the first angel is followed by a second proclaiming: 'Babylon is fallen, is fallen, that great city, because she made all nations drink of the wine of the wrath of her fornication.' Revelation 14:8." — GC 381.

  > "In chapter 14 we had a message announcing the fall of Babylon. Babylon is a term which embraces not only the Roman Catholic Church, but religious bodies which have sprung from her, bringing many of her errors and traditions along with them." — DAR 663.2.

- **Revelation 16:19** — "Great Babylon came in remembrance before God, to give unto her the cup of the wine of the fierceness of his wrath." The cup she filled for others is at last returned to her in judgment.

  > "In verse 19 of the preceding chapter, we were informed that 'great Babylon came in remembrance before God, to give unto her the cup of the wine of the fierceness of his wrath.' The prophet now takes up more particularly the subject of this great Babylon." — DAR 657.2.

- **Revelation 18:1-3** — "Another angel come down from heaven, having great power; and the earth was lightened with his glory... Babylon the great is fallen, is fallen, and is become the habitation of devils." The loud cry — the final, fuller proclamation of the fall, now embracing all apostate religion.

  > "SOME movement of mighty power is symbolized in these verses.… Babylon is a term which embraces not only the Roman Catholic Church, but religious bodies which have sprung from her, bringing many of her errors and traditions along with them." — DAR 663.1-3.

- **Revelation 18:4** — "Come out of her, my people, that ye be not partakers of her sins, and that ye receive not of her plagues." God still has His people within Babylon; they are called out before the plagues fall.

  > "The people of God are there after her fall, and are called out in order that they may not receive of her plagues; and in these plagues is embraced her literal destruction." — DAR 663.3.

  > "Many of the Protestant churches are following Rome's example of iniquitous connection with 'the kings of the earth'… And the term 'Babylon'—confusion—may be appropriately applied to these bodies." — GC 383.

- **Revelation 18:5** — "For her sins have reached unto heaven, and God hath remembered her iniquities." The cup of her guilt is full; judgment is sealed.

  > "As a consequence of her fall, she piles up an accumulation of sins even to the heavens, and becomes subject to the judgments of God, which can no longer be delayed." — DAR 663.

- **Revelation 18:24** — "In her was found the blood of prophets, and of saints, and of all that were slain upon the earth." The persecuting harlot, drunk with martyr-blood, is held to full account.

  > "All the persecution he had witnessed had been from pagan Rome, the open enemy of Christ.… but when he looked forward, and saw a church professedly Christian persecuting the followers of the Lamb, and drunken with their blood, he could but wonder with great amazement." — DAR 658.4.

- **Revelation 19:2** — "He hath judged the great whore, which did corrupt the earth with her fornication, and hath avenged the blood of his servants at her hand." Heaven's verdict and vindication of the saints she slew.

  > "The apostle here introduces the song of triumph which the redeemed saints strike up on victor harps, when they behold the complete destruction of that great system of opposition to God and his true worship comprehended in great Babylon." — DAR 680.

### The Mother and Her Daughters — Fallen Protestantism

- **Ezekiel 23:4** — "Aholah the elder, and Aholibah her sister... Samaria is Aholah, and Jerusalem Aholibah." The two-sister figure: a mother-apostasy reproduced in her offspring — the prototype of Babylon and her harlot daughters.

  > "Babylon is said to be 'the mother of harlots.' By her daughters must be symbolized churches that cling to her doctrines and traditions, and follow her example of sacrificing the truth and the approval of God, in order to form an unlawful alliance with the world." — GC 382-383.

- **Revelation 17:5** — Babylon is named "THE MOTHER OF HARLOTS." She is not Rome to the exclusion of all others; the title "mother" proves "there are other independent religious organizations that constitute the apostate daughters, and belong to the same great family."

  > "Is Rome, then, Babylon, to the exclusion of all other religious bodies? — No, from the fact that she is called the mother of harlots, as already noticed, which shows that there are other independent religious organizations that constitute the apostate daughters, and belong to the same great family." — DAR 658.2.

- **Revelation 14:8 with 18:2** — "Babylon is fallen, is fallen." By her daughters "must be symbolized churches that cling to her doctrines and traditions" — Protestant bodies "once pure" that "have become corrupt," forming "iniquitous connection with the kings of the earth" or seeking "the favor of the world."

  > "The message of Revelation 14, announcing the fall of Babylon must apply to religious bodies that were once pure and have become corrupt.… Many of the Protestant churches are following Rome's example of iniquitous connection with 'the kings of the earth'… and other denominations, by seeking the favor of the world." — GC 382-383.

  > "Babylon is a term which embraces not only the Roman Catholic Church, but religious bodies which have sprung from her, bringing many of her errors and traditions along with them." — DAR 663.2.

---

_Symbol-key summary: a woman = a church (pure: 2 Cor. 11:2; Rev. 12:1 — corrupt: Isa. 1:21; Jer. 3:6-9). Fornication = spiritual idolatry / unlawful church-state union (Rev. 17:2; Jas. 4:4). The scarlet beast = the civil power the church rides and controls (Rev. 17:3; DAR 657.3). Seven heads = seven mountains = successive forms of Roman government; the seat is Rome (Rev. 17:9-10; DAR 659.3). "That great city" = papal Rome reigning over the kings of the earth (Rev. 17:18; DAR 504.2). Babylon = Babel = confusion = apostate religion — mother (Catholic Rome) and daughters (fallen Protestantism) — whose fall is announced in Rev. 14:8 and proclaimed with power in Rev. 18:1-5, with God's people called out before the plagues. (DAR 657-663; GC 381-383.)_

# Part IV — Sanctuary & Consummation

---

# 20. The Sanctuary, the Cleansing, and the Investigative Judgment (1844)

> _The disappointment of 1844 was not the failure of prophecy but the failure of an interpretation. The 2300 days ended exactly on time, but the sanctuary to be cleansed was never the earth — it was the true tabernacle in heaven, of which Moses' was the pattern (Heb. 8-9). As the earthly high priest cleansed the sanctuary on the tenth day of the seventh month from the recorded sins of Israel (Lev. 16), so at the close of the 2300 days our High Priest passed into the most holy place to begin the antitypical Day of Atonement — the investigative judgment. This corrected the two errors of the Adventists: not the earth but the heavenly sanctuary; not the second advent but the opening of the judgment. (DAR 145-157; GC 409-432, 479-491; EW 250-253.)_

### The True Sanctuary Is in Heaven — the Earthly Was Its Pattern

- **Exodus 25:8-9** — "Let them make me a sanctuary; that I may dwell among them... according to all that I shew thee, after the pattern." The earthly tabernacle was built to a heavenly pattern shown Moses in the mount; therefore the original it copied exists above.

  > "The sanctuary in heaven, in which Jesus ministers in our behalf, is the great original, of which the sanctuary built by Moses was a copy." — GC 414.

  > "It consists, first, of the earthly sanctuary as embodied in the tabernacle of Moses... and, secondly, of the more perfect tabernacle, temple, and sanctuary in Heaven, which the Lord pitched, and not man, and which took the place of the former when the typical dispensation gave place to the true." — STTHD 197.

- **Hebrews 8:1-2** — We have "an high priest... a minister of the sanctuary, and of the true tabernacle, which the Lord pitched, and not man." Christ ministers in the genuine sanctuary in heaven, not a building made with hands.

  > "Here is revealed the sanctuary of the new covenant. The sanctuary of the first covenant was pitched by man, built by Moses; this is pitched by the Lord, not by man... One sanctuary was on earth, the other is in heaven." — GC 413-414.

  > "...the more perfect tabernacle, temple, and sanctuary in Heaven, which the Lord pitched, and not man, and which took the place of the former when the typical dispensation gave place to the true." — STTHD 197.1.

- **Hebrews 8:5** — The earthly priests "serve unto the example and shadow of heavenly things," Moses being charged to "make all things according to the pattern shewed to thee in the mount." The shadow proves the substance; the type proves the antitype.

  > "Moses made the earthly sanctuary after a pattern which was shown him. Paul teaches that that pattern was the true sanctuary which is in heaven. And John testifies that he saw it in heaven." — GC 415.

  > "With the earthly sanctuary there was connected a ministration, which Paul says served unto the example and shadow of heavenly things. By the ministration therefore of the earthly sanctuary, is shadowed forth the ministration of the heavenly Sanctuary." — TTHDS 15.2.

- **Hebrews 9:1-5** — The first covenant had "a worldly sanctuary" — the candlestick, table, and shewbread in the first apartment ("the sanctuary"); within the second veil "the Holiest of all," the ark and the mercyseat. Scripture describes the two apartments to teach the structure of the heavenly.

  > "...they found in the Bible a full explanation of the subject of the sanctuary, its nature, location, and services; the testimony of the sacred writers being so clear and ample as to place the matter beyond all question." — GC 411-412.

  > "I was then bidden to take notice of the two apartments of the heavenly sanctuary... In the first apartment I saw the candlestick with seven lamps, the table of shewbread, the altar of incense, and the censer... I looked into the second apartment. I saw there an ark... In the ark were tables of stone containing the ten commandments." — EW 251.3.

- **Hebrews 9:11-12** — "Christ being come an high priest... by a greater and more perfect tabernacle, not made with hands... by his own blood he entered in once into the holy place, having obtained eternal redemption for us." The greater tabernacle is the heavenly sanctuary where Christ now officiates.

  > "The matchless splendor of the earthly tabernacle reflected to human vision the glories of that heavenly temple where Christ our forerunner ministers for us before the throne of God." — GC 414.

  > "...the more perfect tabernacle, temple, and sanctuary in Heaven, which the Lord pitched, and not man, and which took the place of the former when the typical dispensation gave place to the true." — STTHD 197.1.

- **Hebrews 9:24** — "Christ is not entered into the holy places made with hands... but into heaven itself, now to appear in the presence of God for us." The place of His ministry is heaven itself.

  > "The matchless splendor of the earthly tabernacle reflected to human vision the glories of that heavenly temple where Christ our forerunner ministers for us before the throne of God... that temple, filled with the glory of the eternal throne." — GC 414.

### The Two-Apartment Priestly Ministry

- **Hebrews 9:6-7** — The priests went "always into the first tabernacle," but "into the second went the high priest alone once every year, not without blood... for the errors of the people." Two apartments, two phases of ministry: the continual, and the yearly cleansing.

  > "The ministration of the earthly sanctuary consisted of two divisions; the priests ministered daily in the holy place, while once a year the high priest performed a special work of atonement in the most holy, for the cleansing of the sanctuary." — GC 418.

  > "The 10th day of the 7th month every year, the holy sanctuary is cleansed... This is the day of the sprinkling of blood on the mercy seat and seven times before it... This is the day of affliction for all Israel." — BP3 130.3.

- **Hebrews 9:8** — The Holy Ghost signified "that the way into the holiest of all was not yet made manifest, while as the first tabernacle was yet standing." The most holy ministry opens only after the first-apartment work closes — the very transition of 1844.

  > "As Jesus ended His ministration in the holy place and closed the door of that apartment... When fully attired, He was surrounded by angels, and in a flaming chariot He passed within the second veil." — EW 251.2.

- **Hebrews 4:14-16** — "We have a great high priest, that is passed into the heavens, Jesus the Son of God"; therefore "come boldly unto the throne of grace." Christ's heavenly priesthood is the believer's living access.

  > "Jesus has opened the way to the Father's throne, and through His mediation the sincere desire of all who come to Him in faith may be presented before God." — GC 489.

- **Hebrews 7:25** — He "is able also to save them to the uttermost that come unto God by him, seeing he ever liveth to make intercession for them." The first-apartment ministry of intercession ran for eighteen centuries.

  > "For eighteen centuries this work of ministration continued in the first apartment of the sanctuary. The blood of Christ, pleaded in behalf of penitent believers, secured their pardon and acceptance with the Father, yet their sins still remained upon the books of record." — GC 421.2.

- **Hebrews 6:19-20** — Our hope "entereth into that within the veil; whither the forerunner is for us entered, even Jesus, made an high priest for ever." Christ as forerunner went within the veil — quoted by GC to fix the place of His ministration.

  > "Here their hopes centered, 'which hope we have,' said Paul, 'as an anchor of the soul, both sure and steadfast, and which entereth into that within the veil; whither the forerunner is for us entered, even Jesus, made an high priest forever.'" — GC 421.1.

- **Hebrews 10:19-22** — We have "boldness to enter into the holiest by the blood of Jesus... through the veil, that is to say, his flesh." The blood that opens the holiest is His own.

  > "We must by faith enter within the veil, 'whither the forerunner is for us entered.' Hebrews 6:20. There the light from the cross of Calvary is reflected." — GC 489.

  > "The sins of all those who come to Christ for salvation and pardon, are through his blood transferred to the Sanctuary... the heavenly things themselves (the True Tabernacle in heaven) with better sacrifices than these, that is, with the blood of Christ." — TTHDS 21.3.

### The Day of Atonement (Leviticus 16) Is the Type of the Cleansing

- **Leviticus 16:2** — Aaron must "come not at all times into the holy place within the vail before the mercy seat... that he die not." The most holy place was entered on one day only — the type of one closing work.

  > "The 10th day of the 7th month every year, the holy sanctuary is cleansed... This is the day of the sprinkling of blood on the mercy seat and seven times before it, and seven times on the Golden Altar." — BP3 130.3.

  > "We there see the high priest on the tenth day of the seventh month entering in within the vail into the most holy place, to make an atonement for the people and cleanse the sanctuary. Leviticus 16." — TTHDS 21.1.

- **Leviticus 16:15-16** — The high priest brought the goat's blood "within the vail... and sprinkle it upon the mercy seat," making "an atonement for the holy place, because of the uncleanness of the children of Israel... in all their sins." The sanctuary was cleansed of the recorded sins borne into it through the year.

  > "Then kill the goat whose lot falls to the Lord... and with his blood enters the holy again and sprinkles his blood upon the mercy seat and seven times before it... to cleanse it because of their transgression." — BP3 130.4.

  > "It is accomplished by blood, not by fire. It is a part of Christ's work as priest, not as king. It is the ministration performed in the most holy place to complete the round of service, and end the work." — STTHD 249.1.

  > "The sins of Israel were thus transferred to the sanctuary, and a special work became necessary for their removal. God commanded that an atonement be made for each of the sacred apartments." — GC 418-419.

- **Leviticus 16:18-19** — He sprinkled the blood "seven times, and cleanse it, and hallow it from the uncleanness of the children of Israel." The act is expressly called a cleansing — accomplished by blood, not by fire.

  > "He also sprinkles the blood of the bullock, and the goat seven times upon the Golden Altar and upon the horns thereof, to cleanse it because of their transgression." — BP3 130.4.

  > "It is accomplished by blood, not by fire. It is a part of Christ's work as priest, not as king." — STTHD 249.1.

- **Leviticus 16:29-30** — "In the seventh month, on the tenth day of the month, ye shall afflict your souls... For on that day shall the priest make an atonement for you, to cleanse you, that ye may be clean from all your sins." A fixed day of soul-searching and final cleansing for Israel.

  > "Thus the daily atonement for individual sins goes on throughout the year, until the 10th day of the 7th month, then the trumpets sound to call the people to their... This is the day of affliction for all Israel, and is to be a Sabbath or rest, from even to even." — BP3 130.1-130.3.

  > "We there see the high priest on the tenth day of the seventh month entering in within the vail into the most holy place, to make an atonement for the people and cleanse the sanctuary." — TTHDS 21.1.

- **Leviticus 16:33-34** — "He shall make an atonement for the holy sanctuary... for all the people... to make an atonement for the children of Israel for all their sins once a year." The closing service of the yearly round — the last work of the priest.

  > "It is the ministration performed in the most holy place to complete the round of service, and end the work." — STTHD 249.1.

  > "In the typical system, which was a shadow of the sacrifice and priesthood of Christ, the cleansing of the sanctuary was the last service performed by the high priest in the yearly round of ministration. It was the closing work of the atonement—a removal or putting away of sin from Israel." — GC 352.2.

- **Leviticus 23:27-29** — "On the tenth day of this seventh month there shall be a day of atonement... ye shall afflict your souls"; whoever "shall not be afflicted in that same day... shall be cut off." Probation closed for any who neglected the day — the type of the close of probation in the judgment.

  > "In the typical service, while the high priest was making the atonement for Israel, all were required to afflict their souls by repentance of sin and humiliation before the Lord, lest they be cut off from among the people. In like manner, all who would have their names retained in the book of life should now... afflict their souls before God." — GC 489-490.

  > "The following will explain the Convocation day: Leviticus 16:17, 33... day of affliction 29 v., rest day or Sabbath, and when to begin it, 32." — BP3 130.4.

### What the Heavenly Cleansing Removes — Sin from the Record

- **Hebrews 9:22-23** — "Almost all things are by the law purged with blood... It was therefore necessary that the patterns of things in the heavens should be purified with these; but the heavenly things themselves with better sacrifices than these." Both sanctuaries are cleansed; the heavenly with Christ's blood.

  > "In Hebrews 9 the cleansing of both the earthly and the heavenly sanctuary is plainly taught... 'It was therefore necessary that the patterns of things in the heavens should be purified with these [the blood of animals]; but the heavenly things themselves with better sacrifices than these'... even the precious blood of Christ." — GC 417.

  > "the patterns of things in the heavens (the earthly holy places) were cleansed with the blood of goats and calves, but the heavenly things themselves (the True Tabernacle in heaven) with better sacrifices than these, that is, with the blood of Christ; and Paul here plainly states that both were cleansed for one and the same reason." — TTHDS 21.3.

- **Hebrews 9:26** — "Now once in the end of the world hath he appeared to put away sin by the sacrifice of himself." The putting-away of sin is the work — the heavenly sanctuary holds the recorded sins to be blotted out.

  > "...so the actual cleansing of the heavenly is to be accomplished by the removal, or blotting out, of the sins which are there recorded. But before this can be accomplished, there must be an examination of the books of record." — GC 421.

  > "This, then, is what was to take place at the end of the 2300 days, when the sanctuary was to be cleansed." — STTHD 249.

- **Hebrews 9:27-28** — "As it is appointed unto men once to die, but after this the judgment: so Christ was once offered... and unto them that look for him shall he appear the second time without sin unto salvation." Judgment precedes the second appearing; the cleansing comes before the coming.

  > "The cleansing of the sanctuary therefore involves a work of investigation—a work of judgment. This work must be performed prior to the coming of Christ to redeem His people; for when He comes, His reward is with Him to give to every man according to his works." — GC 421-422.

  > "It prefigured the closing work in the ministration of our High Priest in heaven, in the removal or blotting out of the sins of His people... This service involves a work of investigation, a work of judgment; and it immediately precedes the coming of Christ in the clouds of heaven." — GC 352.2.

- **Acts 3:19-21** — "Repent... that your sins may be blotted out, when the times of refreshing shall come"; the heaven must receive Christ "until the times of restitution." The blotting out of sins is a future, definite work tied to Christ's return — not the moment of pardon.

  > "The work of the investigative judgment and the blotting out of sins is to be accomplished before the second advent of the Lord... the apostle Peter distinctly states that the sins of believers will be blotted out 'when the times of refreshing shall come from the presence of the Lord; and He shall send Jesus Christ.' Acts 3:19, 20." — GC 485.

- **Malachi 3:1-3** — "The Lord, whom ye seek, shall suddenly come to his temple"; He sits "as a refiner and purifier of silver... that they may offer unto the Lord an offering in righteousness." The Lord's coming to His temple is His entry into the most holy to purify His people — the same event as Daniel 8:14.

  > "This coming is foretold also by the prophet Malachi: 'The Lord, whom ye seek, shall suddenly come to His temple...' The coming of the Lord to His temple was sudden, unexpected, to His people. They were not looking for Him there." — GC 424.

  > "The coming of Christ as our high priest to the most holy place, for the cleansing of the sanctuary, brought to view in Daniel 8:14; the coming of the Son of man to the Ancient of Days, as presented in Daniel 7:13; and the coming of the Lord to His temple, foretold by Malachi, are descriptions of the same event." — GC 426.1.

### Daniel 8:14 — the 2300 Days End, the Sanctuary Is Cleansed (1844)

- **Daniel 8:13** — "How long shall be the vision concerning the daily... to give both the sanctuary and the host to be trodden under foot?" The question that frames the answer: how long until the sanctuary is vindicated.

  > "These two verses close the vision proper of chapter 8; and they introduce the one remaining point which of all others would naturally be of the most absorbing interest to the prophet and to all the church; namely, the time the desolating powers previously brought to view were to continue." — DAR 154-157.

  > "It consists, first, of the earthly sanctuary... and, secondly, of the more perfect tabernacle, temple, and sanctuary in Heaven, which the Lord pitched, and not man." — STTHD 197.

- **Daniel 8:14** — "Unto two thousand and three hundred days; then shall the sanctuary be cleansed." On the year-day principle (Num. 14:34; Eze. 4:6) the 2300 years, dated from 457 B.C., reach to A.D. 1844; and since no earthly sanctuary then stood, the sanctuary cleansed is the heavenly.

  > "There remain 1810 years, extending far down into this dispensation, even to 1844. There the 2300 days terminated, and there we come to the cleansing of the sanctuary, the sanctuary, of course, of this dispensation." — STTHD 197.2.

  > "At the termination of the 2300 days, in 1844, there had been no sanctuary on earth for many centuries. Thus the prophecy, 'Unto two thousand and three hundred days; then shall the sanctuary be cleansed,' unquestionably points to the sanctuary in heaven." — GC 326-329, 417.1.

  > "This, then, is what was to take place at the end of the 2300 days, when the sanctuary was to be cleansed... there is no sanctuary now in Palestine. There was none there when the 2300 days ended, in 1844. And how can a sanctuary be cleansed that does not exist?" — STTHD 249.2.

- **Daniel 9:24** — "Seventy weeks are determined" — cut off — upon thy people; the 490 years are the first allotment of the 2300, dating both periods from 457 B.C. and fixing 1844 as the terminus.

  > "The word here rendered determined signifies 'cut off;' and there is no period from which the seventy weeks could be cut off but the 2300 days of the previous vision... 'Seventy weeks are cut off.' Cut off from what? — The 2300 days, most assuredly." — DAR 192.

  > "Seventy weeks, representing 490 years, are declared by the angel to be cut off, as specially pertaining to the Jews... As the 2300 days was the only period of time mentioned in chapter 8, it must be the period from which the seventy weeks were cut off." — GC 326-327, 410.

- **Daniel 7:13-14** — "One like the Son of man came... to the Ancient of days... and there was given him dominion, and glory, and a kingdom." Christ's approach to the Ancient of Days at the judgment is the same event as His coming to the temple in Daniel 8 and Malachi 3.

  > "The coming of Christ as our high priest to the most holy place, for the cleansing of the sanctuary, brought to view in Daniel 8:14; the coming of the Son of man to the Ancient of Days, as presented in Daniel 7:13; and the coming of the Lord to His temple, foretold by Malachi, are descriptions of the same event." — GC 426.1.

  > "In 1844, the judgment of verse 10 began to sit; namely, the investigative Judgment, in the heavenly sanctuary, preparatory to the coming of Christ." — DAR 144.

### Daniel 7:9-10 and Revelation 11:19 — the Judgment Scene Opened in the Most Holy

- **Daniel 7:9-10** — "The Ancient of days did sit... the judgment was set, and the books were opened." The investigative judgment is a courtroom scene in heaven, its verdict drawn from the books of record.

  > "Thus was presented to the prophet's vision the great and solemn day when the characters and the lives of men should pass in review before the Judge of all the earth... It is He, the source of all being, and the fountain of all law, that is to preside in the judgment." — GC 479.

  > "In 1844, the judgment of verse 10 began to sit; namely, the investigative Judgment, in the heavenly sanctuary, preparatory to the coming of Christ." — DAR 144.

- **Revelation 11:19** — "The temple of God was opened in heaven, and there was seen in his temple the ark of his testament." The opening of the most holy place — where the ark and the law are — depicts the very transition of 1844, when the inner apartment ministry began.

  > "Therefore the announcement that the temple of God was opened in heaven and the ark of His testament was seen points to the opening of the most holy place of the heavenly sanctuary in 1844 as Christ entered there to perform the closing work of the atonement." — GC 433.

  > "I was then bidden to take notice of the two apartments of the heavenly sanctuary... The veil was lifted, and I looked into the second apartment. I saw there an ark... In the ark were tables of stone containing the ten commandments." — EW 251.3.

  > "We know it is the holy of holies that is here opened; for the ark is seen; and in that apartment alone the ark was deposited. This took place at the end of the 2300 days, when the sanctuary was to be cleansed... when the seventh angel commenced to sound." — DAR 507.

- **Revelation 14:7** — "Fear God, and give glory to him; for the hour of his judgment is come... worship him that made heaven, and earth." The first angel's message announces the opening of the investigative judgment in 1844 — the right message at the right time.

  > "It is this work of judgment, immediately preceding the second advent, that is announced in the first angel's message of Revelation 14:7: 'Fear God, and give glory to Him; for the hour of His judgment is come.'" — GC 352.2.

  > "It proclaims the hour of God's Judgment come. But the Judgment pertains to the closing up of the work of salvation for the world; and the proclamation announcing its approach can therefore be made only as we come near the end." — DAR 590.

### The Two Errors Corrected — and the Doctrine the Disappointment Uncovered

- **Daniel 8:14 (the sanctuary)** — Miller, holding the "popular view," made the earth the sanctuary and its cleansing the purification of the earth by fire at the advent; his error was "accepting the popular view as to what constitutes the sanctuary." Error one: not the earth, but the heavenly sanctuary.

  > "Miller, as has been stated, adopted the generally received view that the earth is the sanctuary, and he believed that the cleansing of the sanctuary represented the purification of the earth by fire at the coming of the Lord... His error resulted from accepting the popular view as to what constitutes the sanctuary." — GC 352.1.

  > "The sanctuary is the earth, or at least some portion of the earth. Its cleansing is to be by fire. But the renovation of the earth by fire is to take place only at the second coming of the Lord... The point of time at length came; but the Lord did not... What was the matter? Where had the mistake been made?" — STTHD 19.3.

- **Daniel 8:14 (the event)** — The cleansing was "the closing work of the atonement — a removal or putting away of sin," involving "a work of investigation, a work of judgment," immediately preceding the advent — not the advent itself. Error two: not the second coming, but the opening of the judgment.

  > "It was the closing work of the atonement—a removal or putting away of sin from Israel... This service involves a work of investigation, a work of judgment; and it immediately precedes the coming of Christ in the clouds of heaven with power and great glory; for when He comes, every case has been decided." — GC 352.2.

  > "As in the typical service there was a work of atonement at the close of the year, so before Christ's work for the redemption of men is completed there is a work of atonement for the removal of sin from the sanctuary. This is the service which began when the 2300 days ended." — GC 421.2-422.1.

- **Revelation 3:7-8 (EW)** — Christ "had shut the door of the holy place... and had opened the door into the most holy, which He opened in 1844, where the ark is." The shut door and the open door are the change of apartments at the end of the 2300 days.

  > "I saw that Jesus had shut the door of the holy place, and no man can open it; and that He had opened the door into the most holy, and no man can shut it (Revelation 3:7, 8); and that since Jesus has opened the door into the most holy place, which contains the ark, the commandments have been shining out to God's people." — EW 42.2.

- **The vindication of the disappointed** — "Man has erred; but there has been no failure on the part of God... man erroneously believed the earth to be the sanctuary." Jesus "has risen up and shut the door of the holy place... and has opened a door into the most holy place and entered in to cleanse the sanctuary." The prophecy stood; the interpretation was mended.

  > "'Has God's word failed? Has God failed to fulfill His promises? No; He has fulfilled all that He promised. Jesus has risen up and shut the door of the holy place of the heavenly sanctuary and has opened a door into the most holy place and entered in to cleanse the sanctuary... Man has erred; but there has been no failure on the part of God... but man erroneously believed the earth to be the sanctuary to be cleansed at the end of the prophetic periods.'" — EW 250.1.

- **The center of present truth** — "The subject of the sanctuary and the investigative judgment should be clearly understood by the people of God... The sanctuary in heaven is the very center of Christ's work in behalf of men." This is the doctrine the disappointment uncovered — the key that opened the whole prophetic structure.

  > "The subject of the sanctuary and the investigative judgment should be clearly understood by the people of God. All need a knowledge for themselves of the position and work of their great High Priest." — GC 488.2.

  > "The sanctuary in heaven is the very center of Christ's work in behalf of men. It concerns every soul living upon the earth. It opens to view the plan of redemption, bringing us down to the very close of time and revealing the triumphant issue of the contest between righteousness and sin." — GC 488.3.

---

# 21. The Law and the Sabbath in Prophecy

> _The last great controversy is fought over the law of God, and over its heart, the Sabbath. When the temple in heaven is opened, the ark — the receptacle of the ten commandments — is seen (Rev. 11:19), and the law is shown unchanged. The dragon makes war on the remnant who "keep the commandments of God" (Rev. 12:17; 14:12); the little horn "thought to change times and laws" (Dan. 7:25) — and the only law it dared touch was the fourth commandment, tearing out the Sabbath and erecting Sunday in its place. The Sabbath is the seal of God and the memorial of creation, restored in the first angel's call to "worship him that made heaven, and earth" (Rev. 14:7; Isa. 58). The commandment-keepers stand at the close of probation. (DAR 140-141, 627-628; GC 433-454, 640; EW 32-33.)_

### The ark in the heavenly temple holds the law

- **Revelation 11:19** — "The temple of God was opened in heaven, and there was seen in his temple the ark of his testament." The ark, by definition, is the receptacle of the law; the heavenly sanctuary contains the great original of which Sinai's tables were a copy. This opening marks the beginning of the judgment, when attention is drawn back to God's law.

  > "Therefore the announcement that the temple of God was opened in heaven and the ark of His testament was seen points to the opening of the most holy place of the heavenly sanctuary in 1844 as Christ entered there to perform the closing work of the atonement." — GC 433.1-2.

- **Hebrews 9:4** — The earthly ark held "the tables of the covenant" — the ten commandments. The ark's value was solely as the place of the law; so the opening of the heavenly ark sets the Decalogue before the church.

  > "The ark in the tabernacle on earth contained the two tables of stone, upon which were inscribed the precepts of the law of God. The ark was merely a receptacle for the tables of the law, and the presence of these divine precepts gave to it its value and sacredness." — GC 433.2.

- **Hebrews 8:5** — The earthly priests "serve unto the example and shadow of heavenly things." The pattern was shown Moses in the mount; the law in the heavenly ark is "the great original," its earthly transcript unerring.

  > "The law of God in the sanctuary in heaven is the great original, of which the precepts inscribed upon the tables of stone and recorded by Moses in the Pentateuch were an unerring transcript… Not one command has been annulled; not a jot or tittle has been changed." — GC 434.1.

- **Revelation 15:5** — "The temple of the tabernacle of the testimony in heaven was opened." The sanctuary is called the place of "the testimony" — another name for the law of God — confirming the law stands at the center of heaven's worship.

  > "Within the holy of holies, in the sanctuary in heaven, the divine law is sacredly enshrined—the law that was spoken by God Himself amid the thunders of Sinai and written with His own finger on the tables of stone." — GC 433.2.

- **Early Writings (EW 32-33)** — In vision the ark was seen, the tables opened, "the ten commandments written... with the finger of God," and "the fourth, the Sabbath commandment, shone above them all... a halo of glory was all around it." The Sabbath was not nailed to the cross; "the pope had changed it... for he was to change times and laws."

  > "Jesus opened them, and I saw the ten commandments written on them with the finger of God… But the fourth, the Sabbath commandment, shone above them all; for the Sabbath was set apart to be kept in honor of God's holy name… I saw that the Sabbath commandment was not nailed to the cross… But the pope had changed it from the seventh to the first day of the week; for he was to change times and laws." — EW 32.3.

### The controversy is over the commandments

- **Revelation 12:17** — "The dragon was wroth with the woman, and went to make war with the remnant of her seed, which keep the commandments of God, and have the testimony of Jesus Christ." The last church is marked by commandment-keeping; this is the very point of Satan's final assault.

  > "And the dragon was wroth with the woman, and went to make war with the remnant of her seed, which keep the commandments of God, and have the testimony of Jesus Christ." — DAR 516.3.

- **Revelation 14:12** — "Here is the patience of the saints: here are they that keep the commandments of God, and the faith of Jesus." The fruit of the threefold message is a commandment-keeping company — distinguished, of necessity, by the seventh-day Sabbath.

  > "It brings out a company of whom it can be said, 'Here are they that keep the commandments of God and the faith of Jesus.'… those who receive the message are rendered peculiar by their practice in reference to the commandments of God." — DAR 627.3.

  > "These truths, as presented in Revelation 14 in connection with 'the everlasting gospel,' will distinguish the church of Christ at the time of His appearing. For as the result of the threefold message it is announced: 'Here are they that keep the commandments of God, and the faith of Jesus.'" — GC 453.3.

- **Revelation 22:14** — "Blessed are they that do his commandments, that they may have right to the tree of life, and may enter in through the gates into the city." Obedience to the commandments is the condition of entrance to the city of God — the law's authority reaches to the very end.

  > "It brings out a company of whom it can be said, 'Here are they that keep the commandments of God and the faith of Jesus.' In the very heart of Christendom this work is done; and those who receive the message are rendered peculiar by their practice in reference to the commandments of God." — DAR 627.3.

- **Ecclesiastes 12:13** — "Fear God, and keep his commandments: for this is the whole duty of man." The whole of human duty is bound up in the commandments; the controversy turns on whether men will obey or transgress them.

  > "While conflicting doctrines and theories abound, the law of God is the one unerring rule by which all opinions, doctrines, and theories are to be tested. Says the prophet: 'If they speak not according to this word, it is because there is no light in them.'" — GC 452.2.

- **John 14:15** — "If ye love me, keep my commandments." Christ makes obedience the test of love; the remnant's commandment-keeping is no legalism but loyalty to the Lawgiver.

  > "These truths, as presented in Revelation 14 in connection with 'the everlasting gospel,' will distinguish the church of Christ at the time of His appearing… 'Here are they that keep the commandments of God, and the faith of Jesus.'" — GC 453.3.

### The little horn thought to change the law — and touched the Sabbath

- **Daniel 7:25** — The little horn "shall... think to change times and laws." These are not human laws (which it could change at will) but the laws of the Most High — which it could only _think_ to change, not truly change. The papacy expunged the second commandment, divided the tenth, and tore the Sabbath from the fourth, erecting Sunday in its place.

  > "It has, in its catechisms, expunged the second commandment of the decalogue to make way for its adoration of images. It has divided the tenth commandment to make up the number ten. And, more audacious than all! it has taken hold of the fourth commandment, torn from its place the Sabbath of Jehovah, the only memorial of the great God ever given to man, and erected in its place a rival institution to serve another purpose." — DAR 140.3.

- **Daniel 7:8** — The little horn rose "among" the ten horns with "a mouth speaking great things." Identified as the papacy (538-1798), this is the power that presumed to alter the Decalogue — the "great words against the most High."

  > "Pagan Rome could slay the infants, but spare the mothers; but papal Rome slew both mothers and infants together. No age, no sex, no condition in life, was exempt from her relentless rage… And shall 'think to change times and laws.'… the times and laws in question were such as this power should only think to change, but not be able to change. They are… the laws of the Most High." — DAR 140.2-3.

- **Daniel 7:25 (with 2 Thess. 2)** — Daniel's little horn and Paul's "man of sin" are complements: "Only by changing God's law could the papacy exalt itself above God." The change of the Sabbath is precisely how that self-exaltation was accomplished.

  > "Says Daniel, of the little horn, the papacy: 'He shall think to change times and the law.' Daniel 7:25, R.V. And Paul styled the same power the 'man of sin,' who was to exalt himself above God. One prophecy is a complement of the other. Only by changing God's law could the papacy exalt itself above God." — GC 446.1.

- **Isaiah 24:5** — "They have transgressed the laws, changed the ordinance, broken the everlasting covenant." Scripture foretells a transgression that _changes_ an ordinance — fulfilled in the substitution of Sunday for the Sabbath of the everlasting covenant.

  > "The papacy has attempted to change the law of God… the fourth commandment has been so changed as to authorize the observance of the first instead of the seventh day as the Sabbath… An intentional, deliberate change is presented: 'He shall think to change the times and the law.' The change in the fourth commandment exactly fulfills the prophecy." — GC 446.2.

- **The Catholic catechism** — Compared with the Bible, the catechisms drop the second commandment, split the tenth, and rewrite the fourth to enjoin Sunday — Rome openly claiming the change was "made by the church," not by God.

  > "If now the reader will compare the ten commandments as found in Roman Catholic catechisms with those commandments as found in the Bible… the second commandment is left out, that the tenth is divided into two… and that the fourth commandment (called the third in their enumeration) is made to enjoin the observance of Sunday as the Sabbath… They expressly claim a change here, and also that the change has been made by the church." — DAR 554.1-2.

### The law of God is perpetual and unchangeable

- **Matthew 5:17-18** — "Think not that I am come to destroy the law... Till heaven and earth pass, one jot or one tittle shall in no wise pass from the law." Christ did not abolish but established the law; its smallest stroke stands while heaven and earth endure.

  > "They saw, as never before, the force of the Saviour's words: 'Till heaven and earth pass, one jot or one tittle shall in no wise pass from the law.' Matthew 5:18. The law of God, being a revelation of His will, a transcript of His character, must forever endure, 'as a faithful witness in heaven.'" — GC 434.1.

- **Psalm 111:7-8** — "All his commandments are sure. They stand fast for ever and ever." The commandments are not temporary ordinances but eternal verities — incapable of being changed by any earthly power.

  > "Not one command has been annulled; not a jot or tittle has been changed. Says the psalmist: 'Forever, O Lord, Thy word is settled in heaven.' 'All His commandments are sure. They stand fast for ever and ever.' Psalm 119:89; 111:7, 8." — GC 434.1.

- **Psalm 89:34** — "My covenant will I not break, nor alter the thing that is gone out of my lips." God Himself will not alter His word; the claim that He transferred the Sabbath's sanctity to another day contradicts His own declaration.

  > "And I saw that if God had changed the Sabbath from the seventh to the first day, He would have changed the writing of the Sabbath commandment… But I saw that it read the same as when written on the tables of stone by the finger of God, and delivered to Moses on Sinai." — EW 33.1.

- **Psalm 119:142** — "Thy law is the truth." The law is identified with truth itself; to change it is to corrupt the truth — the very work charged to the little horn.

  > "The law of God, being a revelation of His will, a transcript of His character, must forever endure, 'as a faithful witness in heaven.' Not one command has been annulled; not a jot or tittle has been changed." — GC 434.1.

- **James 2:10-12** — "Whosoever shall keep the whole law, and yet offend in one point, he is guilty of all." The Decalogue is an indivisible unity; to break the fourth commandment is to break the whole — so men "shall be judged by the law of liberty."

  > "I saw that the Sabbath commandment was not nailed to the cross. If it was, the other nine commandments were; and we are at liberty to break them all, as well as to break the fourth." — EW 32.3.

- **Romans 3:31** — "Do we then make void the law through faith? God forbid: yea, we establish the law." The gospel does not annul the law but upholds it; faith and obedience stand together. _(Waggoner / Jones, the law and the gospel.)_

- **Romans 7:7** — "I had not known sin, but by the law." The law remains the standard that defines sin; were it abolished, sin itself would be undefinable.

  > "The first step in reconciliation to God is the conviction of sin. 'Sin is the transgression of the law.' 'By the law is the knowledge of sin.' 1 John 3:4; Romans 3:20. In order to see his guilt, the sinner must test his character by God's great standard of righteousness." — GC 467-468.

### The Sabbath: memorial of creation and seal of God

- **Genesis 2:1-3** — "God blessed the seventh day, and sanctified it: because that in it he had rested from all his work." Instituted at creation, before sin and before Israel, the Sabbath is the world's first memorial and belongs to all mankind.

  > "Hallowed by the Creator's rest and blessing, the Sabbath was kept by Adam in his innocence in holy Eden; by Adam, fallen yet repentant, when he was driven from his happy estate. It was kept by all the patriarchs, from Abel to righteous Noah, to Abraham, to Jacob." — GC 453.1.

- **Exodus 20:8-11** — "Remember the sabbath day... the seventh day is the sabbath of the LORD thy God... For in six days the LORD made heaven and earth... and rested the seventh day." The fourth commandment alone names and titles the Lawgiver as Creator — making it the seal of His law.

  > "In the very bosom of the Decalogue is the fourth commandment, as it was first proclaimed: 'Remember the Sabbath day, to keep it holy… for in six days the Lord made heaven and earth, the sea, and all that in them is, and rested the seventh day: wherefore the Lord blessed the Sabbath day, and hallowed it.' Exodus 20:8-11." — GC 434.2.

  > "The seal of God's law is found in the fourth commandment. This only, of all the ten, brings to view both the name and the title of the Lawgiver. It declares Him to be the Creator of the heavens and the earth, and thus shows His claim to reverence and worship above all others." — GC 452.1.

- **Mark 2:27-28** — "The sabbath was made for man... therefore the Son of man is Lord also of the sabbath." Made for man at creation, the Sabbath is Christ's own institution; He is its Lord, not its abolisher.

  > "But the Bible points to the seventh day, and not to the first, as the Lord's day. Said Christ: 'The Son of man is Lord also of the Sabbath.' The fourth commandment declares: 'The seventh day is the Sabbath of the Lord.'" — GC 446.3.

- **Luke 23:56** — The women "rested the sabbath day according to the commandment." Even across the crucifixion the Sabbath stood unchanged — Christ resting in the tomb on the seventh day, His followers honoring it "according to the commandment."

  > "But I saw that it read the same as when written on the tables of stone by the finger of God, and delivered to Moses on Sinai. 'But the seventh day is the Sabbath of the Lord thy God.'" — EW 33.1.

- **Ezekiel 20:12** — "I gave them my sabbaths, to be a sign between me and them, that they might know that I am the LORD that sanctify them." The Sabbath is God's appointed sign of sanctification — a sign that identifies His people.

  > "'Moreover, also I gave them my Sabbaths, to be a sign between me and them, that they might know that I am the Lord that sanctify them.'" — THB 44.1.

- **Ezekiel 20:20** — "Hallow my sabbaths; and they shall be a sign between me and you, that ye may know that I am the LORD your God." Repeated for emphasis: the Sabbath is the token by which God's worshipers know Him from all others.

  > "'I am the Lord your God: walk in my statutes, and keep my judgments, and do them; and hollow my Sabbaths, and they shall be a sign between me and you, that ye may know that I am the Lord your God.'… The Sabbath is a sign between God and his people; hence it seals his law as genuine." — THB 44.1-2.

- **Isaiah 8:16** — "Bind up the testimony, seal the law among my disciples." The seal of God's law is found in the fourth commandment — the only precept bearing the name, title, and dominion of the Lawgiver, the Creator.

  > "'Bind up the testimony, seal the law among My disciples.' Isaiah 8:16. The seal of God's law is found in the fourth commandment. This only, of all the ten, brings to view both the name and the title of the Lawgiver… Aside from this precept, there is nothing in the Decalogue to show by whose authority the law is given." — GC 452.1.

- **Sign and seal are one** — "Sign and seal are synonymous terms" (Rom. 4:11). The Sabbath being the sign between God and His people, it seals His law as genuine; "take this fourth commandment from the ten, and the seal of the living God is gone."

  > "Sign and seal are synonymous terms. See Romans 4:11… The Sabbath is a sign between God and his people; hence it seals his law as genuine… Take this fourth commandment from the ten, and the seal of the living God is gone, and the knowledge of their author is taken from us." — THB 44.2.

### The Sabbath restored in the last message — the final test

- **Revelation 14:7** — "Worship him that made heaven, and earth, and the sea, and the fountains of waters." The first angel's call to worship the Creator is in the very language of the fourth commandment — a summons back to the Sabbath as the judgment hour strikes.

  > "In Revelation 14, men are called upon to worship the Creator; and the prophecy brings to view a class that, as the result of the threefold message, are keeping the commandments of God. One of these commandments points directly to God as the Creator… It follows that the message which commands men to worship God and keep His commandments will especially call upon them to keep the fourth commandment." — GC 437-438.

  > "And worship him that made heaven, and earth, and the sea, and the fountains of waters… Here is the patience of the saints: here are they that keep the commandments of God, and the faith of Jesus." — DAR 585.3.

- **Isaiah 58:12-14** — "Thou shalt be called, The repairer of the breach... if thou turn away thy foot from the sabbath... and call the sabbath a delight." A last-day people repair the breach made in God's law by restoring the trodden-down Sabbath — the very work of the third angel's message.

  > "The breach was made in the law of God when the Sabbath was changed by the Roman power. But the time has come for that divine institution to be restored. The breach is to be repaired and the foundation of many generations to be raised up." — GC 452.4-453.

- **Ezekiel 22:26** — "They have... hid their eyes from my sabbaths, and I am profaned among them." The priests' sin was profaning the holy and hiding their eyes from the Sabbath; the last message exposes and reverses that apostasy. _(Cf. Isa. 58:13.)_

- **Isaiah 66:22-23** — "As the new heavens and the new earth... so shall your seed and your name remain. And it shall come to pass... from one sabbath to another, shall all flesh come to worship before me." The Sabbath outlasts this world and is kept in the earth made new — the final proof of its perpetuity.

  > "Hallowed by the Creator's rest and blessing, the Sabbath was kept by Adam in his innocence in holy Eden… It was kept by all the patriarchs, from Abel to righteous Noah, to Abraham, to Jacob." — GC 453.1.

- **The line of demarcation** — The third angel's message brings its adherents to the seventh day, the only mark that distinguishes them; "no plainer line of demarcation could be drawn between two classes" — Sabbath-keepers and Sunday-keepers.

  > "No plainer line of demarcation could be drawn between two classes. The time which one class regard as sacred, and devote to religious uses, the other look upon as only secular, and devote to ordinary labor… The message brings its adherents to the seventh day; for in this way only are they made peculiar." — DAR 627.3-628.1.

- **Two seals, two marks** — God's seal is His Sabbath; the beast's mark is its rival sabbath, Sunday. "Christendom will at last be divided into just two classes" — those sealed with the seal of the living God and those who receive the mark.

  > "Thus God has a seal, or mark, which is his Sabbath. The beast also has a seal, or mark, which is his Sabbath… Christendom will at last be divided into just two classes; to wit, those who are sealed with the seal of the living God, that is, have his mark, or keep his Sabbath; and those who are sealed with the seal of the beast." — DAR 628.2.

- **The seal of the living God** — "Too late they see that the Sabbath of the fourth commandment is the seal of the living God." In the final crisis the Sabbath becomes the great test of loyalty, distinguishing the worshipers of God from the worshipers of the beast.

  > "Too late they see that the Sabbath of the fourth commandment is the seal of the living God. Too late they see the true nature of their spurious sabbath and the sandy foundation upon which they have been building. They find that they have been fighting against God." — GC 640.1.

  > "Christendom will at last be divided into just two classes; to wit, those who are sealed with the seal of the living God, that is, have his mark, or keep his Sabbath; and those who are sealed with the seal of the beast, that is, have his mark, or keep his Sabbath." — DAR 627-628.

- **Worship Christ, keep His commandments** — "If thou wilt enter into life, keep the commandments" (Matt. 19:17). The Lord who is worshiped as Creator in the first message is the Lord who points men to obedience as the way of life — the law and the gospel meeting in the Sabbath.

  > "These truths, as presented in Revelation 14 in connection with 'the everlasting gospel,' will distinguish the church of Christ at the time of His appearing… 'Here are they that keep the commandments of God, and the faith of Jesus.' And this message is the last to be given before the coming of the Lord." — GC 453.3.

---

# 22. The Two Covenants — the Everlasting Gospel in Prophecy

> _The two covenants are not two periods of time but two conditions of the heart. The "old" is man's own promise — "all this we will do" — self-confidence that ends in bondage (Hagar, Sinai). The "new" is God's promise, His law written on the heart by faith (Sarah, the Jerusalem above, liberty). One everlasting covenant runs from Eden through Abraham, sealed in the blood of Christ, and this righteousness by faith is the very "everlasting gospel" the first angel proclaims (Rev. 14:6) — the faith by which the 144,000 and the remnant live. (EVCO 6, 83-84; GTI 184; GC 467-478.)_

### One Everlasting Covenant — from Eden, confirmed in Christ

- **Genesis 3:15** — "I will put enmity between thee and the woman, and between thy seed and her seed." The first preaching of the gospel: the moment man sinned and lost his inheritance, restoration was promised through the woman's Seed. The everlasting covenant begins here, not at Sinai.

  > "But God works all things after the counsel of His own will; not one of His purposes will fail; and so as soon as man had sinned and lost his inheritance, a restoration was promised through Christ, in these words: 'And I will put enmity between thee and the woman, and between thy seed and her seed; it shall bruise thy head, and thou shalt bruise his heel.' … In these words the destruction of Satan and all his work was foretold." — EVCO 38.

- **Genesis 17:7** — "I will establish my covenant... for an everlasting covenant, to be a God unto thee, and to thy seed after thee." One covenant, called everlasting, sealed with Abraham — the same one "through the blood of the everlasting covenant" by which men are perfected.

  > "It is the one everlasting covenant, which is so often spoken of in the Bible. It is 'through the blood of the everlasting covenant' that men are made perfect in every good work to do the will of God." — EVCO 83.

- **Hebrews 13:20** — "the blood of the everlasting covenant." There is but one everlasting covenant, and it stands in Christ's blood — the unbroken line that runs through the whole Bible.

  > "One line runs through the entire Bible, God's everlasting covenant. Standing at the Cross, one can see the working of God's eternal purpose, which He purposed 'in Christ before the world began.' The history from Paradise lost till Paradise restored, is set forth as in a panorama." — EVCO 6.

  > "It is the one everlasting covenant, which is so often spoken of in the Bible. It is 'through the blood of the everlasting covenant' that men are made perfect in every good work to do the will of God." — EVCO 83-84.

- **Galatians 3:8** — "the scripture... preached before the gospel unto Abraham, saying, In thee shall all nations be blessed." The covenant to Abraham was the everlasting gospel itself — gospel and covenant are one.

  > "So we see that the making of this everlasting covenant with Abraham was simply the preaching of the everlasting Gospel of the kingdom… The promise to Abraham was a Gospel promise, and nothing else, and the covenant was the everlasting covenant, of which Christ is Mediator." — EVCO 84.

  > "That the covenant and promise of God are one and the same thing, is clearly seen from Galatians 3:17, where it appears that to disannul the covenant would be to make void the promise." — GTI 132.

### The Old Covenant — the people's own promise (self-righteousness, bondage)

- **Galatians 4:22-25** — "These are the two covenants; the one from the mount Sinai, which gendereth to bondage, which is Agar." Hagar, the bondmaid, answers to "Jerusalem which now is, and is in bondage with her children." The old covenant is symbolized by the slave-woman: she can bring forth none but slaves.

  > "What are the two covenants?—The two women, Hagar and Sarah; for we read that Hagar is Mount Sinai, 'which gendereth to bondage.' That is, just as Hagar could not bring forth any other kind of children than slaves, so the law, even the law that God spoke from Sinai, can not beget freemen." — GTI 184.

  > "The difference is just the difference between a freewoman and a slave. Hagar's children, no matter how many she might have had, would have been slaves, while those of Sarah would necessarily be free." — GTI 187.

- **Exodus 19:8** — "All that the LORD hath spoken we will do." The fatal error: the people made themselves the surety. Instead of "let it be done unto us according to Thy will," they assumed the responsibility of working the works of God in their own strength.

  > "The proper response of the people therefore would have been, 'Amen, even so, O Lord, let it be done unto us according to Thy will.' On the contrary they said, 'All that the Lord hath spoken we will do;' … It was the same self-confidence that led their descendants to say to Christ, 'What shall we do, that we might work the works of God?'" — EVCO 332.

- **Romans 10:3** — they "going about to establish their own righteousness, have not submitted themselves unto the righteousness of God." Their promises "were good for nothing" — this self-confidence is the essence of the old covenant.

  > "It is only when men are ignorant of God's righteousness, that they go about to establish their own righteousness, and refuse to submit themselves to the righteousness of God. … Their promises were good for nothing, because they had not the power to fulfill them." — EVCO 332.

- **Hebrews 8:7** — "For if that first covenant had been faultless, then should no place have been sought for the second." The fault was not in God's holy law but in the people who promised in their own strength (v. 8, "finding fault with them").

  > "But it was not by virtue of the covenant that was made at Sinai, that mercy and peace were there. That covenant brought the people nothing, although everything was there for them to enjoy." — EVCO 323.

  > "Both covenants contain promises, and are founded upon them. … The 'new covenant' is better than the one made at Sinai. … It is better, because the promises upon which it is founded are better." — EVCO 326.

- **Galatians 4:24** — "which gendereth to bondage." The law spoken from Sinai, however holy, "can not beget freemen" any more than Hagar could bear a free child — the moment it is leaned on for righteousness, it brings bondage.

  > "Just as Hagar could not bring forth any other kind of children than slaves, so the law, even the law that God spoke from Sinai, can not beget freemen. It can do nothing but hold them in bondage." — GTI 184.

  > "One is the shadow of the other, the result of placing the veil of unbelief before the light of life." — EVCO 344.

### The New Covenant — God's promise, the law written on the heart (liberty)

- **Jeremiah 31:31-33** — "I will put my law in their inward parts, and write it in their hearts; and will be their God, and they shall be my people." The new covenant is not a new law but the old law in a new place — God Himself does the writing.

  > "Its scope is identical with that of the new covenant, in which God says, 'I will put my law in their inward parts, and write it in their hearts; and will be their God, and they shall be my people.'" — EVCO 84.

  > "In the beginning, man was created in the image of God. He was in perfect harmony with the nature and the law of God; the principles of righteousness were written upon his heart. But sin alienated him from his Maker. … His heart must be renewed by divine grace; he must have a new life from above." — GC 467-468.

- **Hebrews 8:8-10** — "I will make a new covenant... I will put my laws into their mind, and write them in their hearts." Both covenants are made "with the house of Israel" and founded upon promises; the new is better because its promises are God's, not man's.

  > "Both covenants are made with 'the house of Israel'… Both covenants contain promises, and are founded upon them. … The 'new covenant' is better than the one made at Sinai. … It is better, because the promises upon which it is founded are better." — EVCO 326.

- **Hebrews 8:6** — Christ is "the mediator of a better covenant, which was established upon better promises." The superiority lies wholly in the Promiser: God cannot fail.

  > "That covenant brought the people nothing, although everything was there for them to enjoy." — EVCO 323.

  > "The 'new covenant' is better than the one made at Sinai. … It is better, because the promises upon which it is founded are better." — EVCO 326.

- **Hebrews 8:12-13** — "I will be merciful to their unrighteousness... their sins... will I remember no more." Forgiveness, not human resolve, is the foundation of the new covenant.

  > "Both covenants contain promises, and are founded upon them. … It is better, because the promises upon which it is founded are better." — EVCO 326.

  > "We may go to Jesus and be cleansed, and stand before the law without shame and remorse. 'There is therefore now no condemnation to them which are in Christ Jesus, who walk not after the flesh, but after the Spirit.'" — GC 477.

- **Hebrews 10:16-17** — "I will put my laws into their hearts... And their sins and iniquities will I remember no more." The same promise restated: heart-obedience plus full pardon.

  > "In the new birth the heart is brought into harmony with God, as it is brought into accord with His law. … The old life of alienation from God has ended; the new life of reconciliation, of faith and love, has begun." — GC 467-468.

- **Ezekiel 36:26-27** — "A new heart also will I give you... And I will put my spirit within you, and cause you to walk in my statutes." The mechanism of the new covenant: God supplies the heart that keeps His law.

  > "But Christ is the only Light of the world, the Light of life; so that the true and living law is found only in Him. It is His life, because it is in His heart, and out of the heart are the issues of life." — EVCO 344.

  > "It is the work of conversion and sanctification to reconcile men to God by bringing them into accord with the principles of His law. … His heart must be renewed by divine grace; he must have a new life from above. This change is the new birth." — GC 467.

- **Galatians 4:26-28** — "Jerusalem which is above is free, which is the mother of us all... we, brethren, as Isaac was, are the children of promise." Sarah, the freewoman, is the new covenant; her children are free, born "by promise."

  > "What are the two covenants?—The two women, Hagar and Sarah… just as Hagar could not bring forth any other kind of children than slaves, so the law, even the law that God spoke from Sinai, can not beget freemen." — GTI 184.

  > "The difference is just the difference between a freewoman and a slave. Hagar's children, no matter how many she might have had, would have been slaves, while those of Sarah would necessarily be free." — GTI 187.

- **2 Corinthians 3:6** — "the letter killeth, but the spirit giveth life." Ministers of "the new testament" deal not in the letter (the old covenant's dead works) but in the life-giving Spirit.

  > "His heart must be renewed by divine grace; he must have a new life from above. … In the new birth the heart is brought into harmony with God, as it is brought into accord with His law." — GC 467-468.

### The Two Covenants Contrasted

|              | Old Covenant (Hagar / Sinai)               | New Covenant (Sarah / Jerusalem above)  |
| ------------ | ------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------- |
| **Promiser** | the people — "all this we will do"         | God — "I will write it in their hearts" |
| **Result**   | bondage, self-righteousness, death         | liberty, righteousness by faith, life   |
| **Child**    | born "after the flesh" — a slave           | born "by promise" — free                |
| **Law**      | leaned on for righteousness → "the letter" | written within by the Spirit → life     |

_(Gal. 4:22-31; EVCO 323-326, 332, 344; GTI 184-187.)_

### Justified by Faith, Not by the Law — Abraham the Pattern

- **Genesis 15:6** — "And he believed in the LORD; and he counted it to him for righteousness." Abraham was justified by faith before circumcision, before Sinai — the pattern for every believer.

  > "The promise to Abraham was a Gospel promise, and nothing else, and the covenant was the everlasting covenant, of which Christ is Mediator." — EVCO 84.

  > "Faith means the reception of the life of Christ into the heart, together with God's righteousness… God has promised to every man who believes him nothing less than the freedom of the world." — WOR 77.

- **Romans 4:3-5** — "to him that worketh not, but believeth on him that justifieth the ungodly, his faith is counted for righteousness." Righteousness is reckoned, not earned — the heart of the new covenant.
